This is the reproducibility guarantee that separates a real MLOps workflow from ad-hoc notebooks. **The split-call shape is not unique to the mint step** — Phase 10's `training_cycle.py` (`PROJECT_PLAN.md`, Phase 10) already wraps this same DVC chain in exactly this way at its other registry boundary, calling the DVC-tracked portion through `evaluate`/`error_analysis` as one Prefect task, then pausing for human review before a separate task runs `register.py`'s promote/reject call — an orchestrator task sequencing around a DAG tool's `repro` calls, not a single monolithic process, is the standard shape for this, not a special case this project invented for the mint step.

**Deliverables:**
- `dvc init` — **the raw CSV stays git-committed as a stage `dep`, not `dvc add`-ed.** `configs/config.yaml` reads `datasets/raw/WA_Fn-UseC_-Telco-Customer-Churn.csv` (the authentic Kaggle/IBM filename); it is 977 KB, static, and read-only, so git owns it and DVC only watches it — see the raw-data note below. (`dvc add` would make DVC *own* the file, contradicting that note.)
- `dvc.yaml` — nine stages with explicit `deps` (code + data files), `params` (named config keys — **never whole config directories**, see the params note below), and `outs` (data artifacts, metrics):

  | Stage | Deps (files) | Params (config keys) | Outs |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `ingest` | `data/ingest.py`, `datasets/raw/*.csv` (git-tracked), `sql/schema/` | `configs/config.yaml`: `validation.min_rows`, `validation.max_null_rate` (**no `paths.*` — superseded by the resolved design; see the params note**) | `reports/ingest_receipt.json` (row count, per-column null counts, frame checksum) |
  | `validate` | `data/validate.py`, `data/schema.py`, `reports/ingest_receipt.json` (**invalidation edge — not read by this stage**; the raw CSV is not a direct dep here, so this receipt is the entire path by which a CSV change reaches `validate`) | `configs/config.yaml`: `validation`, `paths.raw_data` | `reports/validation_receipt.json` (always written, pass or fail, no timestamp — **not** `reports/validation/`; see the receipt note below) |
  | `split` | `data/split.py`, `reports/validation_receipt.json` (**invalidation edge — not read by this stage**; split blocks on a passing validation run) | `configs/config.yaml`: `random_seed`, `training_setup.test_size`, `paths.processed_data` (added — `_default_manifest_path()` reads it, and it is trackable only once the prerequisites make it a plain literal) | `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet` |
  | `features` | `features/build.py`, `features/sql_features.py`, `features/schema.py`, `sql/features/`, `reports/validation_receipt.json` (**invalidation edge — not read by this stage**; the stage reads `customer_features` from Postgres, so this receipt is the entire path by which a CSV change reaches it — see the stage 4 entry) | *(none — both `paths.*` keys are superseded, leaving this stage with no `params:` block at all; see its resolved design)* | `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet` (Parquet — static snapshot for downstream stages) |
  | `train` | `models/train/`, `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet`, `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet` | `configs/config.yaml`: `random_seed`, `training_setup` · `configs/training/lightgbm.yaml`, `configs/training/logreg.yaml`, `configs/training/feature_selection.yaml`, `configs/tuning/optuna.yaml` (all four composed **and** read — `logreg` is the comparison family, `selection` the selector hyperparameters) | `reports/train_receipt.json` — **only** (the MLflow identity receipt: `{run_id, logged_model_id, model_uri}`; a file, since outs must be files). `feature_space.txt` / `feature_columns.txt` / `preprocessing.pkl` are **not** outs: they never touch the filesystem, see the out-path note below |
  | `calibrate` | `models/calibrate.py`, `models/shap_values.py` + `models/explain.py` (**added — this stage runs the dev SHAP pass from the in-memory fitted pipeline and emits the per-feature `(mean_abs_shap, direction)` summary `threshold` judges; see the V3 prerequisite for why the computation lives here and not there**), `data/split.py`, `features/accessor.py`, `utils/stats.py` (`paired_bootstrap_ci` — the ΔBrier CI behind the sigmoid/isotonic switch; per-file per the deps convention in this stage's resolved design, not a directory or an import closure), `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet`, `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet`, `reports/train_receipt.json` (**also the run-id source**, not merely a sequencing edge — `dvc repro` passes no `calibration.run_id`, so the module resolves it from this receipt; see the three-module note in the DAG-wide corrections) | `configs/config.yaml`: **`training_setup.delta_threshold` only** — not the whole block (the other eight keys are unread here; calibration runs its own folds off `cfg.calibration.outer_cv_folds`/`inner_cv_folds`) and **not `random_seed`** (`calibrate.py` reads `cfg.calibration.random_state`; see the phantom-param item) · `configs/calibration/default.yaml` (whole file — 11 keys read). **Deliberately absent, both of them:** `mlflow.tracking_uri`, an `${oc.env:…}` placeholder DVC would hash unresolved and therefore never invalidate; and `mlflow.registered_model_name`, read only by the registration block being extracted into `register.py` — correct to omit, but *only once that extraction lands*, so the code split must precede or accompany this stage definition | `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` — **only** (**`cache: false`**, same rationale as `train`'s; `{run_id, logged_model_id, model_uri}`; the `logged_model_id` is load-bearing, not decorative — a run-id-only receipt is a dead edge here, see stage 6; **fit-and-log only, no registry write** — see below). `calibration_summary.json`, `dev_oof_predictions.parquet` and **`dev_shap_values.parquet`** (~2 MB, the full matrix required by the persist-the-evidence rule, alongside the small per-feature summary) are MLflow-only here — none is a DVC `out` or `metric`, same disposition throughout |
  | `threshold` | `models/threshold.py`, `models/diagnostics.py` (V1/V2/V2b), `models/gate.py` (`slope_passes`, the band check), `models/explain.py` (**added — V3 moves into this stage's screen; `models/shap_values.py` does *not* appear here, because this stage judges a per-feature summary rather than computing SHAP — see the V3 prerequisite**), `features/preprocessing.py` (the tenure-cohort edges every slice is computed over), `data/split.py`, `features/accessor.py` — same per-file convention; **`models/calibrate.py` is excluded despite being imported**, its edge arriving through the receipt, `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet`, `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet` (**added — `threshold.py:465` calls `partition(df)`, which defaults to `load_split()`; a re-split changes which rows `t*` is derived on and the original row tracked none of it**), `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` (the dev-OOF vector, the calibration slope **and now the per-feature SHAP summary** are fetched from *that run*, `threshold.py:408`/`:807` — not from a local file; **also the run-id source**, since `dvc repro` passes no `threshold.model_version`) | `configs/threshold/default.yaml` (`random_state`, `n_bootstrap`, **`v3_top_k_features`**, **`v3_min_direction_magnitude`** — the latter two added with the V3 screen; both flip the verdict on identical data and an identical model, which is the operative test for a params entry) · `configs/costs.yaml` (whole file — `load_costs_config` consumes essentially all of it) · `configs/model_promotion.yaml`: **`calibration_slope_band` only** — not the whole file, since `threshold.py:809` reads exactly that one field and the gate bars beside it would rerun the screen spuriously. **Not `random_seed`** (`threshold.py` reads `cfg.threshold.random_state`; see the phantom-param item) and **not `configs/calibration/`** — the calibration knobs reach this stage only through the receipt's `logged_model_id`, and declaring them here would fingerprint one edge twice while implying the receipt edge is decorative | **`metrics:`** `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (**`cache: false`** — git-tracked; the model-independent policy file, and the real edge into `evaluate`. **Under `metrics:`, not `outs:`** — `t*` moving is exactly what a PR reviewer needs to see, DVC's `metrics:` accepts YAML, and since `metrics:` is a kind of out this *replaces* the `outs:` entry rather than duplicating it. Load-bearing beyond preference: `metrics_summary.json` deliberately omits `t*` on the grounds that it is already a metric here, so leaving it under `outs:` puts the shipped threshold in no metrics surface at all) · **`metrics:`** `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json` (`cache: false` — the V1/V2/V2b fairness verdict **plus `v3_direction_sanity`** — including its `checked_count`/`weak_count` coverage counters and the elbow-drift flag, so a collapsing screen is visible rather than presenting as a clean pass — worth `dvc metrics diff` across cycles; **it is also the channel `error_analysis` inherits the V3 audit set through**, via `runs:/<run_id>/threshold/`, never a local `reports/` read). **This stage now halts the DAG on a bad model** — it already raised on a failed calibration-slope screen (`threshold.py:1083`), and V3 joins that same raise, so `evaluate` never opens the seal for a sign-flipped candidate. **`reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` is deliberately *not* an out** — nothing reads it (`error_analysis.py:1188` and `register.py:501` both resolve `runs:/<id>/calibration/…` via `load_dev_oof_predictions`), and it is a re-aligned derivative of an MLflow artifact rather than the canonical copy; it fails both clauses of the out-earns-its-place test, same as the figures. Keep the write as an untracked human mirror |
  | `evaluate` | `models/evaluate.py`, `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet`, `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet`, `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`, `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (**not** `reports/threshold.json` — `evaluate.py:1229` reads the policy file via `load_policy_thresholds`; a plain dep, not a param, since it is `threshold`'s generated out) | `configs/config.yaml`: `random_seed`, `training_setup` · `configs/evaluate/default.yaml`, `configs/costs.yaml` (read directly at `evaluate.py:1305`), `configs/model_promotion.yaml` (the gate bars, `evaluate.py:1397`) | `reports/metrics.json`, `reports/economics.json`, `reports/promotion_decision.json` (the gate's verdict — *computing* it is a pure function of the metrics just produced; only *acting* on it is excluded from the DAG, see below. **Valid as an out only once the review split lands** — until then the notebook writes the human verdict back to this same path, and DVC would revert it; see that prerequisite), `reports/test_predictions.parquet`, plus two declared under their own DVC fields: **`metrics:`** `reports/metrics_summary.json` (new — flat, `cache: false`) and **`plots:`** `reports/plots/decile_lift.csv` (new — the one series no scalar summarises; see the metrics/plots note below) |
  | `error_analysis` | `models/error_analysis.py`, `models/shap_values.py` + `models/explain.py` (**added — the extraction and the pure V3 pieces move out of this module; it calls them rather than owning them**), `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`, `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (`error_analysis.py:1193`), `reports/test_predictions.parquet` (**not** `reports/metrics.json` — B0 repoints the dev-OOF carry-through at `evaluate.py::load_dev_oof_diagnostics`, i.e. `runs:/<run_id>/threshold/dev_oof_diagnostics.json`, so the single read that ever justified this dep is gone. It was over-broad besides: 42 KB of every metric `evaluate` computed, declared to obtain four flag lists, so a change to `test_ece` would have rerun the SHAP pass) | `configs/config.yaml`: `random_seed` · `configs/error_analysis/default.yaml` (**`top_k_shap_features` renamed `top_k_dependence_features`** — it now drives only the dependence grid and the `shap_importance_*` metrics, the binding cutoff having moved to `configs/threshold/default.yaml`) | `reports/error_analysis.json` — **only** (no `figures/`, no `shap_values.parquet`; see the figures and out-path notes below). **Its V3 becomes reported-only** (`direction_sanity_check_test`) once the binding copy moves pre-seal into `threshold`, and it audits the **dev-fixed feature set inherited via `runs:/<run_id>/threshold/dev_oof_diagnostics.json`** rather than re-ranking its own top-k — the two results are only comparable if both ask about the same features. This stage keeps its own test-side SHAP pass for the cohort work, so the second V3 costs nothing extra; the dependence grid renders the union of its own top-k and the inherited set, which changes the panel count inside one figure and not the figure inventory — no registry write, no human input; a pure file transform over `evaluate`'s output, same shape as every other stage |

- **Extract `calibrate.py`'s registration call — a Phase 6 boundary revisit, not new Phase 8 logic.**
  **✅ Done (2026-08-10, `chore/phase-8-prereqs`).** The registry-mutation code moved — `register.py::register_challenger` performs the `mlflow.register_model` mint, tag, and alias-flip; `calibrate.py`'s own `log_model` call no longer passes `registered_model_name=` — **and the call-site decoupling landed too**: `calibrate.py::run_calibration_step` no longer calls `register_challenger` at all, so running `calibrate.py` never mutates the live registry. `register_challenger`'s own signature changed to match — `(cfg, run_id, model_uri, logged_model_id)`, all caller-resolved identifiers, never an in-memory `log_model` `ModelInfo` a second process could not receive — and its reload-parity check now loads `golden_predictions.json` off the run (`_check_golden_parity`, the same helper `run_registration_step`'s own pre-/post-flip checks already used) instead of comparing against an in-memory array. `register.py`'s `__main__` gained a second, mutually-exclusive dispatch mode: `register.model_version` (existing) selects the promote/reject path; the new `register.run_id` selects the mint path, defaulting to `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` when neither is given — one CLI, still the single entry point for every registry write, per its own module docstring. `calibrate.py` tags `calibrated_model_id`/`calibrated_model_uri` directly onto the training-cycle run (so an explicit `register.run_id` override never needs the receipt), and `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` dropped `model_version` for good — it never carried registry state to begin with, since nothing is registered at the point `calibrate.py` writes it. **This is also where `threshold.py`/`evaluate.py`'s default resolution switched from `model_version` to `run_id`, concretely:** `utils/mlflow.py::resolve_model_identifier`'s "neither given" branch used to return the receipt's own stored `model_version`/`model_uri` fields directly; it now reads only the receipt's `run_id` and falls through into the same registry lookup (`resolve_model_version_from_run_id`) the explicit-`run_id` path already used — so the default path now requires a version already minted for that `run_id` (by `register.py`'s mint step, run separately), and raises a clear error naming that CLI if none exists yet, rather than trusting a value baked into the receipt at calibrate-time.

  As shipped, `calibrate.py` does two things in one script: (1) fits the calibrated pipeline and logs it, together with `calibration_summary.json`/`dev_oof_predictions.parquet`, as run artifacts — a pure, deterministic transform whose DVC-visible output is the run-id receipt; and (2) mints an MLflow registry version and points the `challenger` alias at it — a side effect on shared, mutable state. Only (1) belongs in the DVC `calibrate` stage. (2) moves into `register.py`, which becomes the single entry point for minting, **tagging the new version**, pointing `challenger`, reading the promotion verdict, and flipping/rejecting — invoked via a dedicated CLI (resolved below). **Five jobs, not four:** after the extraction every registry write in the cycle lives here, including the six model-version tags `evaluate.py` and `error_analysis.py` write today — see the tag-migration bullet below.
  - **`register.py` must write `promotion_status: pending` at mint time — this is *new* code, not moved code, and it is one of **two** tag-writing items the extraction creates that nothing else in this phase recorded (8b) — the other is the six moved tags in the bullet below.** As shipped, `register.py` only ever *transitions* the tag: `rejected` (`:1186`) and `promoted` (`:1417`), both assuming some other module created the version. There is no mint-time write anywhere in the file, because it has never minted. The extraction must add one, immediately after the version is created and **before** the serving smoke check or any verdict is read.
    - **Why this needs stating rather than leaving to implementation time: the invariant is currently held by *adjacency*, and the extraction destroys the adjacency.** In `calibrate.py`, `_tag_new_version_pending` sits between `log_model` and `_verify_reload_parity`, and its docstring carries the reasoning. Once mint and verdict become neighbours inside one `register.py` function, the tempting simplification is immediate and wrong — *"we mint and decide in one place now, so skip `pending` and write `promoted`/`rejected` directly."* That collapses the fail-safe `CLAUDE.md` is explicit about: `pending` is the mint-time default **because the safe state must be the one you get for free**, and a tag written only on the failure path is the one the next abort path forgets to write.
    - **The failure it prevents is silent, which is why the redundancy is only apparent.** A crash between mint and verdict — the smoke check raising, an OOM, `_reverify_incumbent` refusing the flip against a moved champion — leaves an **untagged** version under the collapsed design. That is invisible to *both* consumers of the tag: `rollback_champion`'s query filters on `promotion_status == "promoted"` (`:344`), and the Phase 14 pending-orphan reaper filters on `pending`. Nothing errors; the version simply sits uncategorised, in neither the rollback surface nor the audit trail.
    - **Move the docstring rationale with the code**, and add the test the current arrangement never needed: a register run that mints and then fails its smoke check must leave the version tagged `pending`, not untagged.
  - **🔴 The six model-version tags move with the mint — found in the stage 8–9 walk, and B1 is where they belong (8c).** `evaluate.py:1777-1808` writes five (`test_pr_auc`, `test_recall`, `test_brier`, `test_calibration_slope`, `eval_run_id`) and `error_analysis.py:1809` writes one (`error_analysis_run_id`), all onto a version `calibrate.py` minted earlier in the same cycle. After the extraction that version does not exist when either module runs: under `dvc repro` the calls fail outright, and on a hand-run carrying an explicit `model_version=N` they succeed against the **previous** cycle's version, silently. All six become `register.py`'s, written at mint beside `promotion_status: pending` — the module that creates the slot is the module that describes it.
    - **This is the extraction's own boundary, not a new rule.** The dividing line below puts a module outside the DAG the moment it *"starts mutating shared, external, pointer-based state"*; both modules have been on the wrong side of it since Phase 7, tagging a registry version from inside what is otherwise a pure file transform. The reordering does not create the violation, it makes it fatal instead of invisible — which is why the fix is a relocation rather than a repair.
    - **`register.py` then needs a channel to those two runs, and the tags it reads today are exactly the channel B1 removes.** It reads `reports/eval_receipt.json` and `reports/error_analysis_receipt.json` — the `{run_id, logged_model_id}` shape `train` and `calibrate` already use — cross-checks each receipt's `logged_model_id` against the model it is minting, and aborts on mismatch. `CLAUDE.md`'s resolve-by-tag rule keeps full force where it does its work: every reader *after* the tags exist still goes through them, and the single bootstrap read is guarded by an equality check rather than trusted. **These two receipts are B1's to write, not Phase 8's** — without them `register.py` cannot complete a manual cycle the moment B1 lands; Phase 8 only *declares* them as stage outs. **Considered and rejected: search MLflow for runs carrying the model's `logged_model_id`.** No new files and no local read, but a search over shared mutable state returns 0, 1 or N, and tie-breaking on recency is precisely the moving pointer the resolve-by-`run_id` invariant forbids.
    - **⚠ A `CLAUDE.md` divergence, flagged rather than made silently.** That document tags the four gate criteria *"on the promoted model version."* Written at mint they land on every version, rejected ones included. That is the better behaviour on `CLAUDE.md`'s own stated rationale — the tags exist for *"someone scanning a version list under pressure"*, and a rejected candidate's numbers are exactly what that person needs to see — but it changes a written rule, so the wording is updated in this same PR.
    - **Test fallout this bullet's parent does not otherwise reach.** `tests/integration/test_error_analysis_subprocess.py:419` asserts the tag write from the module that loses it. `tests/unit/test_register.py:208-216` hand-builds those same tags in its fixture *because* nothing in `register.py` ever wrote them — that fixture collapses into real behaviour, which is a fair signal the move runs in the right direction.

- **`error_analysis` closes a real input gap `register.py` already has, not a stylistic addition.** `CLAUDE.md` states `register.py` "aborts if the model version carries no `error_analysis_run_id` tag, or if that run has no `error_analysis.json`" — `model_card.json`'s fairness/limitations section is assembled from it. Before this stage existed, nothing in the DVC-visible chain produced that artifact, so a full `dvc repro` still could not hand `register.py` everything it structurally requires — only `promotion_decision.json` was covered. `error_analysis.py` computes SHAP values and slice/fairness metrics from `evaluate`'s output files; it makes no registry write and needs no human input to run (`CLAUDE.md` notes it is written by the script, not the notebook, "because Phase 10's automated retrain has no human to run Jupyter"), so it is a pure file transform of the same shape as every other stage in the DAG — it belongs inside it, unlike registration.

> **⚠ Blocking consequence of the extraction, not yet in the deliverables above: `threshold.py` and `evaluate.py` both *require* a registry version that will no longer exist when they run.** `configs/threshold/default.yaml:3-4` and `configs/evaluate/default.yaml:3-4` each declare `model_version: null` with the comment *"required, e.g. `threshold.model_version=1`"* — both resolve their model by **registry version number**, and today that works only because `calibrate.py` mints version 1 before either runs. Extract that mint into `register.py` — which runs *last*, after `evaluate` — and the ordering inverts: `threshold` and `evaluate` would need a version that nothing has created yet. The pipeline does not merely go stale, it cannot execute.
>
> **✅ RESOLVED — this paragraph's own proposed fix (resolve by `run_id` alone, no registry lookup) was not the one implemented, and that is the right call, not a gap.** The "✅ Done" bullet above (`resolve_model_identifier`'s new default path) still ends in `resolve_model_version_from_run_id` — a registry lookup requiring `register.py`'s mint step to have already run for that `run_id`, just with a clearer error message when it hasn't. That does mean a full cycle needs `register.py --run-id` (mint, non-DVC) spliced in between the `calibrate` and `threshold` stages — the opening paragraph above has been rewritten to say so directly (`dvc repro` runs twice, not once, with the mint step between the two calls) rather than describing a single uninterrupted run. **Loosening `threshold`/`evaluate` to skip the registry lookup, this paragraph's original proposal, is the worse fix**, not merely an alternative one: it would let those stages resolve a model version that `register.py` has never tagged `pending`, `promoted`, or `rejected` — exactly the untagged/uncategorised state `CLAUDE.md`'s `promotion_status` design exists to prevent (a version indistinguishable from a crash artifact). Requiring the mint step first is what guarantees every version `threshold.py`/`evaluate.py`/`error_analysis.py` ever tag against is one `register.py` already knows about. The split-call shape this requires is not a special case either — Phase 10's `training_cycle.py` already does the identical thing at the `evaluate → register` boundary (DVC chain as one task, `register.py`'s promote/reject as a separate task after a review pause), so the mint-step split follows the same, already-established pattern one boundary earlier in the cycle.
>
> The stage table above already assumes the fix (both stages dep on `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`, not on a version), and `CLAUDE.md`'s resolution rule already permits it — *"`evaluate.py` resolves its model by explicit `run_id` **or** version number, never by alias"* — so this is a permitted resolution path, not a new exception. But the deliverables named only `calibrate.py` and `register.py`, so the work was invisible. **Add to PR B0's scope, not Phase 8's** — B1 is what removes the version, and B0 must precede it; see the sequencing note in the DAG-wide corrections. Teach these modules to resolve by `run_id` (reading the upstream stage's receipt, itself that stage's out), and keep `model_version` as an accepted alternative for the post-registration/rollback case. ⚠ **Two corrections to this bullet, both settled in the DAG-wide corrections below — read them rather than this sentence, which is left in place only because the surrounding scope list depends on it.** (1) It is **three** modules, not two: `calibrate.py:1443` requires `calibration.run_id` on the same terms and fails identically under `dvc repro`. (2) *"An error to pass both or neither"* cannot be applied literally — a DVC `cmd` is a static string that cannot interpolate a freshly minted run id, so **neither** is exactly what every stage receives under `dvc repro`, and the rule as written would fail the stages it was meant to protect. The rule is: **error on both; neither resolves from the upstream receipt** (`train_receipt.json` for `calibrate`, `calibrate_receipt.json` for `threshold`/`evaluate`). **Fold the comparison-baseline parameter into this same pass** — `evaluate.py` stops reading the `champion` alias and is given its baseline explicitly, for the reasons in the undeclared-dependency note below; it touches the same CLI, the same config file, and the same subprocess test, so doing it separately means paying for all three twice. Update both config comments, both `__main__` CLIs, and both subprocess integration tests, and update the Phase 6/7 Verification lines whose invocations embed `model_version=1` (see the notes on those lines). Log the resolved identifier into `reports/metrics.json` exactly as the existing rule requires — that rule is about attributability, and a run id satisfies it as well as a version number does.
>
> **✅ Done (2026-08-11, `chore/phase-8-prereqs`) — the comparison-baseline half only; the `run_id`/`model_version` half above was already resolved separately.** This bullet and the "Deliberate scope" section further below (its "if full champion reproducibility is ever required" paragraph) reached opposite conclusions on whether `evaluate.py` needed an explicit champion parameter now or could defer it to Phase 10's Prefect flow-start boundary — flagged and resolved in favour of doing it now, since an optional override costs nothing today and removes the undeclared dependency immediately rather than leaving it live through Phases 8 and 9. New `configs/evaluate/default.yaml: champion_version` (default `null`) and `evaluate.py::resolve_evaluation_champion`: an explicit version number is read verbatim and never touches the `champion` alias; the literal string `"none"` pins the cold-start regime explicitly; omitting it (the default) falls back to `resolve_champion_version`'s live alias read — the right behaviour for interactive/notebook use, never for `dvc repro`. `_compute_promotion_decision` calls the new resolver instead of `resolve_champion_version` directly. This does not by itself say who computes the version to pass in under `dvc repro` — that's still the resolved-alias-at-invocation-time step the "Deliberate scope" section assigns to whatever wraps the DVC chain (a hand-run operator today, Prefect from Phase 10) — it only removes the code-level obstacle to passing it in.

> **⚠ This conflicts with `CLAUDE.md`'s current MLflow Model Registry section and the already-shipped Phase 6/7 code — not just with an earlier draft of this plan.** `CLAUDE.md` states outright that *"Phase 6 `calibrate.py` performs the single registration of the calibrated pipeline and points `challenger` at it"* and that *"Phase 5 logs, does not register."* The `calibrate.py` merged in PR #15 does exactly that — mints the registry version and flips `challenger` itself. Adopting the split above therefore requires, beyond this phase: (1) refactoring the shipped `calibrate.py` to drop its registration call; (2) a new, explicitly non-DVC home for that call (resolved by the open question below); (3) a rewrite of `CLAUDE.md`'s registration-boundary rules — the "one registration per training cycle" paragraph, the Phase 5/6/7 responsibility list, and the `calibrate.py`/`register.py` split — so the two documents stop contradicting each other. Flagging this here rather than resolving it silently, per this project's own "flag schema-breaking changes" convention. **Not yet actioned, by decision, not oversight** — `CLAUDE.md` documents current shipped reality, not the roadmap, so rewriting its Model Registry section now would describe code that doesn't exist yet. `CLAUDE.md` and `calibrate.py` are updated together, in the same PR, at Phase 8 implementation time — not before.

> **📌 `reports/validation/` cannot be the `validate` stage's out as implemented — split the summary from the evidence.** Two independent defects, both fatal to it as a tracked output. (1) **Nothing is written on success:** `save_validation_report` returns `None` when every check passes (`validate.py:90-92`), so a *healthy* pipeline produces no output and DVC fails the stage. (2) **What it does write is non-deterministic:** each failing run mints a fresh `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS/` subdirectory (`validate.py:94-95`) — a dozen have already accumulated — so the directory hash changes every run and cascades `split` → `train` → everything downstream regardless of whether the data moved.
>
> **The fix is to separate the two jobs currently fused into one function.** A validation run produces a *summary* (which gates ran, how they resolved) and *evidence* (which rows offended). These have different consumers, different determinism requirements, and different lifetimes:
>
> | | **Receipt** — `reports/validation_receipt.json` (new) | **Evidence** — `reports/validation/<ts>/*.csv` (exists) |
> |---|---|---|
> | Written | Always — pass *or* fail | Only on failure |
> | Contents | Per-check name, pass/fail, `failure_severity`, `affected_rows` **count**, frame checksum | Pandera `failure_cases` — the offending **rows** |
> | Deterministic | Yes — no timestamp, no run id; same input ⇒ same bytes | No — new timestamped dir per run |
> | Tracked by | **DVC** — the stage's out; declare `cache: false` so it is git-tracked and diffable | Nothing — add `reports/validation/` to `.dvcignore` and `.gitignore` |
>
> The receipt is what the DAG needs: always present (so a passing stage doesn't fail), deterministic (so it doesn't cascade), and the thing that makes a raw-CSV change genuinely invalidate `validate` and therefore `split`. The evidence stays CSV — it is row-level tabular data a human opens in pandas — and stays local, unversioned, and messy, which is fine for a debugging side-channel. **Nothing is deleted; the CSVs simply stop being versioned.**
>
> **Where the results actually go — four layers, no new tools.** This is the standard shape for data validation (Great Expectations writes result JSON to a configured Store plus static Data Docs; dbt writes `target/run_results.json`; Deequ writes to a metrics repository), mapped onto what this project already has: **gate** — `sys.exit(1)` plus a `pipeline_blocked` structlog event (the Phase 2 cleanup bullet below); **artifact** — the DVC-tracked receipt above; **metrics** — Prometheus gauges in Phase 13; **notification** — `data-quality.yml` in Phase 11. Forward pointers are wired into both of those phases.
>
> **Not MLflow — and this is the point where the figures rule and the validation rule diverge.** `src/telco_churn/data/` imports zero MLflow today and should keep it that way. Data quality is upstream of and independent of any model: it runs on a different cadence than training, applies to data rather than models, and is meaningful when no model exists at all. Logging it to MLflow would create runs with no model and pollute the experiment list Phase 10 needs to keep comparable across retrain cycles. (The temptation is legitimate in origin — TFX's `ExampleValidator` genuinely does emit validation artifacts into ML Metadata — but MLMD is a *lineage store* where data artifacts are first-class, and MLflow's runs/experiments model is not that shape. In this project DVC is already the lineage store; `dvc.lock` is precisely the raw-CSV → validation → split → features → model chain.) So where figures resolve to "MLflow already owns them, drop the DVC out," validation resolves the opposite way: nothing owns it, which is exactly why it needs a well-formed DVC out.
>
> **Where "when did it fail" comes from, once the timestamp leaves the tracked artifact.** A timestamp inside a content-addressed file changes its hash every run and defeats the caching the DAG exists for — execution time is a property of the *run*, not the *content*. It moves to the four systems that carry time natively: **git history of the receipt** (`git log --follow reports/validation_receipt.json` gives the date *plus* the commit, author, and data version — strictly more than a directory name conveyed), **structlog events** (timestamped by construction; CloudWatch from Phase 12), **CI run history** (Phase 11's scheduled workflow, the durable record for unattended runs), and **Prometheus** (Phase 13 — the only one of the four that gives trending). *Stated limitation:* git records only failures that get committed, so a purely local failure leaves nothing beyond the terminal and the local CSV dir. Acceptable here — the dataset is a static CSV, so a validation failure means broken code, which CI catches. A live ingest with changing upstream data would need the log aggregator as system of record instead.

> **📌 Figures are not DVC outs — MLflow already owns them, foldered and per-run.** Earlier drafts of the stage table listed `figures/` as an out of `error_analysis`. That stage entry cannot be written as specified: `cfg.paths.figures` resolves to a single flat `reports/figures/` (`configs/config.yaml:45`) that **four** stages write into — `calibrate.py:1023` (reliability diagram), `evaluate.py:1529` (PR/ROC/gains/classification report), `economics.py` (EV-by-budget, sensitivity tornado, break-even heatmap), and `error_analysis.py` (the SHAP set). DVC rejects overlapping `outs` across stages, and a directory out is owned *exclusively* — `dvc checkout` deletes whatever the owning stage did not produce.
>
> **The collision is an artifact of the local mirror, not of the figures themselves.** In MLflow there is no collision, because each stage logs to a *different run* under its own artifact path: `comparison/`, `selection/`, `tuning/`, `calibration/figures/`, `threshold/figures/`, and `figures/` on the evaluation and error-analysis runs respectively. Partitioning `reports/figures/` into per-stage subdirectories would rebuild that namespace on disk — worse, and without the per-run attribution MLflow gives for free.
>
> **The general rule this settles: DVC tracks what the DAG consumes; MLflow owns terminal artifacts.** An `out` earns its place by **(a)** propagating staleness to a downstream stage, **(b)** being worth caching, or **(c)** being small and flat enough that its git diff is the thing a reviewer needs to see. **The third clause was unwritten for most of this phase's design and that omission had consequences**, so it is stated here rather than left implied: four surviving outs are justified by it alone (`promotion_decision.json`, `metrics_summary.json`, `decile_lift.csv`, `dev_oof_diagnostics.json`), stage 7's note miscites clause (b) for a file it declares `cache: false` — a file DVC does not cache cannot earn its place by being worth caching — and two large payloads (`economics.json`, and `error_analysis.json` at stage 9) were admitted under a two-clause rule that had no clause they actually met. A payload failing all three is an MLflow artifact plus an untracked mirror, not an out. Figures do neither — no stage declares a PNG as a dep, and MLflow already caches them with stronger attribution (immutable, per-run, reachable from the model version through the `eval_run_id` / `error_analysis_run_id` tags `register.py` resolves anyway). Contrast `reports/test_predictions.parquet`, which `error_analysis.py:1187` genuinely reads: that *must* be an out or the DAG has a hole. Every figure in this project is a leaf; every non-figure artifact in the table is not.
>
> **Decision — no stage declares figures.** `reports/figures/` stays as an untracked convenience mirror (add to `.gitignore` and `.dvcignore`): written, never read by code — the same status `reports/` already holds for `register.py`'s cross-cycle reads. Nothing in `README.md` or `ANALYSIS.md` embeds from it, so no external surface breaks.
>
> **⚠ It could not be a directory out even if it earned one — `reports/figures/` is written by four stages, and a DVC directory out is exclusively owned by a single stage.** `calibrate.py:1023` (1 figure), `threshold.py:764-779` (3), `evaluate.py:1530-1537` (8), `error_analysis.py:1587-1604` (16). Two stages naming the same `out` is a **parse-time DVC error** — `dvc.yaml` fails to load and *nothing in the pipeline runs*, including the stages that had nothing to do with figures. **State it mechanically rather than as a design argument, because that is what makes it survive:** the reasoning above (leaves, written-never-read, MLflow owns them) is a judgement someone can reasonably relitigate, and "declare `reports/figures/` and be done with it" is the obvious simplification to reach for. This is not a judgement. The only way to declare figures at all would be four disjoint per-stage subdirectories, which trades the whole convenience the directory exists for. Every downstream reference to this decision — the metrics/plots note's PNG paragraph, the MLflow-only-artifact rule, stages 6 and 7's out lists — inherits this reason.
>
> *Optional follow-on, not required by this phase:* migrate the four `savefig` → `mlflow.log_artifact` sites to `mlflow.log_figure(fig, "figures/x.png")`, which takes the figure object and writes no local file. `comparison.py:384` and `tuning.py:602` already use it, so this converges two in-repo patterns onto one and lets `reports/figures/` be deleted rather than merely ignored. Deferring it keeps the Phase 7 traceability row (`Lift / gains curves … → reports/metrics.json + reports/figures/`) accurate as written; adopting it requires that row repointed at the MLflow artifact path.

> **What `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` names, and doesn't.** It carries the `LoggedModel` id of the calibrated pipeline — plus the `run_id` downstream artifact fetches resolve against, and the `model_uri` that saves every reader reconstructing it. Hashable and file-referenced, which is why `evaluate` can depend on it directly and why changing the calibration method (`configs/calibration/`) correctly invalidates `evaluate` — **but only because the `LoggedModel` id is what moves; a run-id-only receipt would not, and that is the stage 6 note's subject.** It is not yet a registry **version** with an alias — that's the DVC stage's boundary: it produces the artifact, and `register.py` (resolved below) decides whether to register it.

> **📌 Why `training_setup.delta_threshold` survives the narrowing when the other eight keys don't — it decides whether this stage emits an artifact at all.** The obvious reading is that Δ\* only matters under `calibration.method='auto'`, where it settles sigmoid-vs-isotonic — and since the method is *pinned* to `sigmoid` (`configs/calibration/default.yaml:5`), that it is currently dormant. It is not. `pr_auc_gate_passes` (`calibrate.py:635`) runs on the **pinned** path too (`:755-772`): if the pinned method's per-fold mean AP falls more than Δ\* below the uncalibrated baseline, `calibrate.py` raises `ValueError` and exits 1 rather than shipping a model with degraded ranking. So on today's configuration Δ\* is the bar deciding whether the stage produces `calibrate_receipt.json` or produces nothing — and nothing downstream runs. A param that can flip a stage between *emits its out* and *fails* is the strongest case for declaration there is; leaving it untracked means `dvc repro` reporting the stage cached against a bar that has since moved.
>
> Note this key is declared under **both** stage 5 and stage 6, which is correct rather than duplication — `train` reads the same Δ\* for the LightGBM-vs-LogReg family decision (`calibrate.py:629`'s docstring names the shared lineage). Two stages reading one key means two declarations; DVC deduplicates nothing, and should not.

> **✅ RESOLVED — which *shared* modules belong in each stage's `deps:`: the curated per-file rule the stage designs already use — *modules whose contents change this stage's behaviour on unchanged data* — with the silent-gap risk closed by a test rather than by enumerating the import closure.** Listing only the entry module (`models/calibrate.py`) under-declares, and the gap is silent. `partition()` lives in `data/split.py` and is called by `calibrate`, `threshold`, and `evaluate`; change its merge semantics and all three carve the dev set differently, while `split_manifest.parquet` stays byte-identical — the manifest *generator* did not change, only its *reader*. Stage 3's out therefore propagates nothing, DVC reports three stages cached, and they serve results computed by code that no longer exists. No upstream `out` covers this class of edit, for `split.py`, `features/accessor.py`, or `features/build.py`.
>
> **The measurement that decided it.** Walking the first-party import graph gives closures of **18–22 modules** per model stage (`threshold` 18, `evaluate` 20, `error_analysis` 22), overlapping ~90% — everything transitively reaches `data.validate` → `data.checks` and `features.sql_features` → `utils.db`. So per-stage enumeration buys far less *precision* than it first appears. What it does buy is the exclusion of the modules that matter most: for `calibrate`, the closure omits `models/train/*`, `register.py`, `evaluate.py`, `error_analysis.py`, `economics.py`, `explain.py`, and `data/ingest.py`.
>
> **That omission is why `deps: src/telco_churn/` is rejected, and the argument is specific to this phase rather than aesthetic.** Phase 8's headline work is extracting the registration logic *into `register.py`* — many iterated edits. Under a whole-package dep every one of them invalidates `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis`, i.e. a full recalibration plus sealed-test re-evaluation, repeatedly, during unrelated work. Declaring `src/telco_churn/models/` instead inherits the same defect, since `register.py` lives there. The isolation being bought is precisely `train/` (the expensive half) and `register.py` (the actively-edited half).
>
> **But the closure is the wrong list to declare, and the reason is stage 6's, not a new one.** *"Declaring a marginal dep is free"* holds in stages 1–4, where the receipt is deterministic: a spurious rerun rewrites identical bytes and dies at its own out. **From `train` onward the receipts are identity tokens by design, so every spurious rerun changes the out and cascades** — `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis`. That re-prices over-declaration from *one wasted rerun* to *one wasted rerun plus three cascaded stages*, which is what makes curation worth its maintenance cost in this half of the DAG. Concretely, `features/build.py` is in every model stage's closure but is imported solely for `TARGET_COL`, whose value is materialised as a column name in the features parquet — already a dep, with a mismatch raising `KeyError` loudly. Declaring it would rerun three stages on a docstring edit to a module they never really consume.
>
> **So the rule is the curated one, and the rot objection is answered by enforcement rather than by discipline.** A hand-maintained list fails the same silent way as no list at all — someone adds an import and forgets the entry. So `tests/unit/test_architecture.py` computes the closure and asserts that **every reachable `telco_churn.*` module is either declared in that stage's `deps:` or named in an explicit `# dvc-dep-exempt: <reason>` exclusions block.** Coverage is total; the *decision* stays curated; and the exclusions become auditable in one place instead of implicit in an omission. Parse each stage's `cmd` for its entry module, walk the import graph by AST — ~25 lines, generalising the technique that file already uses. **This is the same piece of work as the `params:`-coverage check already scheduled for that file under the `Declare params:` bullet** — same file, same technique, same phase, deferred for the same reason (no `dvc.yaml` to guard until now). Implement them together.
>
> **⚠ This reverses an earlier draft of this note, which specified the full closure with "no inert-module allowlist."** That draft priced a spurious dep as a wasted rerun and concluded an exclusions list was second hand-maintained state not worth its cost. It is recorded here rather than deleted because the *conclusion* flipped on one input — the cascade above — and the exclusions list turns out to be the mechanism that makes the curation auditable rather than a parallel list to maintain. The per-file convention in the stage designs is authoritative; this note now follows it.
>
> **Two settled details.** (1) **The standing exclusions are `features/build.py` (constant materialised in the parquet), `models/plots.py` (leaf — untracked figures only), and the ubiquitous infrastructure `utils/db.py`, `utils/paths.py`, `utils/logging.py`, `utils/mlflow.py`** — each with its reason recorded at the stage that first argues it, and each requiring the exempt marker so the test still sees them. (2) **`models/train/` stays a directory dep** as stage 5 already has it: directory form is right where the directory belongs to exactly one stage, explicit files everywhere else, and the test must accept either.

> **Deliberate scope — why the DAG stops *before registration*, not before calibration.** Calibration and thresholding are, mechanically, no different from training: both are deterministic functions of hashable file/config inputs (a trained model + a dev fold + a method; calibrated probabilities + `configs/costs.yaml`) producing hashable file outputs. Excluding them from the DAG on the grounds that they are "decisions, not transforms" was too coarse a line — nothing about *fitting* a calibration map or *deriving* `t*` is any less reproducible than fitting the model itself, and drawing the boundary there meant `dvc repro` alone never produced a servable candidate, only a trained-but-uncalibrated pipeline.
>
> **The real dividing line is where a step stops being a file transform and starts mutating shared, external, pointer-based state.** That happens in exactly one place: minting an MLflow registry version and pointing an alias (`challenger` at candidate time, `champion` at promotion time). Two independent reasons this cannot be a DVC stage:
> 1. **`outs` must be files.** A registry version and an alias pointer are neither — the same constraint that already forces `ingest` and `train` to stand in receipt files for their real (DB row / MLflow run) outputs. There is no receipt-file trick that fixes this one, because the *action itself* — not just its result — is the side effect.
> 2. **DVC stages must be safe to force-rerun**, and `dvc repro --force` is listed in `CLAUDE.md`'s key commands as a routine thing to type. `register.py`'s comparative-regime decision (Phase 10+) compares the challenger against whatever the *live* `champion` currently is — mutable registry state DVC cannot hash as a dep. Folding it in would make `dvc repro`'s output depend on external state at the moment it happens to run, breaking the "same deps → same output" guarantee the rest of the DAG relies on.
>
> **Computing the verdict is not the same as acting on it, and that distinction is what let `evaluate` absorb the gate.** `gate.py::decide_promotion` is a pure function over the sealed-test metrics `evaluate` just produced — no live registry state involved in the cold-start regime, and even in the comparative regime it only *reads* the incumbent champion's already-published metrics baseline, never its alias. So `evaluate` stays in the DAG and writes `promotion_decision.json` as an ordinary file out; only the step that *reads that verdict and flips a pointer* (`register.py`) is excluded.
>
> What runs that step, and how the human-approval stamp gets recorded, is resolved directly below. If full champion reproducibility is ever required, the fix is to pin the comparison baseline to a specific run ID rather than the `champion` alias — not to add `register` as a DVC stage. **✅ Done ahead of Phase 10** — the B0-scope note above (§`evaluate.champion_version`) implements exactly this pin as an optional `evaluate.py` override, rather than waiting for Prefect. `evaluate.py` still defaults to a live alias read when the override is omitted, so this paragraph's "if full champion reproducibility is ever required" framing is now about *when a caller opts in*, not whether the code path exists.
>
> **Prefect's role narrows accordingly — it stops re-orchestrating what DVC already orchestrates.** The six-stage version's plan for Phase 10 had `training_cycle.py` calling `train → evaluate` and then `calibrate → threshold → register` as separate Prefect flow tasks, because the DAG didn't cover the middle two. With `calibrate`/`threshold` inside `dvc.yaml`, that collapses: `training_cycle.py` triggers the DVC-tracked chain (`ingest` through `evaluate` — `dvc repro` or an equivalent programmatic call) as a **single task**, then runs `register` as the one remaining, genuinely-Prefect-owned task. Prefect no longer needs to know that `calibrate` and `threshold` exist as distinct steps — that internal structure is DVC's concern now; Prefect's job is "run the reproducible pipeline, then decide whether to register." This is a cleaner division of labour than before: DVC owns *what needs to rerun* within the file-transform chain, Prefect owns *scheduling, retries, and the one action DVC structurally cannot perform.*
>
> **Why both, rather than Prefect alone — the two tools sit in different orchestrator families, and the distinction explains which problems in this phase Prefect can and cannot solve.** Orchestrators split roughly into two kinds. **Workflow-centric** ones (Prefect, Airflow) take the *task* as their unit — a function that runs — and own scheduling, retries, concurrency, observability, alerting, and human-in-the-loop pauses. **Asset-centric** ones (Dagster, Flyte, Kubeflow + ML Metadata) take the *data asset* as their unit — a thing that exists — and own lineage, materialization events, and per-version metadata. Prefect is the first kind. That is not a deficiency; it is a different job, and it is the job this project needs from it.
>
> **The consequence is that adding Prefect in Phase 10 does not retire this phase's receipt-file pattern, and expecting it to would be a misreading.** The receipts exist because DVC requires every out to be a file, so "an MLflow run happened" has to be given a file-shaped shadow — and the timestamp-in-a-hashed-file question follows directly from that. An asset-centric system has a designated home for execution metadata *beside* the artifact (Dagster's materialization metadata, MLMD's separate artifact/execution/event entities), so the question never arises. Prefect has run metadata too, but it does not own the file-transform chain — DVC does — so the receipts stay. Concretely, of the problems raised in this phase: Prefect **resolves** the undeclared champion dependency (it supplies the flow-start boundary where the alias is read once and passed in), the log-destination gap, and the human-review step; it leaves the receipt-determinism rule and the `dvc.lock`-is-not-a-CI-gate consequence exactly as they are, because both are properties of DVC.
>
> **Stated plainly so it can be defended rather than discovered:** removing the receipt pattern would mean replacing DVC with an asset-aware pipeline layer, not adding Prefect on top of it. That is a real architectural fork, and DVC is chosen deliberately — it is git-native (the lockfile is committed beside the code, so any checkout says which data produced which model), needs no running infrastructure, and works on a laptop. Prefect 3's own input-based task caching could cover part of DVC's ground, but not that git-native versioning, which is the property being bought. The cost of the choice is the receipt pattern; the cost is worth naming as a tradeoff rather than presenting the design as free.
>
> **⚠ Phase 10's `training_cycle.py` bullet, as currently written below, has not been updated for this and is now stale in two places:** it lists `runs ingest → validate → features → train → calibrate → evaluate → register` as discrete steps, and it states *"As in Phase 7, `calibrate.py` performs the cycle's only registration and `register.py` mints nothing new"* — both describe the six-stage design this section just moved away from. **Deferral reaffirmed, not just left over** — reconciling this needs a dedicated pass over Phase 10 as a whole (it touches `require_traffic_validation`, the dummy-floor guardrail, and the `require_review` open question together), not a patch bolted onto the Phase 8 discussion that surfaced it. Resolve before Phase 10 is implemented.

> **✅ RESOLVED — between Phase 8 and Phase 10, what actually runs `register` after `dvc repro` stops at `error_analysis`, and where does the human-approval stamp happen?** This question was narrower than it looked before calibration/thresholding/error-analysis moved into the DAG: `dvc repro` alone now produces both `promotion_decision.json` **and** `error_analysis.json` as ordinary file outputs — everything `register.py` structurally requires exists on disk with no manual script-chaining. What remained was the registration/review gap itself. `register.py` hard-blocks on `promotion_decision.json`'s `review` field being `"approved"` (`configs/register/default.yaml`'s `require_review: true`), stamped via `gate.py::record_review` — whose own docstring names "the notebook's closing cell" as the sole owner of loading and re-saving that file. So does a full re-run go `dvc repro` (ingest→validate→split→features→train→calibrate→threshold→evaluate→error_analysis) → open `notebooks/05-evaluation-and-error-analysis.ipynb` to stamp review → run `register.py` by hand? That gap was not hypothetical: this project's most recent clean-slate rerun hit it directly, and stamped review via a one-off inline script calling `record_review` instead of the notebook — workable, but not a documented, repeatable path. Options considered:
>
> 1. ~~Notebook-only, by design, until Phase 10.~~ Rejected — leaves the pipeline's final step with no non-Jupyter entry point for the entire Phase 8–10 gap, which is exactly the discipline this plan otherwise insists on elsewhere (subprocess-invokable `__main__` CLIs for every module).
> 2. **A small dedicated CLI — chosen.** `python -m telco_churn.models.review register.model_version=<v> review.verdict=approved review.notes="..."` wraps `gate.py::record_review`, re-logging `promotion_decision.json` onto the eval run — a first-class, reproducible entry point independent of Jupyter. ⚠ **Amended by the review-split prerequisite (PR B):** choosing a CLI fixed *who* stamps the verdict, not *what it writes to*. `record_review` still returns one fused document, so the CLI inherits the notebook's collision with `evaluate`'s DVC out verbatim. The verdict moves to its own artifact; this option's substance — a Jupyter-independent entry point, distinct from `register.py`'s CLI — is unchanged.
> 3. ~~Defer entirely to Phase 10.~~ Rejected — see below: most of what this "defers" is already shipped, so there is nothing left to defer.
>
> **Resolved in favour of option 2, and — once you look at what `register.py` already does — steps 3 and 4 of the four-step consolidation (mint → point `challenger` → read verdict & confirm `error_analysis` → flip/reject) turn out to require no new Phase 8 work at all.** Reading `promotion_decision.json`, checking `error_analysis_run_id`/`error_analysis.json` exist, and flipping `champion` (or leaving the version `rejected`) are already shipped, working `register.py` code from Phase 7 — including the `require_review` hard-block itself, and `register.py`'s own pre-existing `__main__` CLI (`python -m telco_churn.models.register register.model_version=<v>`; see the Phase 7 Verification line). **Checking for review approval is not Phase 10 work; it already works today.** So Phase 8's actual scope is narrow: move the mint-and-point-`challenger` logic *out* of `calibrate.py` (the extraction bullet above) and *into* `register.py`, so `register.py`'s existing CLI becomes the single entry point doing all four steps — just now also minting.
>
> **Concretely, a full manual cycle pre-Phase-10 is three commands, not one, and the two CLIs involved are distinct scripts with distinct jobs.** `dvc repro` produces `promotion_decision.json`/`error_analysis.json` → the review CLI from option 2 above (`python -m telco_churn.models.review`) stamps the verdict, and never touches the registry → `register.py`'s own CLI reads the now-stamped verdict, mints, and flips/rejects, and never stamps review. Collapsing these into one command is not what "the CLI above" in earlier drafts of this note meant to imply, and reads that way on a second pass — corrected here.
>
> **What Phase 10 actually adds is narrower than "the review step": an orchestration wrapper around those same two CLIs, not a rewrite of either.** The Prefect `pause_flow_run`/`Suspend` → notify → resume mechanism (Phase 10 §`require_review` open question, below) collapses the last two manual commands into one task: a reviewer's resume submits the verdict, which invokes `record_review` (the review CLI's underlying function) and then immediately runs `register.py`'s mint/flip logic within that same task — no change to either script's internals, only to what triggers them. Phase 10's only genuinely new decision is the escalation *policy* — whether `require_review` stays `false` for the unattended cycle or re-escalates (options 1–3 on that question) — not the mechanics of registration or review-checking themselves.
>
> Resolved — implement the full four-step consolidation in Phase 8; nothing here is deferred to Phase 10.

> **The receipt-file pattern applies to `train` too.** An MLflow run ID cannot literally be a DVC `out` — outs are files. It surfaces as a receipt file (`reports/train_receipt.json`), the same pattern `ingest` uses to sequence around Postgres and `calibrate` uses for its own `LoggedModel` id: a stage's real coupling to MLflow is made DVC-visible by depending on the *previous* stage's receipt, not by depending on MLflow directly. (`calibrate`'s token is deliberately *not* a run id — it has no run of its own to name; see the identity-token caveat below.)

> **📌 Receipt determinism is a rule, not an accident of how the first one happened to be written.** A tracked `out` may contain **only values derived from that stage's inputs** — no `datetime.now()`, no `uuid4()`, no hostname, no run counter. DVC decides staleness by comparing bytes, and one stage's out is the next stage's dep, so a field that moves for reasons unrelated to the data cascades the whole downstream chain. Note that `cache: false` does **not** exempt a file: it only stops DVC storing copies in the cache; the hash still enters `dvc.lock` and still invalidates downstream.
>
> **`reports/ingest_receipt.json` is the one file this rule must reach before it is written.** Every other out in the stage table is already the stage's real result — a parquet of features, a JSON of metrics — with contents fixed by what was computed; nobody chooses what goes in them. The ingest receipt is the only out invented purely to stand in for a side effect (rows in Postgres), so its contents are a free choice, and the word *receipt* pulls toward the wrong one: receipts in ordinary life record a transaction *and its time*. `{"rows_loaded": 7043, "loaded_at": "..."}` is the natural thing to write, feels like more provenance rather than less, and is the defect. The stage table above already specifies the correct contents — row count, per-column null counts, frame checksum — and this note is the reason.
>
> The cost of getting it wrong is not constant churn (`ingest`'s own deps are static, so it is normally a cache hit) but a **cascade disproportionate to its cause**: edit a docstring in `data/ingest.py`, `ingest` reruns, the timestamp changes, and `validate` → `split` → `features` → `train` (50 Optuna trials) → `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis` all rerun to produce a byte-identical model. Nothing errors and nothing warns; the symptom is *"why does touching `ingest.py` retrain everything?"*, asked weeks later by someone with no reason to suspect a JSON field. The `when` is already recorded anyway — `utils/logging.py:44` puts a UTC `TimeStamper` in `shared_processors`, so `ingest.py:175`'s `merge_complete` event already carries the time *and* the row counts. A `loaded_at` field would duplicate a fact already captured, into the one medium that punishes it.
>
> **Identity tokens are the stated exception, and the reason they are safe is worth being precise about.** `reports/train_receipt.json` and `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` carry a freshly minted MLflow identifier on every execution by construction — there is nothing to derive them from, because their content *is* an identity token. They cannot invalidate spuriously, because the identifier changes only when the stage genuinely executed, and if `train` genuinely reran there is a new model and everything downstream *should* rerun. Their nondeterminism is aligned with real staleness; a timestamp's is not, because it fires when nothing changed. **That alignment, not derivability, is the operative test** — the derived-values phrasing above is the rule of thumb that satisfies it in the ordinary case, not the thing being checked.
>
> **⚠ "A fresh run id" is the wrong token for `calibrate`, and getting this wrong silently deletes an edge — see the stage 6 receipt note below.** The alignment test says the content must move iff **this** stage executed. A run id satisfies that for `train`, which creates a run. It does **not** for `calibrate`, which *resumes* `train`'s run (`mlflow.start_run(run_id=run_id)`) and therefore has no run id of its own: its receipt would hold `train`'s id, moving only when *train* reran. Flip the calibration method, and `calibrate` refits while its receipt stays byte-identical, so `threshold`/`evaluate`/`error_analysis` never see the change. **The fix is the `logged_model_id`** — minted fresh by each `log_model` call, so it moves iff this stage produced a new artifact. Stated as a rule for whoever adds stage ten: *the exception licenses an identity token, not a run id specifically; pick the identifier whose lifecycle matches the stage's own.*
>
> **The exception covers more than the two receipt files, and saying so prevents a plausible future "cleanup" from breaking something load-bearing.** Three JSON outs embed the same class of identifier inside otherwise-derived content: `metrics.json` carries `run_id`, `model_version`, and `champion_version` (`evaluate.py:1452-1454`); `promotion_decision.json` and `error_analysis.json` carry the evaluated `model_version` likewise. (`threshold.py` writes its identity tokens into the two payloads that go to **MLflow** — `threshold.json` and `threshold_validation.json`, `threshold.py:867-868, 889-890` — while its two DVC outs are token-free by construction: `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` is deliberately model-independent, carrying `costs_config_hash` and no run id, and `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json` is pure diagnostic content. The `threshold` stage therefore needs no exception at all.) Read literally against the derived-values phrasing these look like violations; under the alignment test they are correct, because each changes only when its producing stage actually ran. **Do not strip them.** `metrics.json`'s `champion_version` in particular is read by `register.py::_reverify_incumbent` (`:1292-1310`) to refuse a flip against a stale comparison — removing it in the name of determinism would delete a safety check to satisfy a rule that never prohibited it.
>
> **Consequence to record now, because Phase 11 is where it bites: `dvc.lock` is not a reproducibility oracle in this project and must not be a CI gate.** `dvc.lock` is a list of fingerprints — for each stage, a hash of every input and every output. The standard CI idiom is `dvc repro && git diff --exit-code dvc.lock`, on the reasoning that if re-running the pipeline changes the fingerprints then the committed ones were wrong (someone edited code without re-running, or a stage isn't deterministic). It is a good check on pipelines whose outputs are pure data transforms. It is not one here.
>
> **The analogy that makes it obvious:** to test a recipe, you cook the dish again, photograph it, and compare against the stored photo. Fine — until the camera stamps the current time onto every photo, at which point the two never match even when the dish is identical every time. The comparison has stopped saying anything about the dish. `reports/train_receipt.json` is the timestamp on the photo: it holds a brand-new MLflow run id per execution, which is a label for the run, not a fact about the model.
>
> **Both possible CI configurations are useless, which is a stronger claim than "the check is flaky":**
>
> | CI setup | What happens | Result |
> |---|---|---|
> | Fresh clone, no cache restored | Every stage runs → new run ids → lock moves | **Fails**, with nothing wrong |
> | Cache restored | Nothing runs → nothing rewritten → lock holds | **Passes**, having executed nothing |
>
> Red when the pipeline is healthy, green when it did no work. There is no third setup to configure it into usefulness, because the check tests whether *outputs are byte-identical* while standing in for *whether results are reproducible* — and those two claims come apart the moment one legitimate out is an identity token.
>
> **Gate on these three instead:** (1) **`dvc repro` exits 0** — the pipeline actually executed end to end, which is the real reproducibility test since it runs rather than compares labels; (2) **`dvc metrics diff main`** — did the *numbers* move, which is what a PR reviewer wants ("PR-AUC dropped 0.02", not "a filename changed"); (3) **`dvc status`, once Phase 12's S3 remote allows `dvc pull`** — this catches the one thing the naive idiom was genuinely reaching for, a config edited and committed without re-running, because it compares *deps* against the lock and never inspects whether outs are byte-identical. Recorded here rather than in Phase 11 because Phase 11 is where someone reaches for the idiom, and by then this reasoning exists only in a conversation.

> **⚠ `evaluate` has an undeclared dependency on live registry state — the opposite failure mode from the one above.** *(Read the fix paragraphs before acting on this: the dangerous consequence is already blocked by shipped Phase 7 code, and what remains is narrower than the framing here first suggests.)* `evaluate.py:653` resolves the current `champion` alias, and that value drives three things: which **gate regime** runs (no champion → cold start against absolute bars; champion present → comparative against the incumbent — the docstring at `:641-647` is explicit that misreading this "would silently switch the gate to the wrong regime"), `load_incumbent_proba` (`:661`, which loads and scores the champion on the same sealed-test rows), and `resolve_incumbent_summary` (`:681`, the incumbent's own gate-criteria tags). None of it is a DVC dep, because a registry alias is not a file. So the promotion verdict is a function of state the DAG cannot see.
>
> Concretely: `dvc repro` with no champion yet computes a cold-start verdict; `register.py` then promotes one; the next `dvc repro` finds every dep unchanged, skips `evaluate`, and leaves a cached `promotion_decision.json` holding a verdict reached under a regime that no longer applies. Rollback is the sharper case — re-point `champion` from v4 to v3 and every cached evaluation still compares against v4, with nothing anywhere marking it stale. **Note the direction:** a timestamp makes DVC rerun when nothing changed, which is wasteful but visible; this makes DVC *not* rerun when something did, which is silent — and would reach a promotion decision were it not for the guard described below. What remains after that guard is a `dvc status` that reports a stale evaluation as current — misleading to a human reading the DAG, though no longer able to mislead the registry. The pinned-baseline change below closes that half too, which is why it is scheduled into this phase rather than deferred.
>
> **A champion receipt is the obvious fix and it does not work — record why, because it is the first thing anyone will reach for.** The shape: `register.py` writes `reports/champion_version.txt` on every alias flip, `evaluate` declares it a dep. This is *not* circular, despite `register.py` running after `evaluate` — DVC draws an edge only where one stage's `out` is another's `dep`, and this file is an `out` of nothing (`register.py` is not a stage), so DVC treats it as a source node like `configs/costs.yaml`, written between runs by something outside the DAG. The DAG stays acyclic.
>
> **What breaks it is the promotion itself.** A successful cycle ends with `register.py` flipping `champion` onto the model just evaluated and rewriting the receipt. `evaluate` is now stale, and the rerun resolves an incumbent that *is* the candidate — a self-comparison, producing deltas of zero and a meaningless verdict that overwrites the correct one on disk. `dvc repro` would also never return clean after a promotion; it would want to redo `evaluate` and `error_analysis` every time, forever. A dep that is invalidated *by the act of consuming its own stage's output* is not a dependency, it is a loop laundered through a file.
>
> **The stamp-and-cross-check defence already exists — Phase 7 built it, and this phase adds one line to it.** `evaluate.py:1454` records `champion_version` into `metrics.json`, and `register.py::_reverify_incumbent` (`:1292-1310`) compares that recorded value against the live alias immediately before the flip, raising *"champion moved from X (recorded at evaluation time) to Y since evaluation — refusing to flip against a stale comparison."* Its docstring is explicit that the check sits immediately before the flip rather than earlier. This is the same pattern as `check_threshold_provenance` (`evaluate.py:250-269`) and inherits its virtues: free when correct, and incapable of firing spuriously, since a promotion does not move the alias until after the check has passed. **So the danger is already contained at the point of action, and the DVC-staleness gap above is a legibility problem rather than an unguarded correctness one.**
>
> **The one real hole is the cold-start branch, which is unguarded by construction.** `_reverify_incumbent` only compares when `recorded_champion_version is not None`. A cycle evaluated with no champion records `None`, so the check is skipped entirely — and that is precisely the transition described above: a cold-start verdict, computed against absolute bars, applied after a champion has come into existence. **Fix (small, this phase): when the recorded value is `None`, assert the live champion is `None` too, and raise the same error if it is not.** The absence of an incumbent is as much a fact about the evaluation as its identity is, and the current guard verifies one but not the other.
>
> **The structural fix — pin the baseline instead of reading the alias — and do it *in this phase*, not as a deferred upgrade.** Delete `resolve_champion_version`'s call site from `evaluate.py` and make the baseline something `evaluate` is *told*: an explicit version/run id supplied by its caller. The undeclared dependency then stops existing rather than being tracked, the value becomes an ordinary hashable config input (declare it under `params:`, and DVC's staleness view is correct with no receipt at all), and nothing self-compares because the baseline moves only when someone deliberately moves it. This is already this phase's stated preference for exactly this problem — see the scope note above: *"pin the comparison baseline to a specific run ID rather than the `champion` alias."*
>
> **Why this is acyclic where the receipt was not, in one sentence:** the receipt is written by the *last* step and consumed by a *middle* one, so the act of finishing a cycle invalidates a step that already ran; a pinned baseline is supplied at cycle *start*, so it is an input in the ordinary sense. Same value, different moment — and the moment is the whole difference.
>
> **Who supplies it.** Manually (pre-Phase 10), a CLI argument, exactly as `model_version` already is. Under Phase 10, `training_cycle.py` resolves the alias **once at flow start** and passes it into the evaluate step as a parameter. That is the same principle this phase already applies to `register.py`: mutable shared state is touched at the orchestration boundary, never inside a DAG stage — reading it inside `evaluate` is the same category error as writing it inside would be.
  >
  > **State it as a general rule here, because `evaluate` is not the last module that will want it.** *Mutable shared state — registry aliases, live champion metadata — is resolved once at the orchestration boundary and passed into stages as explicit parameters; no DAG stage resolves it itself.* `register.py` and `evaluate.py` are the two current instances; **`train` is the next one**, and it is already visible. Phase 10's open question on whether the weekly cycle re-searches hyperparameters or refits with pinned ones has a `pinned` branch whose values come from the champion's logged params — another live alias read, which this rule forbids inside a stage and which therefore has to arrive as a parameter the same way the comparison baseline does. **Nothing about that is Phase 8 work** — the policy is Phase 10's to decide, and implementing a mode knob here would be building a later phase's feature. What Phase 8 owes it is exactly two things, both free: state the rule in general form (this paragraph), and note that `configs/tuning/optuna.yaml` is already declared under `train`'s `params:`, so whatever knob Phase 10 adds is DVC-visible without reopening the params declaration.
>
> **One ambiguity to design out:** `baseline_version: null` must not mean both *"cold start, genuinely no incumbent"* and *"nobody passed one."* Use an explicit sentinel for the former and make unset an error, the same both-or-neither discipline this phase already applies to `model_version`/`run_id`.
>
> **The existing `_reverify_incumbent` guard survives this change and gains meaning — it does not become redundant.** `metrics.json` still records whichever baseline was used, and `register.py` still compares it against the live alias before flipping. Under pinning that comparison answers a sharper question: *was the pinned baseline still the champion at the moment of the flip?* A deliberate pin to some earlier version for analysis will now correctly refuse to promote, which is right — a promotion must rest on a comparison against the model actually serving, not against whichever one was interesting to look at.
>
> **Scheduling: this belongs in the same pass as the `run_id` change, not after it.** Phase 8 is *already* changing `evaluate.py`'s resolution logic, its CLI, `configs/evaluate/default.yaml`, and its subprocess test (see the blocking note above). Adding the baseline parameter to that same pass costs one more argument; deferring it to Phase 10 means touching all four surfaces twice, for a change Phase 10 forces anyway — an unattended weekly cycle cannot rest on "whatever the alias happened to say when the job ran."

> **⚠ The `train` stage has a second runtime dependency DVC's `deps` can't see: `POSTGRES_URL`, for Optuna's tuning storage.** `models/train/tuning.py::_build_optuna_storage()` needs a live Postgres connection to persist trial state in its own `optuna` schema — not a file, so it can't be expressed as a DVC `dep` any more than the MLflow edge above can. As of the Phase 7 QA hardening pass, `_build_optuna_storage()` falls back to a local SQLite file (`sqlite:///<project_root>/optuna.db`) when `POSTGRES_URL` is unset, so `dvc repro` / `python -m telco_churn.models.train` still completes on a fresh clone or in CI with no Docker services running — the same zero-infra fallback `configs/config.yaml`'s `tracking_uri` already uses for MLflow. Today `train` always runs on the host (`docker-compose.yml` only containerizes `postgres`/`mlflow`, under the `infra` profile), so this doesn't yet bite. **It becomes load-bearing in Phase 10:** once the Prefect retrain flow runs `train` unattended, whatever executes it must have `POSTGRES_URL` wired in explicitly — the same way `docker-compose.yml`'s `mlflow` service already gets its `--backend-store-uri` — or the SQLite fallback will silently activate instead of raising a clear "not configured" error, and if that environment is an ephemeral container with no volume mounted, the fallback study data is lost on restart. Wire it when Phase 10's `docker-compose.yml`/Prefect worker config is written, rather than rediscovering the gap the way it was found here (a CI run with no infra started).
