> **📌 Required in this phase — replace `_dvc_hash` with a content hash, or every model registered from here on carries empty provenance. ✅ Done (2026-08-14, `chore/phase-8-prereqs`) — folded into that prerequisite commit rather than held for the DVC wrap itself.** `_dvc_hash` is retired (only a docstring reference to the old name survives, `tuning.py:336`), and every stage that mints a manifest — `candidates.py`, `comparison.py`, `feature_audit.py`, `feature_selection.py`, `log_model.py`, `tuning.py` — now writes `data_content_hash: features_sha256()` unconditionally, no `"unknown"` fallback. The `_study_name()` freshness consumer and the test fallout enumerated below landed in the same commit. Left in place as history rather than compressed away, since it explains *why* the fix was needed, not just that it happened. `models/train/common.py:73` resolves `<processed_data>/telco_churn_processed.csv.dvc` and falls back to `"unknown"` on `FileNotFoundError`, logged at **debug**. That fallback is correct today (DVC isn't initialised yet) and becomes a **silent failure the moment this phase lands**, for two compounding reasons. (1) The filename changes — the `features` stage outputs `telco_churn_features.parquet`. (2) More fundamentally, **`.dvc` sidecar files exist only for `dvc add`-ed files**; pipeline *stage outputs* are tracked in **`dvc.lock`**, under `stages.features.outs`. So no `.dvc` file for the parquet will ever exist, whatever it is named. The lookup misses, the handler shrugs, the provenance hash is `"unknown"` forever, and nothing warns — the fallback for *"not tracked yet"* is indistinguishable from *"tracked, but I'm reading the wrong file."*
>
> **Fix — do not read DVC internals at all; reuse the on-disk content hash the repo already computes.** `evaluate.py` already `sha256`s the features artifact it read, for provenance (Phase 7), and deliberately uses that direct content hash rather than `_dvc_hash` *precisely because the recorded-vs-on-disk gap makes the DVC hash unsuitable*. Rather than teach a second mechanism to parse `dvc.lock` — coupling provenance to DVC's file format to record a hash that gates nothing — **retire `_dvc_hash` and populate the manifest's data-lineage field from that same features-parquet `sha256`.** This buys three things: one hashing mechanism instead of two; no coupling to DVC's lockfile format; and a field that populates **unconditionally** — it reflects the bytes actually trained on, with no dependence on whether `dvc repro` re-recorded the lock. Rename the manifest field `dvc_data_hash` → `data_content_hash` and delete the now-dead `_dvc_hash` helper; the old name actively misleads once the value no longer comes from DVC. Add a test asserting a stable, non-`"unknown"` hash for a fixed features artifact. DVC keeps doing what it is actually good at here — the pipeline DAG and content-hashed staleness — without any code reading its lockfile.
>
> **Add `mlflow.log_input` for first-class, UI-visible lineage.** Alongside the manifest hash, log the training set as an MLflow dataset in the `train` stage — `mlflow.log_input(mlflow.data.from_pandas(df, source=...), context="training")`. MLflow computes its own digest, surfaces it in the run UI, and it pairs natively with the `LoggedModel` + `(model, dataset)` metric model this project already uses (Phase 7 evaluation logging). This is the idiomatic MLflow 3 lineage story and the more demonstrable one for a portfolio than a bespoke hash field; the `data_content_hash` remains the machine-checkable audit value, `log_input` the human-facing one.
>
> **The Optuna study-freshness consumer resolves the same way — and more strictly than the `dvc.lock` route would.** The Phase 5→6 Bridge discovered empirically that `tuning.py::_study_name()` content-addresses the Optuna study by hashing the data hash alongside `committed_features`, `search_space`, `cv_folds`, and the other tuning-config knobs, and `optuna.create_study(..., load_if_exists=True)` **resumes** any existing study sharing that name. While the data hash is a fixed `"unknown"` string, a genuine data change does *not* change the study name, so the tuning step silently resumes a study built on the *old* data, mixing incompatible trials from two datasets into one 1-SE selection pool — with nothing in the logs to flag it. Point `_study_name()` at the new `data_content_hash`: a content `sha256` changes the instant the features bytes change, independent of whether `dvc repro` re-recorded the lock, so it is a *stricter* freshness signal than a DVC-recorded hash would be. (`load_if_exists=True` itself is correct and intentional, for genuine crash recovery mid-optimization; the only defect was that `"unknown"` couldn't distinguish "same run resumed" from "different data entirely.")
>
> **This means the first post-Phase-8 training run is expected to produce different tuning numbers than whatever is currently recorded in `ANALYSIS.md` §4c — that is correct, not a regression.** The moment the data hash becomes real, `_study_name()`'s SHA256 digest changes, Optuna finds no existing study under the new name, and starts a genuinely fresh 50-trial search on the next `python -m telco_churn.models.train` (or `dvc repro`) run. The old `"unknown"`-keyed study rows are left behind, harmless, in the `optuna` Postgres schema (orphaned, not corrupted — `DROP SCHEMA optuna CASCADE` clears them if desired, but nothing requires it). Add a regression test: two `run_tuning_step` calls with different `data_content_hash` inputs (everything else held fixed) must produce different study names.
>
> **Ripple — one other *documentation* touch-point, not a Phase 7 reopening. (The code scope is separate and larger: six modules.)** `_dvc_hash` is defined in `train/common.py` and imported by `candidates.py`, `comparison.py`, `feature_audit.py`, `log_model.py`, and `tuning.py` — one definition plus five importers. Mechanically small, but it touches six files, which is what the estimate below accounts for; naming them here stops the scope being re-argued at implementation time. The documentation ripple is the single item that follows: Phase 5's `training_manifest.json` records this slot as "best-effort metadata that gates nothing, `unknown` by design" (DVC is not yet initialised when Phase 7 first runs); under this change it becomes `data_content_hash` carrying a real hash unconditionally. The **initial Phase-7 champion's manifest** keeps `"unknown"` — it was written before this fix: honest historical record, not something to rewrite. Update `CLAUDE.md`'s `training_manifest.json` field description to name `data_content_hash` and its `sha256` source.
>
> **Ripple — the test fallout, which the paragraph above does not reach.** Every test named in this phase's other notes is one being *added*; these are the existing ones this change *breaks*, in five files. Red test runs rather than silent bugs, so the risk is low — but the point of enumerating the six source modules above was to stop the scope being re-argued at implementation time, and this is the half that enumeration missed.
> - **`test_train_common.py:63-106` — three tests deleted with the function.** `test_dvc_hash_returns_unknown_and_logs_debug_when_file_missing`, `…_returns_hash_when_file_present`, `…_warns_on_malformed_file` test `_dvc_hash` directly, along with the section comment at `:27`. Nothing replaces them: `test_accessor.py:166-202` already covers `features_sha256`'s content-hash behaviour (manual-hash equality, stability across calls, change on content change), which is the whole of what survives.
> - **`test_train_candidates.py:150,237` — two `monkeypatch.setattr(candidates, "_dvc_hash", …)` calls** against a symbol that will not exist. Retarget to whatever the modules end up importing, or drop the patch entirely if a real hash over the test's own features file is cheap enough — the fixture parquet is small, and patching a hash to `"unknown"` is now patching in the exact failure state the change exists to eliminate.
> - **Three field-name assertions plus their test names and docstrings.** `test_train_comparison.py:506` and `test_train_feature_audit.py:300` assert `"dvc_data_hash" in run.data.tags`; `test_train_log_model.py:216` asserts it in the manifest. **Renaming the key alone leaves all three passing vacuously in the worst case and failing in the best** — pair each with the sentinel exclusion the Phase 5 Verification amendment already specifies (`!= "unknown"`), so the assertion tests the field's purpose rather than its presence. The test names and docstrings at `test_train_comparison.py:484,490` and `test_train_feature_audit.py:279,285` carry the old name too.

> **Why `split` is its own stage, not folded into `validate` or `features`:** `data/split.py` depends only on `(customerid, churn)` from the validated raw table — never on an engineered feature — so it belongs strictly after `validate` and strictly before `features`, mirroring the module's own docstring. Hashing it separately means changing `features/build.py` never invalidates the split (the `features` stage doesn't depend on it), and changing `test_size`/`random_seed` reruns `split` → `train` → `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` without re-triggering `features`. `train`, `calibrate`, and `evaluate` all declare `split_manifest.parquet` as a dep — they are its three consumers (`data.split.partition()`/`dev_ids()` for `train`'s CV folds and `calibrate`'s dev-fold fitting, `test_ids()` for `evaluate`'s sealed-test scoring) — so a manifest change correctly invalidates all three, giving an explicit provenance chain from raw-data version → split manifest → model that was previously missing.

> **Feature versioning and lineage — what Phase 8 closes:** the `features` stage deps (`features/build.py` + `sql/features/*.sql` + the DVC-tracked raw CSV) are content-hashed by DVC. This is the *provenance* half of feature lineage — it records exactly which feature-engineering code ran on which data version to produce the processed dataset. The *membership* half is already covered by Phase 5 MLflow artifacts (`feature_space.txt` — what was available; `feature_columns.txt` — what was selected). Cross-referencing the DVC `features` stage cache entry with the MLflow run ID (logged as a `train` stage out) gives a complete, reproducible lineage chain: raw data version → feature code version → feature space → selection decision → model. This combination — DVC for provenance, MLflow for membership — is the standard approach for projects without a dedicated feature store.

- **No DVC remote is required, in this phase or any phase before 12.** A local cache is the whole configuration. Phase 12 adds an S3 remote as a genuine capability (shared cache, artifact storage), but nothing depends on it — see the two notes below.

> **⚠ Postgres is a materialised cache of the CSV, not a data source — and it cannot be a DVC `out`.** Earlier drafts of the stage table declared `ingest`'s output as "`customers_raw` table hash." **DVC `outs` must be files or directories in the workspace.** There is no table-hash out; `dvc repro` cannot cache it, restore it, or detect drift in it. The stage as previously specified could not be written, and pointing DVC at the database would not fix it — a DB is mutable external state and DVC is a content-addressed file store. They do not compose.
>
> The resolution is to see that the table's contents are *fully determined* by three things DVC **can** hash: the raw CSV, `sql/schema/001_create_raw.sql`, and `data/ingest.py`. The guarantee this phase ships — raw CSV → features Parquet — therefore holds with Postgres living **inside** the implementation rather than as a node in the graph. `ingest` emits a **receipt file** (`reports/ingest_receipt.json`) as its out: the hashable artifact standing in for the side effect, giving `dvc repro` something to cache and `validate` something to depend on. **An earlier draft justified this by claiming `validate` already did the same thing — that `reports/validation/` "is a receipt in all but name, and `ingest` is simply the stage that never got one." That is exactly backwards, and the receipt note above is why:** `reports/validation/` is failure-only and timestamp-named, so it is neither always-present nor deterministic — the two properties that make a receipt work. Both stages need one built; `ingest` is not catching up to `validate`, they are getting the same new thing. The pattern itself stands unchanged — a hashable artifact standing in for a side effect DVC cannot express — it simply has no prior instance in this repo to point at.
>
> **The limitation, stated rather than discovered:** if someone hand-edits `customers_raw`, the receipt and every dep are unchanged, so DVC will not notice. That is inherent and acceptable — **the CSV is the source of truth, the table never is**, and `dvc repro --force` rebuilds it. Any workflow that treats the DB as authoritative is outside the guarantee. (The SQL layer exists because Phase 4's feature engineering is deliberately done in SQL views as a portfolio demonstration; drop Postgres and you drop that. Keep it, and the DAG needs a receipt to sequence around it.)

> **⚠ The raw CSV is committed to git — DVC lists it as a `dep`, it does not own it.** `deps` and `outs` are different contracts. `outs` are what DVC *owns*: cached, gitignored, replaced by a pointer. `deps` are what DVC *watches*: hashed into `dvc.lock` so it knows when to re-run. **A dep may be any path in the repo** — it need not be `dvc add`-ed, gitignored, or in a remote. Change the CSV and `dvc repro` re-runs everything downstream; lineage is fully preserved.
>
> DVC should own raw data when it is too large for git, changes over time, or needs its own version history. **None of those hold here:** the file is 977 KB, static, and `CLAUDE.md` marks `datasets/raw/` read-only. What DVC actually buys this project is the pipeline DAG — `dvc repro`, content-hashed staleness, cross-stage lineage — and that works with a local cache and no remote at all. The derived artifacts (`telco_churn_features.parquet`, `split_manifest.parquet`) are *regenerable by definition*; a fresh clone rebuilds them rather than pulling them, so a remote for them is a compute-saving cache, never a correctness requirement.
>
> **This is what makes the fresh-clone reproducibility claim (End-to-End Verification §1) true.** Gitignoring a one-megabyte static CSV behind a local cache would make the repo un-clonable in exchange for nothing, and would leave Phase 11's CI unable to fetch data from a remote that does not exist until Phase 12. The competence a reviewer looks for is in `dvc.yaml`, `dvc.lock`, and `dvc repro` — not in whether a small static file is gitignored. *(Licensing: the dataset is IBM's Cognos Analytics sample, redistributed in thousands of public repositories. Low risk, but it is a judgment call.)*

- **SQL view materialisation (required):** The Phase 4 SQL views recompute on every read — acceptable for development but not for a DVC pipeline. The `features` stage entry point must execute the SQL graph **once** — `build_sql_features(engine, sql_dir)`, then `read_sql_table("customer_features", engine, columns=SQL_FEATURE_COLS)` into `build_feature_df(df)` (which takes a DataFrame, not an engine) — then immediately write the result to `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet` before exiting. The `train` stage lists that Parquet file as its sole data dependency, not Postgres. This gives three guarantees: (1) every training run reads a static, content-hashed snapshot; (2) `dvc repro` never blocks on the DB when the features hash is unchanged; (3) if Postgres is unavailable, all downstream stages still run from the cached Parquet — **on a machine that has already run the pipeline once, which is the only case this guarantee covers.** The DB is only contacted during the `features` stage, which DVC skips if its deps (raw CSV + `build.py` + SQL files) are unchanged.
  > **⚠ Guarantee (3) is a warm-machine property and must not be read as a fresh-clone one — the two cases are exactly the ones that matter, and they resolve opposite ways.** `datasets/processed/` is gitignored and there is no DVC remote before Phase 12, so a clean checkout has nothing to restore from: the cache that would rescue you exists only if you already ran the thing it would be proving you can run. On a fresh clone the `features` stage *must* reach a live database. That is a precondition to state, not a defect to fix (the SQL feature work is deliberate) — see the fresh-clone bullet below for the CI consequence.

- **Rewrite `build.py`'s `__main__` block in place — it is not deleted. ✅ Decided (see the stage 4 entry).** The `if __name__ == "__main__"` block in `src/telco_churn/features/build.py` is a Phase 4 development scaffold — it wires together the full feature pipeline (config load → DB connect → SQL views → `build_feature_df` → write CSV) so the pipeline could be verified manually before DVC existed. In Phase 8 it *becomes* the DVC `features` stage entry point, with three changes: Parquet output instead of CSV, a zero-row guard, and DVC managing invocation. **The fourth change this note used to claim — `compose_config(overrides=sys.argv[1:] or None)` + `activate_config(cfg)` in place of `load_config()` (`build.py:71`) — moves to the prerequisite PR, where it is not optional:** deleting the `oc.env` wrapper there is what strands `test_sql_features_postgres.py:295`, whose redirect names an *output* directory, so leaving `build.py` override-blind until this phase means a subprocess test writing into the real `datasets/processed/` in between. That also makes the prerequisite's "all nine entry points take overrides identically" true as stated rather than true one phase later. **An earlier draft of this bullet said the block "should be removed... it becomes dead code once the stage entry point exists," which presupposed a separate entry-point module** (a `features/materialize.py`); that reading was rejected, because the composition would still dep on `build.py` anyway, so the split buys a naming distinction and pays for it in churn plus two rewritten subprocess tests (`test_sql_features_postgres.py:282,324`, which under this decision are edited rather than replaced). What is retired is the *CSV-writing dev scaffold*, not the block. The core logic (`build_feature_df`, column constants) stays in `build.py` permanently. Also delete `datasets/processed/telco_churn_processed.csv` from the repo — it is superseded by the DVC-tracked `datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet`. **The entry point must reject a zero-row result before writing the Parquet output** — a broken SQL view should be a hard failure, not a silent empty artifact (eighth-pass QA item 4) — via an explicit `raise`, **not** `assert`, which `python -O` strips and which would silently restore the exact failure the guard exists to prevent.
- **Repoint the features accessor — one function, not four call sites.** Phase 6 introduced `features/accessor.py::load_features()` as the single owner of the processed artifact's path, format, and `sha256`. In Phase 8, change `pd.read_csv` → `pd.read_parquet` and the filename → `telco_churn_features.parquet` **inside that one function body**. The `train` stage in `dvc.yaml` declares the file as a dep, so DVC re-runs training whenever the features hash changes.
  > **⚠ The precondition this note previously asked someone to confirm does not hold — `models/train/common.py` is *half* migrated, and the half that isn't is the one that reads the data.** `calibrate.py:158`, `threshold.py:464`, `evaluate.py:191` and `error_analysis.py:512` all go through `load_features()`. `common.py` holds **two** paths to the same file: `_load_processed` (`:57-58`) builds `…/telco_churn_processed.csv` inline and `pd.read_csv`s it, while `_build_dev_dataset` (`:128`) resolves `features_path()` through the accessor — and that function's docstring asserts *"this can't go stale at Phase 8's CSV->parquet rename,"* which is true of that function and false of the module. The two agree today only because `${oc.env:PROCESSED_DATA_DIR,…}` resolves identically down both routes.
  >
  > **Flipping the accessor makes them disagree, and one of the two regimes is silent.** *(A) The old CSV is still on disk* — `_load_processed` reads the CSV while `_build_dev_dataset` stamps `source=…/telco_churn_features.parquet` onto the MLflow dataset, so every run in that window **trains on one file and records the other**, with no error and no warning. That corrupts precisely the artifact this phase exists to establish: `mlflow.log_input`'s digest is the human-facing half of the `data_content_hash` lineage story. `FeatureOutputSchema.validate(df)` (`:59`) does not catch it — both files satisfy the same schema. *(B) The CSV is deleted* — `FileNotFoundError`, loud and immediate. Regime A exists only in the window between editing `accessor.py` and deleting the CSV, which is why this is a **sequencing constraint, not a cleanup item**.
  >
  > **Fix — delete `_load_processed` rather than repointing it.** Its body *is* `load_features()`'s body (resolve canonical path → read → validate against `FeatureOutputSchema`); it is a duplicate written before the accessor existed. `_load_dev_features` calls the accessor directly instead. Keeping a one-line alias would preserve exactly the condition that caused the drift — a second name inside `models/train/` for "load the features file." Its `cfg` parameter goes with it: it was threaded through solely to build that path, and the tests already pass `cfg=None` (`test_train_common.py:220,235`). Blast radius is one `src/` call site (`train/__main__.py:28` — **which is the same two lines the prerequisite's `activate_config(cfg)` insertion lands on**: `compose_config` at `:27`, `_load_dev_features(cfg)` at `:28`. One drops the argument, the other adds a line between them, so make it one edit rather than two passes over the same block) and four spots in `test_train_common.py` — `:159`/`:173` call `_load_processed` directly (delete; `test_accessor.py:113-158` already covers the schema guard), `:217`/`:232` monkeypatch it (retarget to `common.load_features`). Note that `_dvc_hash` (`:80-105`) is this module's *third* inline path construction and is separately retired by the `data_content_hash` item above, for which `accessor.py::features_sha256()` is the natural source; after both changes `common.py` constructs **zero** paths, which is the state that makes the `_build_dev_dataset` docstring's claim actually true.
  >
  > **Two ordering constraints, both load-bearing.** (1) **The prerequisite PR must land first.** Delegating changes *how* the path resolves, not just where it points: `_load_processed(cfg)` uses the composed cfg with Hydra overrides applied, whereas `load_features()` resolves via `load_config()`, which is override-blind until `activate_config()` exists. Delegate first and `test_train_subprocess.py` silently stops redirecting and writes into the real `datasets/processed/`. (2) **The accessor edit, the `_load_processed` deletion, the Parquet write in `build.py`, and the deletion of the old CSV land in one atomic commit**, so regime A never exists.

- **Update the Phase 5 notebooks' processed-data reads:** `03a-model-selection.ipynb:1104`, `03b-feature-selection.ipynb:553`, and `03c-hyperparameter-tuning.ipynb:709` each independently build `processed_path = get_project_root() / cfg.paths.processed_data / "telco_churn_processed.csv"` and `pd.read_csv` it — the same scaffold as the `src/` loader, repeated three times. Replace all three with a `load_features()` call, so the format is owned in one place and the notebooks stop encoding the storage decision. Until this phase lands, none of these notebooks are reproducible from a fresh clone: the raw CSV is present (git-tracked), but `datasets/processed/` is gitignored and there is no DAG to rebuild it, so `telco_churn_processed.csv` only exists after manually running the Phase 4 feature pipeline (`02a`/`02b` or `features/build.py`). Wiring the `features` stage in this phase is what closes that gap — `dvc repro` regenerates it from the committed CSV.

- **Repoint `05-evaluation-and-error-analysis.ipynb`'s figure reads at MLflow — a live staleness bug, not a tidy-up.** Cell 72 sets `figures_dir = reports_dir / "figures"` and roughly twenty subsequent cells read PNGs from it by fixed path (`pr_curve_test.png`, `reliability_diagram_test.png`, `gains_lift_test.png`, `ev_by_budget.png`, `sensitivity_tornado.png`, the `shap_*` set). That is precisely the failure mode `CLAUDE.md` prohibits for `register.py` and `drift_reference.json`: a fixed local path reflects whichever run last executed on *this machine*, not the cycle under review — so the notebook can render SHAP plots from one training cycle beside a `metrics.json` from another, with nothing flagging the mismatch. Because this notebook's closing cell stamps `review: approved` onto `promotion_decision.json`, that lands on the promotion gate itself rather than on a rendering detail. `04-calibration-and-threshold.ipynb` already does it correctly — `mlflow.artifacts.download_artifacts(run_id=run_id, artifact_path="calibration/figures/reliability_diagram.png")`. Apply the same pattern in `05`, resolving `eval_run_id` / `error_analysis_run_id` from the model version rather than hardcoding a run.
- **No manual retraining flags (replaces the notebook's `RETRAIN_BEST` / `LOG_ARTIFACTS` booleans):** the hand-set flags that decided what to recompute do **not** migrate into `src/`. DVC's content-hashed DAG determines staleness — changing a dep reruns exactly the affected stages and nothing else. This is the engineering replacement for the manual flags (former Group B item).
- **Phase 2 cleanup — `clean_dataframe()` is already removed, and `validate_clean()` is deleted rather than repointed.** An earlier draft of this bullet scheduled the `clean_dataframe` removal (done ahead of this phase — it survives nowhere in `src/` or `tests/`) and instructed *"update `validate_clean()` to expect the features stage output directly."* **That instruction cannot be executed:** the features output preserves the 11 NaNs by design, so it can never satisfy `CleanedSchema`'s non-nullable `totalcharges`, and no other artifact in the DAG is that shape either. `validate_clean` is deleted as unreachable — after its one unique invariant is promoted to `RawSchema`, so a real check is not lost with it. See the `CleanedSchema` note in the stage-2 entry below for the full argument and the test-by-test consequences.
- **Phase 2 cleanup:** In the DVC `validate` stage entry point, catch `ValidationError`, emit a `pipeline_blocked` structured log event, and call `sys.exit(1)`.
- **Phase 2 cleanup — add `write_validation_receipt()` to `validate.py`, leaving `save_validation_report()` untouched.** The receipt writer runs unconditionally on every validation call (success included) and emits `reports/validation_receipt.json`: per-check name, pass/fail, `failure_severity`, `affected_rows` count, and the frame checksum — **no timestamp, no run id, no absolute paths**, so identical input yields identical bytes. `save_validation_report()` keeps its current behaviour exactly (failure-only, timestamped dirs, row-level CSVs) and becomes an untracked debugging side-channel: add `reports/validation/` to both `.dvcignore` and `.gitignore`. Declare the receipt `cache: false` in `dvc.yaml` so it lands in git and `git log --follow` becomes the failure-history record. Tests: receipt written on the all-pass path; byte-identical across two runs on identical input; `affected_rows` and severity round-trip for a failing gate.
- **SQL migration strategy — ✅ moved out of this phase to Phase 10; see the note below for why, and Phase 10's `prediction_outcomes` bullet for where it lands.** `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` is idempotent for creation but blind to changes — adding a column, renaming one, or tightening a constraint is silently skipped on re-run. That is a real defect; it is simply not one this phase's database has. Nothing here is blocked on it: `customers_raw` is a materialised cache of a static read-only CSV, so any schema change is applied by dropping and re-ingesting via `dvc repro --force`, which is already the documented recovery path.

- **Declare `params:`, not whole config directories — now reflected in the stage table above, which previously contradicted this bullet.** Earlier drafts had `train` dep on `configs/` entire and `calibrate` on `configs/calibration/`, so *any* config edit anywhere reran a 50-trial tuning study: change a threshold knob in `configs/threshold/default.yaml`, retrain the model. That also made this phase's Verification line (*"change a hyperparameter in `configs/training/lightgbm.yaml` → reruns `train` onward"*) vacuous — with a blanket dep it is equally true of every config edit, which is the failure the line exists to demonstrate against. DVC's `params:` field hashes **named keys inside YAML files** rather than the files themselves and accepts arbitrary paths, so each stage declares exactly the knobs it reads. Secondary payoff: declared params show up in `dvc params diff`, which pairs with the metrics diff below to make a Phase 11 PR comment saying what changed *and* what it did.
  - **Enforce the rule with a guard test, because the hand-maintained half is the half that rots.** `params:` is a *copy* of a fact that lives in the code — `dvc.yaml` says "watch `calibration.method`", `calibrate.py` says `cfg.calibration.method`, and nothing connects the two. Add to `tests/unit/test_architecture.py`: AST-collect every `cfg.<section>.<key>` chain in a stage's modules, parse `dvc.yaml`, assert the stage's `params:` covers them. Same technique as the guards that file already carries, applied to the one correspondence in this phase that no tool checks.
    - **The drift is invisible at the moment it is introduced, which is why discipline does not catch it.** Add a config read to a stage module without declaring it and *nothing goes wrong*: the edit changed the module, the module is a `dep`, so the stage reruns and the result is correct. The list is now wrong and asymptomatic. It bites later, when someone changes only that key's **value** — no code file moves, DVC sees nothing on its watch list move, skips the stage, and `dvc status` reports the pipeline current. The artifact was not built with the setting sitting in the config, and the tool you would consult to check says everything is fine. A test fails in the PR that adds the read, while the author still has the context; the alternative is someone else's debugging session months later with no breadcrumb pointing at a YAML list.
    - **Check both directions — declared-but-unread is the same defect inverted.** `configs/calibration/default.yaml`'s `gate_metric` is the live instance: referenced nowhere in `src/`, found only by reading the file by hand. Declared, it would buy spurious reruns; left as a live-looking key, it invites someone to declare it. Resolve it (wire into `pr_auc_gate_passes`, or demote to a comment) when this guard lands, since the guard is what would have found it. **⚠ The two directions are equally *motivated* but not equally *checkable*** — the AST limitation below is benign in one and produces false positives in the other, so read that bullet before implementing this one.
    - **The exemption list is the part worth writing down, since both categories look like ordinary config.** **Env-resolved keys** — `mlflow.tracking_uri`, `database.url` — resolve differently on a dev box than in CI and thrash `dvc.lock`; they may never be params. **Identity tokens** — `calibration.run_id` and its siblings — are fresh per cycle and would invalidate their stage every run. Note that `paths.processed_data` *leaves* the first set once the prerequisite makes it a plain literal, which is the whole point of that item.
      - **Third category, decided here rather than left open in stage 8's audit: `paths.*` is exempt.** Output-location keys change *where* an artifact is written, not *what it contains*, and `dvc.yaml` already states those locations literally in `outs:` — as params they duplicate a fact the DAG holds better elsewhere. **What settles it is that they are partly unhashable as params anyway:** `config.yaml:61-63` defines `feature_discovery_reports`, `validation_reports` and `figures` as `${paths.reports}/…`, and DVC parses YAML with its own reader, so it hashes the literal `${paths.reports}/figures` string — editing the root moves the real value while the declared param's hash never changes. `config.yaml:24-26` already documents exactly this hazard for a cross-file `random_state: ${random_seed}`; the same defect is live *inside* `config.yaml` and had gone unnoticed. **The exemption is scoped to report/figure output locations** — `paths.reports`, `paths.figures`, `paths.validation_reports`, `paths.feature_discovery_reports` — which are exactly the interpolated ones and exactly the ones restated literally in `outs:`/`metrics:`. **✅ Superseded, and in the direction of *more* exemption, not less: no `paths.*` key is ever a param — the whole prefix, not a scoped subset.** An earlier draft of this bullet exempted only the report/figure locations and had `paths.raw_data`/`processed_data`/`sql_features` declared at stages 1–4 with `paths.costs_config`/`model_promotion_config` beside them, on the reasoning that they *"point at files whose contents the stage declares, so repointing the key silently invalidates the declaration it sits beside."* That states the problem correctly and then reaches for the wrong instrument. **A params entry hashes the key's *value*, not the correspondence.** On a repoint it fires exactly once; afterwards `deps:` goes on hashing the abandoned path while the code reads the new one, so *silently wrong* becomes *visible once, then silently wrong* — with a declaration sitting there that looks like the case is handled. **And the obvious rebuttal fails on a mechanism worth stating, because it generalises:** *"the stage's declared out will catch it"* is untrue — **DVC checks that a declared out exists and hashes it; it never verifies the stage authored it.** The abandoned file is still on disk (git-tracked when `cache: false`, cache-restored when cached), so DVC finds it unchanged and reports the stage clean. That kills the rescue for every variant of this, including the write-side keys where it looks safest.
      - **The correspondence is a two-document invariant, so it is enforced by a guard that reads both documents — not by a param and not by a literal pin.** A pin (`assert cfg.paths.policy == "configs/policy"`) freezes one side of a symmetric invariant, says nothing about `dvc.yaml`, does not fail safe when a key is added, and forbids the *legitimate* coordinated repoint — so the fix becomes "edit the assertion", and an assertion people routinely edit to go green is not a guard. The rule instead lives in `test_architecture.py`, beside the params coverage guard that must parse `dvc.yaml` anyway: **every `paths.*` key must be bound to the document that restates its path.**
        - **DAG keys** (`raw_data`, `processed_data`, `sql_features`, `policy`, `costs_config`, `model_promotion_config`, `reports`) must **equal**, or be the **immediate parent of**, a path named anywhere in `dvc.yaml` — `deps:`, `outs:`, `metrics:`, `plots:`, **and `params:` file keys**, since `configs/costs.yaml` and `configs/model_promotion.yaml` appear only as whole-file params entries and a rule omitting those would fail two correct keys. *Immediate parent, not prefix:* under a prefix rule, repointing `processed_data` from `datasets/processed/` to `datasets/` still matches `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet` and passes while the code writes elsewhere — the silent case sailing straight through the check written to catch it.
        - **Mirror keys** (`figures`, `validation_reports`, `feature_discovery_reports`) name paths deliberately absent from `dvc.yaml`, so they are checked against the documents that *do* restate them: they must appear verbatim in both `.gitignore` and `.dvcignore`. **This is what stage 2's `paths.validation_reports` note was reaching for** — it declares that key as a param because moving it *"silently stops the ignore entries matching and timestamped evidence dirs begin landing in git"*, which is the same correspondence problem against a different second document, and a param rots there for the identical reason. So the exclusion list stops being a hole: it is a **classification**, both branches are checked, and a key belonging to neither document fails both.
        - **It fails safe on addition** — a new `paths.*` key defaults to checked and fails until classified — and it permits the coordinated repoint, which is the actual requirement. **It also makes the `paths.processed_data` literal prerequisite *more* necessary, not less:** an `${oc.env:…}` value can no more be compared against a `dvc.yaml` literal than it can be hashed, so that item survives intact and only its conclusion (*"therefore declare it as a param"*) changes.
        - **Honest cost/benefit, since the threat model is thin.** Nobody is likely to repoint `paths.policy` in a project with a static dataset. The value is that it collapses three mutually contradictory rules into one, deletes ten params entries that provide **false assurance**, and makes *"no `paths.*` in `params:`"* enforceable rather than aspirational — at roughly twenty lines inside a `dvc.yaml` parse that has to exist regardless. Standalone, it would not be worth building. **Consequence for stage 8's discrepancy 4, restated under the superseding rule:** all four keys it names — `paths.costs_config`, `paths.figures`, `paths.reports`, and the stage 5 `paths.reports` phantom — resolve the same way now, by removal. That discrepancy's conclusion that `paths.costs_config` was *"a genuine missing declaration"* is the one part of the audit that does not survive: it was a genuine missing **correspondence**, and the guard is what closes it.
      - **Fourth category: `mlflow.experiment_name` is exempt everywhere — and stage 5, which declares it today, is the row that changes.** The key is read by four modules through `ensure_experiment_metadata` (`utils/mlflow.py:239-247`), which sets the tracking URI, calls `set_experiment`, and writes the experiment description and tags. Three stages create runs under it (`train`, `evaluate`, `error_analysis`); `calibrate` calls it inertly; `register` calls it too but is not a DVC stage. **It is declared at exactly one of them.** Whichever way this resolves, three rows are currently wrong — and it is **L1-class**, since the read sits inside a `utils/` helper that appears in no stage's `deps:`, so the coverage guard cannot see it. Found by hand, which is the pattern the ordering note above predicts.
        - **It is a location key: changing it changes where the record lands, never what a stage produces.** Four couplings checked, all absent. **(1)** Nothing reads the experiment tags back. **(2) There is no `search_runs` call anywhere in `src/`** — cross-stage resolution is by explicit `run_id` (receipts, tags) or by registry, both experiment-independent, so a rename cannot change what any stage *finds*. **(3)** The comparative regime's champion lookup is `search_model_versions(f"name='…'")` (`register.py:337`) — the **registry**, which is orthogonal to experiments. **(4)** Optuna resumption was the one plausible content coupling and it is ruled out: `_build_optuna_storage` is `RDBStorage` keyed by `_study_name` = data hash + config digest, not MLflow, so a rename cannot orphan a study or silently restart tuning.
        - **✅ Verified empirically on this project's MLflow 3.14, rather than assumed.** The scenario that would break the claim is a rename landing between partial reruns: `evaluate` creates its run in the new experiment while logging metrics against a `LoggedModel` in the old one. Probed directly — the metric attaches cleanly, no error, and the model keeps its original `experiment_id`. **The linkage design in `CLAUDE.md` is what makes this true:** metrics attach to the *(model, dataset)* pair precisely so a separate evaluation run stays attributable, and that choice makes attribution independent of which experiment the run lives in. The exemption is not tolerance of a small risk — it follows from a decision already made and now measured. A ragged rename splits the **UI** record and nothing else.
        - **Declaring it would not merely buy nothing — it would forge a signal.** A rename would invalidate `train` (the Optuna stage, the DAG's most expensive), `evaluate` and `error_analysis`; those reruns mint **new run ids**, which change the receipts, which are identity tokens — so a cosmetic rename cascades through the whole lower DAG and produces a diff indistinguishable from a genuine model change. **And the thing declaring appears to promise is unachievable anyway: it gives a rerun, not a migration.** `set_experiment` under a new name creates a new experiment and leaves every historical run in the old one; MLflow does not move them. So declaring forces a fresh cycle into the new name — which is exactly what `dvc repro --force` does explicitly, and better for being chosen.
        - **No guard, and no correspondence to guard — this is where it parts company with `paths.*`.** `paths.*` needs the two-document check because `dvc.yaml` independently restates those paths in `deps:`/`outs:` and the two can diverge silently. `mlflow.experiment_name` is stated **once**, in `config.yaml:46`; `dvc.yaml` names no MLflow experiment and nothing else restates it. **So this exemption is complete rather than a trade** — `paths.*` cost ~20 lines of guard, this costs one entry in the coverage guard's existing exemption list. **⚠ Do not add a bespoke assertion that the key never appears in a `params:` block.** That is a negative pin on one named key: it guards a single instance of a general rule, is silent on the next one, and is the shape the correspondence bullet above rejects. The gap it would close is uniform across all four categories — nothing stops you declaring `calibration.run_id` either — so a special check here would be inconsistent, not thorough.
        - **`calibrate` is independent evidence that "declare wherever read" is the wrong criterion.** The stage-6 note already establishes the key is *inert* there — `start_run(run_id=…)` resumes, and a resumed run's experiment is fixed by the run, not the active setting. Declare-where-read would put it in stage 6's `params:`, where it provably cannot affect anything. The criterion is the one already written into the exemption rule — *would changing this change what the stage produces?* — not *does this stage read it?*
      - **Keep a guard rule regardless, because the exemption covers the instances and not the class.** Fail any declared param whose composed value contains `${` unless its interpolation root is also declared. One check; it generalises the `oc.env` prerequisite from a single wrapper to the whole interpolation class, and it is what stops a future non-`paths` key from reintroducing the defect silently — same failure mode as the phantom, and equally asymptomatic.
    - **Stated limitations, corrected against `src/` — the original list named two patterns that do not occur and omitted four that do.** `cfg["a"]["b"]` and `getattr(cfg, name)`: **zero instances anywhere under `src/`**; both were hypothetical. The live blind spots, in descending order of consequence, are **(L1)** config files loaded by path rather than through `cfg`, **(L2)** `.get("key", default)` reads, **(L3)** whole-subtree reads via `OmegaConf.to_container`, and **(L4)** alias binding, which occurs in three forms of which the fix below covers one. Each has its own sub-bullet. The existing guards in `test_architecture.py` share the general limitation and have been worth having; what makes correcting the list necessary is that **L1 is not a coverage gap but a structural blind spot** — and it happens to contain the highest-consequence missing edge in the table.
      - **Consequence: this is two tests, not one, and the cheap one ships first.** They have different soundness properties and almost disjoint machinery. **(1) `test_loaded_configs_are_declared`** — file-granular: collect `OmegaConf.load(<path>)` sites across a stage's modules, resolve the argument to a literal, assert the file appears in that stage's `params:` or `deps:`. No key resolution, no alias tracking, no block expansion. Hard failure from day one. **(2) `test_params_match_reads`** — key-granular, the pass described in this bullet; `read ⊆ declared` may be a hard failure immediately, since under-collection is benign in that direction, while `declared ⊆ read` stays advisory until module collection, L2, L3 and L4 are all in. **The original single-test framing had the priority backwards:** the key-granular check is the harder half *and* the weaker one, and it is the half that cannot see the defect that motivates the guard most.
      - **⚠ That limitation is *asymmetric* across the two directions above, and treating it as symmetric makes the declared-but-unread half unsound.** In the **read ⊆ declared** direction it is benign exactly as stated: a read the guard cannot see is a read it does not check, so the guard buys less coverage but never returns a wrong answer. In the **declared ⊆ read** direction the same blind spot inverts into a **false positive** — an alias-hidden read is indistinguishable from no read at all, so absence of evidence is scored as evidence of absence. The defect the two directions chase is symmetric; the guard's blind spot is not.
      - **The live instance is stage 5, and it would fail on a correct declaration.** `models/train/` binds the block before reading it — `cc = cfg.training_setup` then `cc.cv_folds` / `cc.cv_repeats` (`candidates.py:79`, and the same pattern at `common.py:274`, `comparison.py:317`, `feature_audit.py:53`). A guard that expands a declared block into its keys and demands each be read sees `cfg.training_setup` with no key attached, sees nothing else, and flags stage 5's `training_setup` as over-broad. It is not: stage 5 genuinely reads `cv_folds`, `cv_repeats`, `class_weight` and more. The likely response to that failure is fragmenting a block declaration that should stay whole — the guard causing the defect it exists to prevent.
      - **⚠ The *second* stated limitation — "a key read inside a helper in a module the test has not mapped to that stage" — is the same failure and it fires first, on the same row.** `models/train/__main__.py` reads **no config keys at all**: it composes `cfg` (`:27`) and passes it to six helpers (`:36-42`), and all 73 `cfg.` reads live in `candidates.py`, `common.py`, `comparison.py`, `feature_audit.py`, `log_model.py`, `tuning.py`. A pass over the entry point alone therefore collects an **empty** read set for stage 5, which inverts the guard completely: `declared ⊆ read` flags `random_seed`, `training_setup` and all four training config files as phantoms, while `read ⊆ declared` passes vacuously. **The two limitations compound in a fixed order** — stage 5 trips both, and the alias fix below is unreachable code until module collection is fixed first, since the four aliasing sites are in modules the pass never opens.
      - **Collect the read set over the modules a stage's `deps:` names, not over its entry point — and note this is only *sufficient* after the extraction lands.** It resolves stage 5 directly, whose dep is the directory `models/train/`. It does **not** resolve stage 8, whose deps name `models/evaluate.py` and deliberately exclude `models/calibrate.py` (that edge travels through the receipt), leaving the two `ece_n_bins` readers invisible — the live instance recorded in stage 8's audit note. Walking the **import closure** instead would find them, but would also walk calibrate.py's registration block, whose five `mlflow.registered_model_name` reads (`:1221`–`:1349`) stage 8 never executes — a false positive of exactly the kind this bullet exists to prevent. Neither traversal is correct while cfg-reading helpers sit inside `__main__`-bearing modules. **They converge once PR C lands:** `calibration_metrics.py` is a pure module with no registration code, so it becomes a legitimate stage 8 dep and deps-walk returns the same answer as import-closure. The ordering already holds — PR C is a prerequisite and this guard cannot exist before `dvc.yaml` does — so deps-walk is the right implementation at the moment it is written. **This is a third independent argument for the extraction: it is what makes the guard implementable, not merely more accurate.**
      - **Fix: resolve single-level aliases in the AST pass, and gate the strict check on having done so.** Track local assignments whose value is a `cfg.<name>` chain, bind the target name to that section, and rewrite later attribute reads on it (`cc = cfg.training_setup` ⇒ `cc.cv_folds` resolves to `training_setup.cv_folds`). It is a small addition to a pass that already walks the tree, and it covers all four live instances — **but only once the pass reaches those modules at all**, per the bullet above. **Only with aliases resolved may `declared ⊆ read` be a hard failure**; without it that direction must be advisory or exemption-listed, and this bullet is where the choice is recorded rather than left for an implementer to discover by tripping it.
      - **A block declaration must also be expanded before it is compared, or the check passes the defect it targets.** `params: [training_setup]` covers `training_setup.fixed_recall_thresholds`, so a naive prefix match scores a 1-of-10-keys-read declaration as fully justified — which is precisely stage 8's over-declaration (see its params note). Expansion and alias resolution are therefore one change, not two: expansion without aliasing produces the stage 5 false positive above, and aliasing without expansion leaves the stage 8 false negative in place. **Three pieces in total, in this order — module collection, alias resolution, block expansion** — and shipping any prefix of that list leaves the `declared ⊆ read` direction unsound rather than merely incomplete, which is why it stays advisory until all three are in.
      - **L1 — three config files are read by path, never through `cfg`, and the key-granular pass sees none of them.** Five `OmegaConf.load` sites exist in `src/`; three load files the DAG declares — `utils/mlflow.py:205` (`load_model_promotion_bars` → `configs/model_promotion.yaml`, declared by stages 7 and 8), `threshold.py:123` (`load_costs_config` → `configs/costs.yaml`, stages 7 and 8), and `threshold.py:423` (`load_policy_thresholds` → `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, stages 8 and 9). In the `declared ⊆ read` direction the pass flags all three as phantoms across four stages; in the `read ⊆ declared` direction it offers **zero** coverage on them. **The second half is the damning one:** `configs/model_promotion.yaml` sitting in no stage's deps — called out below as the highest-consequence missing edge in the table — *is* this class, so the guard as originally specified would not have caught the defect that most motivates it. Test (1) above is the answer and needs none of the key machinery. Declare these three whole, check them whole, and exempt them from key-level phantom detection.
        - **It compounds with module mapping, and that part is a code move, not a guard feature.** `load_model_promotion_bars` lives in `utils/mlflow.py:184` — a module named in **no stage's `deps:` anywhere in the table**, stage 6's included, which lists `utils/stats.py` file-by-file. The gate-bars parser can therefore change and invalidate nothing. The module is mixed surface: tracking-URI resolution and experiment metadata are plumbing that cannot change artifact content, while the bars parser decides promotions. **Moving it to `models/policy_config.py` is recorded in the PR C prerequisite** — same defect the extraction exists to fix, one module further out, and stages 7 and 8 already dep on that module so the edge closes with no new dep. It lands beside `load_costs_config` and `load_policy_thresholds`, which puts **all three of this bullet's `OmegaConf.load` sites in one file** — so the file-granular test above scans one module rather than every stage's module set.
      - **L2 — `.get("key", default)`, live and *exclusive*.** `tuning.py:341` and `:343` read `timeout_seconds` and `min_completed_trials` this way and **nowhere else**, so an attribute-chain-only pass sees neither. Under key-expanded `declared ⊆ read` against `configs/tuning/optuna.yaml` both flag as phantoms on stage 5 — a third false positive on that row, after the alias and module-mapping ones above. **Fix:** handle `Call` nodes whose func is `Attribute(attr="get")` on a resolved cfg chain with a literal first argument. A few lines, and it belongs in v1 rather than a follow-up.
      - **L3 — whole-subtree reads.** `tuning.py:312` calls `OmegaConf.to_container(tuning_cfg.search_space, resolve=True)`, reading every key beneath `search_space`; `threshold.py:140` has the same shape. **Fix: represent the read set as prefixes, not leaves** — a read of `tuning.search_space` covers all its descendants. This is the exact mirror of block expansion on the declaration side, so both directions collapse into one prefix-covering comparison instead of two special cases pointing opposite ways.
      - **L4 — alias binding has three forms; covering one is the right stopping point, but only with an escape hatch.** `tuning.py` uses all three for the same name: **local assign** (`tuning_cfg = cfg.tuning`, `:308`, `:339`) ✅ covered by the fix above; **function parameter** (`:156`, called at `:424`, reads at `:172`, `:173`, `:200`, `:211`, `:219`) ✗; **dict round-trip** (stored `:387`, retrieved `tuning_cfg = setup["tuning_cfg"]` at `:414`, read at `:429`) ✗. Extend to single-call-site parameter binding — match the callee's `def` in the same module, bind the parameter name to the argument's resolved chain — and **declare the dict round-trip out of scope**, with a per-stage allowlist of keys the guard is told to treat as read, one line each and visible in review. Chasing config through a container is dataflow analysis, which is how a unit test becomes a static analyser. **The hatch is required rather than optional:** no key today is read *exclusively* through the two hard forms — `n_trials`, `search_space` and `es_validation_size` each also have a local-alias read — but that is luck, not design, and the hatch is what stops the guard failing a correct declaration the day it runs out.
  > **⚠ The narrowing cannot be derived from Hydra — composition in this project is global, not per-stage.** There is exactly one composition entry point, `utils/paths.py:51`'s `compose(config_name="config")`; no module declares its own `config_name`. Every stage therefore composes the *identical* object, `train` included — `cfg.threshold`, `cfg.evaluate`, and `cfg.register` are all loaded whether or not it reads them. So "enumerate the group files a stage composes" yields **the same nine files for every stage** and reproduces the blanket-dep problem in longer form. The narrowing must come from a different question — *which keys does this module actually read?* — which is a claim about the code, not about Hydra, and cannot be derived mechanically. **Corollary:** a per-stage `params:` list is deliberately narrower than what Hydra loaded, so it goes stale silently the moment a module starts reading a new key. Treat it as reviewable code, and confirm each stage's key list by reading the module at implementation time rather than trusting the table above.
  >
  > **Two genuinely missing edges, neither of which any `configs/`-shaped dep would have caught.** (1) **`configs/model_promotion.yaml` is currently in no stage's deps at all.** It holds the pre-registered gate bars (`pr_auc_bar`, `recall_bar`, `calibration_slope_band`) and is read by `evaluate.py:1397` and `threshold.py:809`, but it is loaded via `OmegaConf.load` **outside** Hydra's defaults list — deliberately, so a shipping bar cannot be moved by a CLI override — so composition never touches it. Consequence today: **raising a promotion bar does not invalidate `evaluate`**, and `dvc repro` leaves a `promotion_decision.json` computed against the superseded gate. Given that this file is what §0 pre-registers, it is the highest-consequence missing edge in the table. (2) **`configs/costs.yaml` must be an explicit `evaluate` param** — `evaluate.py:1305` reads it directly. It is presently covered only transitively (a costs edit reruns `threshold`, which rewrites `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, which `evaluate` deps on); that chain happens to work because the policy file embeds `costs_config_hash`, but resting an invalidation edge on an incidental field of a generated file is not a design. Declare what you read.
  >
  > **Three mechanics to get right when writing the file.** (1) **An empty-value entry (`configs/training/lightgbm.yaml:`) means "every key in this file"** — invalidation-identical to a plain file dep; the gain is `dvc params diff` visibility, not narrowing. Fine for leaf group files; do not mistake it for the fix. (2) **A path cannot be both `deps:` and `params:`.** Once `configs/config.yaml` moves to `params:` with a named key list, everything unnamed in it is untracked for that stage — including `paths`, which is how `dvc.yaml`'s hardcoded dep paths could silently desync from `cfg.paths`. (3) **DVC parses params YAML raw, with no OmegaConf resolution.** `${oc.env:POSTGRES_URL,postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/telco_churn}` is hashed as that literal template. Usefully, this keeps `dvc.lock` machine-independent; but it also means declaring the `database` or `mlflow` blocks writes a literal `user:pass` string into the lockfile, which `detect-secrets` (`.pre-commit-config.yaml:83-87`) will flag and which would need a `.secrets.baseline` entry — for zero invalidation benefit, since the template itself never changes. **Keep both blocks out of `params:`** and state the limitation instead: an env-var-driven change of database or tracking server is invisible to the DAG by construction (the same property Phase 11's `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` note relies on).

- **Declare `metrics:` and `plots:` — without them `dvc metrics diff` has nothing to read, and that command is this phase's replacement for the lockfile CI gate.** In `dvc.yaml` these are kinds of out, not additions to `outs:`, so the entries move rather than duplicate. Recommended shape: emit a small flat `reports/metrics_summary.json` — **exactly what `gate.py::decide_promotion` reads, in both regimes, plus identity and `test_ev_base`** (enumerated in the note below) — and declare *that* as the `metrics:` file, keeping the full nested `metrics.json` as an ordinary out. `dvc metrics diff` renders nested structures poorly, and a PR reviewer wants the five numbers the gate turns on, not the sliced-fairness tree. Declare `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` as metrics too — `t*` moving is exactly what a reviewer needs to see, DVC's `metrics:` accepts YAML, and since `metrics:` is itself a kind of out this replaces rather than duplicates that stage's `outs:` entry (keep the `cache: false`). Whatever goes under `metrics:` must be written **in the same function and from the same payload dict** that assembles `metrics.json` (`evaluate.py:1447`), never recomputed — a second file carrying the same numbers drifts from the first otherwise, the same discipline the persist-the-evidence rule applies elsewhere — and must carry `model_version`, so the numbers in a PR comment attach to an artifact rather than floating free.
  > **📌 Which metrics — the selection rule is "exactly the gate's inputs", not a curated shortlist, and "the four gate criteria" is the wrong description.** That phrase belongs to the **four model-version tags** `CLAUDE.md` specifies (`test_pr_auc`, `test_recall`, `test_brier`, `test_calibration_slope`), which exist for rollback legibility — a human scanning a version list under pressure. The gate reads a *different* set, and it differs by regime. **Cold start** (`gate.py:118-126`): `pr_auc` ≥ `pr_auc_bar`; `recall` ≥ `recall_bar` at the shipped `t*`; **`bss` > 0 — not Brier** (monotone in Brier given the fixed prior, but BSS is what is read); and the calibration-slope **CI** against `[0.80, 1.25]` via `slope_passes(ci_lower, ci_upper, band)`. **Comparative** (`GateInputs:94-102`): three paired-bootstrap deltas each with a CI — `pr_auc_delta`, `brier_delta`, `recall_delta` — plus the same slope CI check. Brier and BSS are therefore both required and are not redundant: each is read in exactly one regime.
  >
  > | Key | Source in `metrics.json` |
  > |---|---|
  > | `model_version`, `champion_version`, `regime` | top level |
  > | `test_pr_auc` (+ `_ci_lower` / `_ci_upper`) | `ranking.pr_auc*` |
  > | `test_recall` | `classification[base].recall` |
  > | `test_brier` | `calibration.brier` |
  > | `test_bss` | `calibration.bss` |
  > | `test_calibration_slope` (+ `_ci_lower` / `_ci_upper`) | `calibration.calibration_slope.*` |
  > | `test_ev_base` | `business_impact.scenarios.base.ev` |
  > | `test_{pr_auc,brier,recall}_delta_obs` (+ `_ci_lower` / `_ci_upper`) | comparative regime only |
  >
  > About twenty keys, under 1 KB — still nowhere near the `sliced` tree that makes `dvc metrics show` unreadable on the full file. **The CI bounds are mandatory, not decoration:** omit them and the summary cannot reproduce the slope verdict, the one criterion evaluated as an interval rather than a point. **`t*` is deliberately absent** — it lives in `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, already declared as the `threshold` stage's `metrics:`, so `dvc metrics diff` picks it up from there; duplicating it here would manufacture exactly the drift the same-payload-dict rule above exists to prevent.
  >
  > **`cache: false` on the metrics file is load-bearing, not a tidiness preference.** `dvc metrics diff main` has to materialise `main`'s copy of the file. For a **cached** out it resolves the hash in `main`'s `dvc.lock` and looks it up in the local cache or a remote — and **there is no DVC remote before Phase 12**, while CI runs on a fresh clone with an empty cache, so the lookup finds nothing and the diff comes back empty. With `cache: false` the file is an ordinary git blob at that revision and the comparison is pure git. Without it the headline use case does not run on the one machine it exists for.
  >
  > **And the diff requires no pipeline execution in CI — worth stating, because it looks like it should conflict with Phase 11's deliberately constrained reproducibility job.** It does not: the developer runs `dvc repro` locally and commits `dvc.lock` alongside the metrics file; CI renders the delta between two committed blobs. `dvc metrics diff main --md` emits a ready-to-post markdown table. That is precisely what makes this a sound replacement for the lockfile gate — it costs nothing to run and reports a real quantity, where the lockfile check is expensive and reports a tautology.
  >
  > **`cache: false` does not generalise to every JSON out — apply it by size, and the sizes here are not uniform.** `cache: false` means git stores the bytes, so an out that changes every training cycle is committed every training cycle. `promotion_decision.json` (1.7 KB), `dev_oof_diagnostics.json` (8.4 KB), `metrics_summary.json` (<1 KB) and `metrics.json` (42 KB) are fine and want the diffability. **`economics.json` (513 KB) and `error_analysis.json` (619 KB) must stay cached** — git-tracking half a megabyte of churn per cycle is real history bloat for no diff value. The parquet outs stay cached regardless: binary, and nothing diffs them. Note for consistency with the receipt-determinism note above — `cache: false` changes *where the bytes live*, not whether they are fingerprinted into `dvc.lock`.
  >
  > **📌 Why `plots:` at all, given MLflow already owns every figure — and the answer is "barely, and only for one series."** The two tools compare along different axes: **MLflow compares *runs*, DVC compares *git revisions*.** In a PR the question is "what does this branch do to the model?", which is a revision question — MLflow can answer it, but only if the reviewer leaves GitHub, opens the MLflow UI, and works out which run corresponds to which branch. That gap is what `plots:` closes, and it is genuinely narrow.
  >
  > **The rule that keeps it narrow: a curve earns a `plots:` entry only when no scalar already in the metrics table answers its question.** Applied here, most curves fail it. The **PR curve** is summarised by PR-AUC, which is the selection metric and already a metric entry. The **reliability diagram** is summarised by the calibration slope, which is a gate criterion and already a metric entry. Only the **decile lift/gains table** survives: no scalar says *where* in the ranking a change happened, and the rows already exist as structured data (`evaluate.py:1264`, a top-level `decile_lift` key in `metrics.json`), so declaring it is a small CSV write rather than a new computation. Declare that one; skip the rest.
  >
  > **The `plots:` entry is decision-inert, and that separation is required rather than incidental.** Nothing reads `decile_lift.csv` — not `gate.py`, not `register.py`, not `metrics_summary.json`. It exists solely so a PR reviewer can see *where* in the ranking a change landed. This is the same line §0's invariants already draw: lift is named there among the diagnostics that are "reported, never binding," so a series that gated anything would breach the one-metric-drives-selection rule. Three tiers, and they do not overlap: **`metrics:`** carries the gate's inputs and is decision-bearing; **`plots:`** carries review context and can never gate; **MLflow** owns everything else, as leaves. A future change that starts reading a plot file to make a decision is a policy change, not a refactor.
  >
  > **Do not declare the PNGs themselves.** DVC image plots can only be rendered side by side — no overlay, no delta — so two pictures in a PR comment is barely more than one, whereas plotting the *data* lets DVC render Vega-Lite and draw the candidate's curve on top of `main`'s in a single chart. Declaring `reports/figures/` would also contradict the figures decision above, since four stages write into that one directory and a DVC directory out is exclusively owned. One format constraint to design around: **DVC plots accepts JSON/YAML/CSV/TSV and images — not parquet**, so the decile-lift series ships as a small CSV, unlike the prediction artifacts.
  >
  > **⚠ Both files are declared and *neither is written by anything today* — this is the one part of the metrics/plots design that is new code, and it is blocking rather than cosmetic.** No `metrics_summary` writer exists anywhere in `src/`, and the decile rows only ever land nested in `metrics_payload` (`evaluate.py:1460`); nothing emits a CSV. **`dvc repro` errors outright when a declared `metrics:`/`plots:` target is absent after the stage runs**, so the stage 8 row is unexecutable until both writers land. They are **co-requisites of that row, not prerequisites of the phase** — same PR, since neither is a change to existing behaviour that the row's *content* depends on. (Contrast the prerequisites, each of which makes a row that would otherwise be *wrong* correct; these make a correct row *runnable*. The plan already treats new-code-in-a-wrap-phase this way — `register.py`'s mint-time `promotion_status: pending` is flagged *"new code, not moved code"* and sits in the deliverables, not the prerequisites.)
  >
  > **The rule that keeps this a wrap rather than scope creep: new *serialization* of already-computed values, never new computation.** Both writers qualify and must be built so that they visibly do — which is the same discipline as the same-payload-dict rule above, stated as a boundary rather than a caution. (1) **`flatten_metrics_summary(metrics_payload, regime) -> dict[str, float | str]`**, a pure function beside `_assemble_payloads` (`evaluate.py:1447`), taking the very dict that becomes `metrics.json`; the key table above is its specification, including the regime branch that adds the three comparative deltas. (2) **The decile CSV written from `core_metrics["decile_rows"]`** — the same list already placed at `metrics.json`'s `decile_lift` key — *not* re-derived by calling `sealed_test_decile_lift` a second time. Pure functions, I/O at the caller, paths anchored via `get_project_root()`. Around twenty lines between them. **The test for any future addition here is whether it could be computed from `metrics.json` alone**; if it could not, it is analysis and belongs upstream, not in a DVC-facing projection.

- **`dvc repro` from a fresh clone requires a live Postgres — the "reproducible end-to-end" claim is false without it, and Phase 11 must provide one.** The `features` stage runs `sql/features/` against the database, and `ingest` writes to it. Both are the DAG's entry points, so a clean checkout plus `dvc repro` fails at stage one on any machine without the `infra` profile up. This is a precondition to state, not a defect to fix — the SQL feature work is deliberate. Decide it here rather than at CI-writing time: Phase 11's workflow needs a `services: postgres:16` block, `POSTGRES_URL` pointed at it, and the DAG run starting from `ingest` rather than from a cached mid-pipeline state. Note the contrast with MLflow and Optuna, both of which already have zero-infra SQLite fallbacks; Postgres has none and cannot, because the feature SQL is the point.
  > **State the precondition positively — Postgres is the *only* infrastructure `dvc repro` needs, and naming the minimal command is worth more than naming the constraint.** MLflow resolves to `sqlite:///mlflow.db` when `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` is unset and Optuna to a local study, so neither requires a server. The minimal invocation is therefore **`docker compose up postgres -d`** — the same one Phase 1's Verification line already uses — not `docker compose up`, which by Phase 14 also starts the API and Streamlit, neither of which the DAG touches. The `infra` profile adds the MLflow UI: convenience, not requirement. End-to-End Verification §1 has been narrowed to the same command.
  >
  > **✅ The CI decision, stated in one place — three jobs, three triggers, and only one of them needs a database.** It is a split rather than a single answer because "does CI run `dvc repro`?" resolves differently depending on what is being asked:
  >
  > | Job | Trigger | Postgres? | Runs |
  > |---|---|---|---|
  > | **Lint / type / test** | every push & PR | **No** — the suites spin their own ephemeral instance via `testcontainers`; adding a service container here would be redundant | `ruff`, `mypy`, `pytest` |
  > | **Metrics diff** | every PR | **No** | `dvc metrics diff main --md` → posted as a PR comment. Needs no pipeline execution: a git-to-git comparison of two committed `cache: false` files. Seconds |
  > | **Cold-DAG reproducibility** | scheduled + `workflow_dispatch` (**not** per-PR) | **Yes** — `services: postgres:16` | `dvc repro` from `ingest`, on a clean checkout |
  >
  > **Why the third is not per-PR:** a cold DAG includes `train`, a 50-trial Optuna study. That is the runtime the Phase 11 note bounds by restricting *when* the job runs rather than *what* it does. **MLflow gets no server in any job** — the `sqlite:///mlflow.db` fallback is the point, and `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` must be explicitly withheld so a CI run can never write into the production tracking server. Phase 11's `data-quality.yml` is a fourth, separate workflow that also needs Postgres but runs only `ingest` + `validate`, so it stays cheap enough to schedule daily.
  >
  > **One implementation detail for whoever writes the workflow:** the repo's default is `POSTGRES_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/telco_churn`, so either the service block's `POSTGRES_USER`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD`/`POSTGRES_DB` must be set to match it, or `POSTGRES_URL` must be set explicitly in the job env. **Prefer the explicit env var** — it is visible in the workflow file rather than resting on a coincidence between two sets of defaults that nothing checks.

- **Give Optuna resumption an escape hatch — `tuning.resume`, whose default is settled by the resolution note below (complete studies always reused; `resume` governs *partial* ones only, and defaults to `false`).** The `_dvc_hash` note above fixes the specific bug where a fixed `"unknown"` data hash let `load_if_exists=True` resume a study built on different data. Resumption itself stays correct and wanted, for crash recovery mid-optimization. But once the study name is content-addressed, the only way to force a genuinely fresh search is to change an input, which is exactly what you do *not* want to do when the reason you want a fresh search is that you suspect the study state itself. Add `tuning.resume=false` → `load_if_exists=False`, so a name collision raises loudly instead of silently continuing. One config knob, no new mechanism.
  > **The stronger justification is verification, not suspicion — and it is the one that belongs in the code comment.** The sampler is seeded (`sampler_seed`), the CV is seeded, and LightGBM's `n_jobs` is pinned to 1 specifically for determinism, so a fresh search *should* land on the same 50 trials as a resumed one. **That claim is untestable without a way to force a fresh study.** `tuning.resume=false` is therefore the instrument that makes the reproducibility of the search checkable, which is a better reason to ship it than "in case you distrust the study state" — both are true, only the first earns it a place.
  >
  > **Reuse of a *complete* study delivers the stronger of the two things "reproducible" can mean, and that is the part to keep.** *Same output*: reuse gives it exactly — the same 50 trials, the same 1-SE selection, the same params. *Re-derive from scratch*: that is what reuse forgoes. **Do not, however, justify reuse as the memoisation that makes tuning affordable in the DAG — that argument is wrong.** DVC already skips the whole `train` stage when its deps are unchanged, so the study is only ever consulted once DVC has *decided* to re-run. Resumption's real value is narrower: crash recovery, and `--force` speed. The one remaining case where it acts as a cache — a non-data dep changes, DVC re-runs, and the study key is unchanged so zero trials are drawn — is the code-version hole named below, which is a defect rather than a benefit. See the resolution note further down for how the default should actually be split (complete studies always reused; partial ones re-searched unless explicitly resumed).
  >
  > **📌 The impurity is the *storage*, not `load_if_exists` — and one half of it is already fixed in shipped code.** The `n_trials` semantics question ("add N more" vs. "reach N total") is settled: `tuning.py:429` computes `n_remaining_trials = max(n_trials - len(study.trials), 0)`, so a forced rerun on unchanged deps runs **zero** trials and reuses all 50. There is no doubling risk. What remains is that `_build_optuna_storage()` (`tuning.py:258-294`) puts the study in **Postgres (`optuna` schema)** when `POSTGRES_URL` is set and `sqlite:///optuna.db` otherwise — **external mutable state that DVC neither hashes as a dep nor owns as an out**. Content-addressing `_study_name()` keys that store correctly (you never resume across data versions) but keying is not visibility: `train`'s output remains a function of its declared deps *plus whatever the store already holds*. **Do not try to bring the store itself into the DAG** — it cannot be a DVC out on the Postgres path (outs must be files, the same constraint that forced the receipt pattern), and making it one on the SQLite path alone would make reproducibility semantics differ between the two backends, the exact divergence the MLflow fallback rule exists to prevent. Its status is Postgres's own: a materialised cache, never a source of truth, rebuilt by `--force`. **Caveat to state:** `docker compose down -v` destroys the study, so a `--force` after a volume wipe is a materially different run from a `--force` after a restart, and nothing in the DAG can tell them apart. **The store staying external is not the same as the impurity being unresolved** — the resolution note below shows it reduces to key completeness plus the crash-recovery path, both of which *are* addressable without moving the store.
  >
  > **Log how much was reused — the one piece of code work here, and what makes the assertions below possible.** `tuning_summary` carries `n_trials_requested` and `n_completed_trials` (`tuning.py:709-710`) but nothing separating trials run *now* from trials inherited. A manifest reading "50 completed" is identical whether the search ran in full, was reused ten times, or crashed at trial 3 and resumed. Add **`n_trials_reused`** and **`n_trials_run_this_invocation`**.
  >
  > **Two checks, at two different cadences.** (1) **In this phase's Verification line:** after `dvc repro --force` on unchanged deps, assert `n_trials_run_this_invocation == 0` and that the selected trial number is unchanged — this converts "the study was reused" from an invisible fact into a checked one. (2) **Once, and not in CI:** run with `tuning.resume=false` and confirm the fresh search selects the same trial; record the outcome in `ANALYSIS.md` §4c, which is being rewritten for this phase anyway. Fifty trials is a claim you establish once and cite, not a recurring gate.
  >
  > **✅ The impurity is solvable, and smaller than it looks — it decomposes into key completeness and store presence, and only the second is irreducible.** Treating "resumption" as a single impure thing overstates it. Split properly:
  >
  > **Part 1 — key completeness. Solvable, and already mostly solved.** `_study_name()` (`tuning.py:296-324`) hashes the data version, frozen features, search space, `cv_folds`, `random_state`, the early-stopping settings, and the pruner **including `n_warmup_steps`** — its docstring correctly reasons that a trial pruned under one policy and one that ran unpruned under another are not a fair comparison, so pooling them would let 1-SE selection compare apples to oranges. **Three real gaps remain in that key:**
  > - **`metric` and `direction` are absent.** Change `tuning.metric` from `average_precision` to `roc_auc` and the study name does not move — so a study whose trial values were scored on a *different objective* is resumed and pooled by the 1-SE rule. This is the exact defect the pruner is in the key to prevent, on a larger axis, and it is live today.
  > - **`sampler_seed` and `n_startup_trials` are absent.** Harmless against a completed study; they change which trials are drawn against an incomplete one.
  > - **The objective code is not in the key.** `models/train/` is a DVC dep, so editing it re-runs the stage — but the study name is unchanged, so the stage resumes, runs zero trials, and returns the *old* trials' selection under new code.
  >
  > **Part 2 — store presence. This is where it actually resolves, and it is narrower than "resumption is impure".** With a complete key: **a complete study (50/50) is provably identical to a fresh search** — `n_remaining_trials` is 0, nothing is drawn, and selection runs over exactly the trial set a fresh run would have produced, *given determinism, which is precisely what the one-time `resume=false` check verifies*.
