That path is pure by construction. **A partial study is not:** resuming at trial 31 uses a TPE sampler re-fitted on 30 existing trials taking its 1st–20th draws, where a fresh run's trials 31–50 take that sampler's 31st–50th draws — same seed, different sequence. **So the impurity is confined to crash-recovery resumption**, not to resumption as such.
  >
  > **The fix, in three parts.** (1) **Complete the key** — add `metric`, `direction`, `sampler_seed`, `n_startup_trials`. Mechanical, and it closes the live metric-pooling bug above. (2) **Split `resume` along the line that matters rather than the on/off line:** a **complete** study is always reused (pure, free, needs no flag); a **partial** study is discarded and re-searched **unless** `tuning.resume=true`. That inverts the default in the only place it was ever impure — purity becomes the default and crash recovery becomes a deliberate, flagged act — while keeping the free reuse that a blanket `resume=false` default would have thrown away. (3) **The code-version hole gets a decision, not a mechanism:** state that a training-code change wanting a fresh search requires `resume=false`. Folding a module hash into the key is the alternative and is worse — the study name would churn on every unrelated edit under `models/train/`, discarding studies constantly for a hole that is cheaper to document.
  >
  > **With (1) and (2), the counter stops being an observation and becomes an exact indicator:** `n_trials_run_this_invocation > 0` on an unchanged-dep run means you are in the one remaining impure path; zero means provably pure.
  >
  > **⚠ The standing from-scratch guarantee comes free from CI, and is easy to delete by accident.** The scheduled cold-DAG job decided in the fresh-clone bullet above runs against `services: postgres:16` — an ephemeral container with an empty `optuna` schema on every run — so **that job already re-searches from scratch, on a cadence, with no extra configuration**. The one-time local `resume=false` check is a bootstrap; this is the durable one. Saying so matters because the obvious "optimisation" — persisting the Optuna volume across CI runs to save time — would silently remove the only from-scratch verification in the system, and would look like a pure speed-up while doing it.

- **Mark the deps no stage opens — but call them *invalidation edges*, not "sequencing", because that word describes the wrong failure.** Deps that a stage reads and deps that merely order it look identical in YAML, so the next reader cannot tell whether deleting one breaks a file read or loosens an ordering. A comment fixes that. **Exactly three deps qualify after this phase**, and all are event receipts: `validate` ← `reports/ingest_receipt.json`, `split` ← `reports/validation_receipt.json`, and `features` ← `reports/validation_receipt.json`. **The third was added when stage 4 was designed** — earlier drafts said two, on a stage table that left `features` a root node of the DAG, so a changed CSV reran `ingest`/`validate`/`split` while `features` stayed cached and `train` read stale features against a fresh split. `split` and `features` are siblings under `validate`: two distinct receipt files, three edges. See the stage 4 entry for why the anchor is the validation receipt rather than the ingest receipt or the split manifest.
  > **"Sequencing" understates what removal costs.** It implies the stage would merely run at the wrong time. It wouldn't: the raw CSV is **not** a direct dep of `validate`, so `ingest_receipt.json` is the entire path by which a CSV change reaches that stage. Delete it and `validate` does not run late — it stops running *at all* when the data changes, and every downstream stage inherits the stale chain. Use `# invalidation edge — not read by this stage; carries upstream staleness`, which names the consequence rather than the mechanism.
  >
  > **Three categories, and the middle one is the actual hazard.** *(a) Hard reads* — `error_analysis` ← `test_predictions.parquet` (`:1187`) and ← `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (`:1193`); `evaluate` ← the policy file (`:1229`); `calibrate`/`threshold`/`evaluate` ← the run-id receipts, which become genuine reads once the registration extraction lands (see the blocking-consequence note above — an earlier draft of this bullet described `evaluate` ← `calibrate_receipt.json` as an ordering fiction, which was true only of the pre-extraction design). *(b) A **conditional** read* — `error_analysis` ← `reports/metrics.json`: `_load_dev_oof_diagnostics_carrythrough` (`error_analysis.py:1484-1494`) opens it **if it exists** and returns `{}` otherwise. *(c) Not read* — the three receipt edges above, across two receipt files.
  >
  > **Category (b) is the one a cleanup will actually delete, because nothing breaks when it does.** A hard read fails loudly the moment you remove its dep; a soft read does not. Drop that edge and `error_analysis.json` silently loses `sliced.dev_oof_diagnostics`, which feeds `model_card.json` — a stakeholder-facing artifact quietly missing a section, with no error anywhere. **Comment it too**, as a read that tolerates absence rather than as an optional dep. The stage table above previously described this edge as "partly for sequencing"; that was wrong and is corrected there.
  >
  > **Why a comment is the only in-file tool: DVC has no order-only primitive.** Make does — `target: normal-prereqs | order-only-prereqs` — and DVC's `deps` list has no equivalent distinction. So this convention is emulating a feature the tool lacks, not inventing a house style, which is worth saying in the file itself so it reads as necessity rather than decoration.
  >
  > **And back it with a test, because a comment is documentation, not enforcement.** A small unit test that parses `dvc.yaml` and asserts each stage's `deps` set contains the expected paths converts "someone might clean this up" into a failing build. It is a few lines, it needs no pipeline execution, and it is the only mechanism here that actually holds.

- **Housekeeping, small but each one bites once if skipped.** (1) `.dvcignore` for `reports/figures/`, `reports/validation/`, **and `mlruns/`** — the first two are deliberately untracked per the notes above; `mlruns/` is the MLflow artifact store, it grows with every run, and DVC walks all three on every status check. (2) A documented `dvc.lock` conflict-resolution policy — see the note below; **not** a merge driver. (3) `make repro`, `make dag`, `make metrics`, `make params` targets, matching the existing Makefile shortcut convention — `dvc dag` in particular is the single best artifact this phase produces for a portfolio reader.
  > **⚠ Do not reach for the DVC merge driver here — it does not do what the name suggests, and the conflict it would address is largely designed away already.** Two problems. **(a) `.gitattributes` alone is inert.** The file already exists in this repo, so adding `dvc.lock merge=dvc` is a one-line edit — but git also needs `merge.dvc.driver` defined in **local** config, which is not committed. A fresh clone therefore gets the attribute, finds no driver, and **silently falls back to the default text merge**; nothing reports the gap. Every clone would need a manual setup step that nothing verifies. **(b) Merging a pipeline lockfile is semantically wrong.** Two branches that both ran the pipeline hold *different hashes for the same stage*. There is no union to compute — the correct resolution is to take one side and re-derive: `git checkout --theirs dvc.lock && dvc repro`, **never a hand-edit**. Document that as the policy.
  >
  > **And the premise that motivates a driver does not hold under this project's CI decision.** "Every retrain branch will conflict once PRs are routine" assumes pull requests run the pipeline; the fresh-clone bullet above decides they do not — the cold-DAG job is scheduled, not per-PR. So `dvc.lock` moves only when someone deliberately re-runs, confining conflicts to concurrent model-change branches. At this project's scale a written policy beats a mechanism that needs per-clone setup to work at all.

> **📌 Out paths: the bare filenames were a signal, not sloppiness — and "prefix them all with `reports/`" is the wrong fix.** Earlier drafts of the stage table mixed `reports/`-prefixed paths with bare filenames (`calibration_summary.json`, `shap_values.parquet`, `feature_space.txt`), which reads fine as prose but cannot be transcribed into `dvc.yaml`. The obvious repair — prefix everything, since "the code writes it all under `reports/`" — is false for most of them, and applying it yields a pipeline that fails on its first run with *output does not exist*.
>
> **The bare names are bare because they are MLflow artifact paths, not filesystem paths.** That column was populated from `CLAUDE.md`'s "Logged artifacts per run" list, where `feature_space.txt` is a path *inside a run*. Five of the bare names are never written to disk at all:
>
> | Artifact | Sole writer | Local file? |
> |---|---|---|
> | `feature_space.txt` | `mlflow.log_text` — `train/log_model.py:273` | No |
> | `feature_columns.txt` | `mlflow.log_text` — `log_model.py:274` | No |
> | `preprocessing.pkl` | written to a `tempfile` dir, logged, dir deleted — `log_model.py:278` | No |
> | `calibration_summary.json` | `mlflow.log_dict` — `calibrate.py:1225` (that module's only local write is a PNG) | No |
> | `shap_values.parquet` | tmp dir → `log_artifact` — `error_analysis.py:1780-1786` | No |
> | `test_predictions.parquet` | `evaluate.py:1830` | **Yes** — `reports/` |
> | `error_analysis.json` | `error_analysis.py:1817` | **Yes** — `reports/` |
>
> **Two independent reasons an artifact is not an out — keep them apart, because only the second was previously written down.** *(a)* **There is no local file.** The five above are `mlflow.log_text` / `log_dict` calls, which send content straight to the tracking server; `preprocessing.pkl` and `shap_values.parquet` are materialised in a `tempfile` dir solely so `log_artifact` has something to upload, and that dir is deleted on block exit. DVC has no path to declare. *(b)* **There is a local file, but no stage consumes it** — the figures, which are written by `savefig` **and** logged to MLflow, so they exist in both places; they are excluded because they are leaves and because four stages write into one directory DVC would treat as exclusively owned. Reason (b) was already recorded in the figures note; reason (a) was not, which is precisely why these five read as though they had merely lost a `reports/` prefix. Note also that the direction is *MLflow-only*, never *disk-only*: the single disk-only artifact in the whole DAG is `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, and it is an out.
>
> **The rule, stated so it is grep-verifiable: if a path's only writer is `mlflow.log_*`, it is not an out.** This is the figures decision applied to five more artifacts — all five are leaves (no stage deps on them), and the run-id receipts already carry their stages' staleness signal, so declaring them buys nothing and costs a broken stage. `preprocessing.pkl` is the only one that feels load-bearing; it isn't, because Phase 9 serving resolves `models:/telco-churn-pipeline@champion`, never a local pickle.
>
> **Two further defects the prefix framing hides, both fixed in the table above.**
> 1. **`dev_oof_predictions.parquet` was attributed backwards.** It was listed as a `calibrate` out and a `threshold` dep; on disk it is neither. `calibrate.py:1260` logs it to MLflow only, `threshold.py:408` fetches it back from that run, and `threshold.py:1022` writes `reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` — making it a `threshold` **out**. As previously tabled, `calibrate` failed for a missing output and `threshold` for a missing dependency.
> 2. **`reports/threshold.json` does not exist, and the file carrying the real edge was unlisted.** `threshold.py:937` logs `threshold/threshold.json` to MLflow; the local write is `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` (`:1009-1011`). Both `evaluate.py:1229` and `error_analysis.py:1193` call `load_policy_thresholds()`, which reads *that* file — so it is the path by which a `configs/costs.yaml` change reaches evaluation. Naming a file nothing writes and omitting the one that matters meant the cost→evaluation dependency did not exist in the DAG at all. Declare it `cache: false` so it stays git-tracked and diffable, the same treatment `validation_receipt.json` gets. (A `reports/threshold.json` mirror could be added to `threshold.py::_write_local_artifacts` following the `evaluate.py:1824-1830` pattern, but it would be a second, redundant edge — the config file is what the code reads.)
>
> `reports/economics.json` was also simply missing from `evaluate`'s outs despite being written at `evaluate.py:1826` and read back by `register.py`. **Rebuild the Outs column from the code, not from prose, and make every remaining path explicit and repo-root-relative.**

- **⚠ The estimate was *(1 day)* and is now *(3–5 days)* — and the phase is arguably two.** The original scope was "wrap existing scripts in `dvc.yaml`." It now also contains: extracting registration from `calibrate.py`, re-teaching two modules to resolve by `run_id`, a new `review` CLI, retiring `_dvc_hash` across six modules, adding `write_validation_receipt()`, the Phase 2 code cleanup, the champion cross-check, `params:`/`metrics:` declarations, and doc amendments across four phases plus `CLAUDE.md`. **✅ Decided: split into two PRs — and `run_id` resolution goes in 8a, not 8b.** A third, *earlier* PR landed first, on branch `chore/phase-8-prereqs` (folded into Phases 0–7 above): it changed what the stage definitions in 8a can say (`paths.processed_data` stops being an env lookup) and guards a refactor 8a and 8b both perform.
  > **The obvious seam does not hold, which is why the split needs stating rather than assuming.** Putting `run_id` resolution in 8b means 8a writes `dvc.yaml` while `threshold` and `evaluate` still resolve by `model_version` — which works only because `calibrate.py` mints version 1, so those stage commands would have to hardcode `model_version=1`: correct for the first cycle and wrong for every one after. **8a would ship a lockfile containing two stages that cannot re-run.** Teaching both modules to accept a run id is *additive* — `model_version` stays a valid alternative, as the blocking-consequence note above already specifies — so it carries no behavioural risk and belongs with the mechanical work. The comparison-baseline parameter rides along with it, for the reason already given there (same CLI, same config file, same subprocess test).
  >
  > - **8a — reproducibility.** All nine stages; `dvc init`; `.dvcignore` / `.gitattributes`; the receipts and their determinism rules; `params:` / `metrics:` / `plots:`; `data_content_hash` across the six modules; the Optuna study-key completion and resumption split; the CSV→Parquet accessor swap; the three notebook updates; the `validate.py` cleanup and test removals; **plus `run_id` resolution and the comparison-baseline parameter**. Mechanical, self-contained, independently valuable.
  > - **8b — the registration boundary.** Extracting the mint from `calibrate.py`; consolidating mint / point `challenger` / read verdict / flip into `register.py`; the `review` CLI and its subprocess test; the `CLAUDE.md` registry-section rewrite; the Phase 6/7 Verification-line amendments. This is the behavioural change to shipped Phase 6/7 code and gets its own PR and its own review.
  >
  > **The Phase 6 Verification line's `model_version=1` invocation stays valid through 8a and is rewritten in 8b** — the right place, since 8b is the change that falsifies it.
  >
  > **⚠ The prerequisites section has since taken work off *both* halves, and the four lines above were written before it existed.** They are left standing because the reasoning in them is still correct — it is the *destinations* that moved. Three corrections. **(1) `run_id` resolution is no longer 8a's.** It is **PR B0**, two PRs earlier, for a reason 8a could not have known: B1 removes the registry version those modules resolve against, so the migration must precede B1, not merely precede the stage rows. The scope enumeration at the head of this item still counts it (*"re-teaching two modules to resolve by `run_id`"*) — **it is neither two modules nor this phase's**; it is four, in B0. **(2) The seam argument in the note above is superseded in destination but strengthened in conclusion.** It argued run-id resolution cannot wait for 8b, because 8a would otherwise ship a lockfile with two stages that can only re-run against a hardcoded `model_version=1`. That still holds exactly as written; B0 simply satisfies it with more margin. Keep it — it is the record of *why* this cannot be deferred, and the failure it names is the one a future reader will be tempted to re-propose. **(3) ✅ 8b is retired, and Phase 8 is one PR behind five prerequisites.** Its listed content has migrated to **PR B1** — the mint extraction, the `challenger`/verdict/flip consolidation, the `review` CLI, the `CLAUDE.md` registry rewrite, and the Phase 6/7 Verification-line amendments. **The rule that places the last two is the one this sub-block already states:** *an amendment lands with the PR that falsifies it.* B1 is what falsifies all of them — it removes the version that makes `evaluate.model_version=1` valid, adds the review CLI, widens `register.py`'s job to four steps, and moves `review` out of `promotion_decision.json`; the `CLAUDE.md` registry section says *"Phase 6 `calibrate.py` performs the single registration,"* which B1 makes false. **B0 was considered as the home for the amendments and is wrong** for the reason that defines it: B0 is purely additive and falsifies nothing, so after B0 the Verification lines are still *true* — parking the amendments there would make the plan describe behaviour that does not exist yet for the whole B0→B1 window, the same defect pointed the other way.
  >   - **What retires is the split's *basis*, not just its content.** 8a/8b existed to separate mechanical work from *"the behavioural change to shipped Phase 6/7 code \[that\] gets its own PR and its own review."* With every behavioural change now B1's, Phase 8 is homogeneous — nine stage rows, `dvc init`, the receipts, the two guards, `data_content_hash`, the accessor swap, the notebooks — and there is no second *kind* of work left to separate. Order becomes linear: **A → B0 → B1 → B2 → C → Phase 8.**
  >   - **B1 gains a property it lacked:** it now changes a behaviour and the description of that behaviour in one reviewable unit, so a reviewer can check the amended Verification lines against the diff instead of trusting a later PR to catch up.
  >   - **⚠ Pre-empting the obvious re-proposal.** Phase 8 as one PR is still large, and someone will suggest splitting it again. **The seam being retired — mechanical vs. behavioural — no longer exists**, so reviving 8a/8b would be splitting on a distinction the work no longer has. If size alone later forces a split, the seam that actually holds is different: **stages 0–4 (data) vs. stages 5–9 (model)**, which have disjoint deps and can be verified independently. That is a decision for when the rows are written, and it is not a revival of this one.
  >   - **The comparison-baseline parameter's assignment follows from an argument that now points elsewhere.** It was placed in 8a because it *"rides along with"* run-id resolution — *"same CLI, same config file, same subprocess test."* That premise is unchanged and it is still true; what changed is that run-id resolution is in B0, so the same sentence now argues for B0. B0's bullet does not currently list it. **Confirm the move rather than assuming it** — the ride-along argument is about shared touch points, and B0's touch list (four modules, four step configs, their subprocess tests) does contain them, but a parameter is a scope decision and this note is not the place to make it.

- **✅ Alembic moved out of this phase — to Phase 10, not Phase 12.** Adopting it here would add a tool, a config, a `versions/` tree, and a CI step to a phase whose entire thesis is reproducible *file* transforms, for a defect this phase's database does not have. But **RDS is not the forcing function either**, and placing it in Phase 12 would misfire for two reasons.
  > **The criterion is non-regenerable state, and three transitions are easy to conflate.** *(1) Phase 9* — `GET /customer/{customerid}` reads `customers_raw` to prefill the UI, so the table becomes a **serving dependency**: you can no longer drop it casually, but it is still fully regenerable and recovery stays `dvc repro --force`. *(2) Phase 10* — `prediction_outcomes` (live predictions joined to realised labels as they mature) and `churn_scores` appear. **These are genuinely non-regenerable**: nothing rebuilds them from the raw CSV, and drop-and-re-ingest destroys them permanently. That is the criterion — *a database holding state you cannot regenerate* — and it is met here, three phases before RDS. *(3) Phase 12* — RDS adds a second environment needing lockstep advancement: real, but secondary. **Dropping and recreating a table inside an RDS instance is an ordinary DDL operation; what you cannot recreate is three months of matured labels, and that is equally true on local Docker Postgres.**
  >
  > **Adopting it at RDS time would also actively misfire.** Local would stay on `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` while RDS alone was migration-managed — the two environments diverging, which is the precise failure migrations exist to prevent — and the schema would have to be baselined after the fact (`alembic stamp head`) rather than managed from birth. Phase 10 avoids both: there is an actual second schema version to write, so `sql/schema/001_create_raw.sql` becomes migration `001` and `prediction_outcomes` becomes `002`. Adopting a versioning tool at the moment you first have a version to record beats adopting it speculatively **and** beats retrofitting it.
  >
  > **⚠ Implementation consequence to carry forward: `ingest.py` must stop executing `sql/schema/001_create_raw.sql` directly.** Otherwise there are two sources of schema truth and they will disagree. Schema creation becomes `alembic upgrade head`, run as a bootstrap/deployment step. That is a change to shipped Phase 1 code — a third reason it does not belong bolted onto this phase, which already carries two behavioural changes. **Delivery:** a standalone `chore/` PR landed just before Phase 10 needs it, per this project's branch conventions.

- **Carried-forward cleanup from earlier phases — Phase 2's own "Phase 8 cleanup required" list is the authoritative checklist.** Three items live there and are mirrored in this phase's deliverables: remove `clean_dataframe()`; add the `pipeline_blocked` / `sys.exit(1)` handling to the stage entry point; add `write_validation_receipt()` alongside an unmodified `save_validation_report()`. Work that list directly when implementing — it also carries the note on how Phase 2's Verification line must be extended (receipt written on the `exit 0` path, plus byte-identical receipts across two passing runs). Listed here so the items reach this phase's todo list rather than being discovered only by re-reading Phase 2.

- **Earlier-phase text this phase falsifies — each phase's own ⚠ note is authoritative; this is the index to them.** Deliberately *not* scoped to Verification lines: this phase also falsifies deliverables prose (Phase 7's `gate.py`/`evaluate.py`/notebook bullets — covered by Phase 7's ⚠), a Phase 10 bullet (`training_cycle.py`, stale in two places — covered by its own ⚠), and two index entries (the directory tree's notebook comment and the notebook table's Phase 7 row, both amended in place already). Five instances of one failure class were being tracked in five unconnected places, which is why each was *discovered* rather than checked; the scope is widened so there is one list to consult before implementing. **Items 1–4 below are the ones needing an edit that has not already been made.** Items 1–3 describe shipped behaviour that this phase changes: items 1–2 (Phase 6, Phase 7) land in **8b**, since they describe the registration-boundary rewrite; item 3 (Phase 5) lands in **8a**, since it describes the `data_content_hash` fix. Neither lands before its own PR, and neither is left to rot. Item 4 is a companion action item riding along in the same list for the same reason — it belongs to **8a** alongside item 3 — and it too is only ever discovered by someone re-running something, which by then is too late.
  1. **Phase 6 — the only one that becomes outright false, fix it first.** Its opening clause asserts `calibrate` "registers version 1 (`challenger`, `promotion_status: pending`)"; after the extraction `calibrate.py` mints nothing and points no alias. Rewrite to: fits, logs the calibrated pipeline, writes `calibration_summary.json` / `dev_oof_predictions.parquet` / `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`. Its second invocation (`threshold.model_version=1`) becomes a run-id invocation for the same reason.
  2. **Phase 7 — four amendments, substance unchanged.** `evaluate.model_version=1` → run id; "until *the notebook* records an approval" → *until an approval is recorded, by the review CLI or the notebook*; `register.py`'s described job widens from "flips the alias" to all four steps (mint → point `challenger` → read verdict → flip or reject); and the `(review: pending)` parenthetical drops, since the field leaves `promotion_decision.json` entirely. The end-state assertion is already correct and stays verbatim.
     - **Three more lines in Phase 7's *deliverables* fall with them** — `gate.py`'s, `evaluate.py`'s and the notebook's, all still describing the fused document. Enumerated in Phase 7's own ⚠, which now covers the deliverables as well as the Verification line, since a reader meets the deliverables first and they previously carried no marker at all. Seven clauses, one edit pass, one PR: **8b**.
  3. **Phase 5 — survives intact; extend rather than rewrite.** All four of its existing assertions stay true (50 trials complete, model logs at `runs:/<run_id>/model`, reload-parity passes, registry empty at phase end) — Phase 8 breaks none of them, so nothing above is edited. One assertion is *added*: `training_manifest.json` carries a real `sha256` under `data_content_hash`, never `"unknown"`.
     - **The issue it guards against is a silent one.** That field answers *"which exact data was this model trained on?"*, and today it is filled by looking for a `.dvc` sidecar that will never exist — falling back to the literal string `"unknown"`, logged at **debug**, no warning, no non-zero exit. Phase 8 repoints it at a features-parquet `sha256`; if that rewiring is wrong in any way, the field falls back to `"unknown"` exactly as before and is **indistinguishable from a working system**. Nothing observable separates the two states, so the only way to catch it is to read the value.
     - **Both halves of the assertion are load-bearing.** Checking the old key name passes vacuously after the rename (the key is gone, so nothing can be wrong with it), and checking mere presence passes too — `"unknown"` *is* a value the field can legally hold. The name and the sentinel exclusion have to be asserted together.
     - **Carve-out:** the initial Phase 7 champion's manifest keeps `"unknown"` and is not backfilled — it was written before the fix existed, so the value is an accurate historical record, not a defect. The assertion binds manifests written from this phase onward.
  4. **`ANALYSIS.md` §4c — companion work item, easy to miss because it lives outside this document.** It records tuning numbers that will no longer reproduce once `_study_name()` keys on the real `data_content_hash` (item 3 above): the first run starts a fresh 50-trial search and lands elsewhere. Re-run, then update §4c with the new study's numbers and a one-line note that the earlier figures came from the `"unknown"`-keyed study — the old numbers were not wrong, they were computed under a study-naming bug that silently resumed across datasets, and saying so is more useful than a silent overwrite.

- **`CLAUDE.md` updates — five, split across both Phase 8 PRs, none landing before the PR whose code they describe.** They are enumerated here because the first two are currently argued inside blockquote notes above and are easy to lose track of at implementation time. The rule governing all five is the one stated in the registration-boundary note: `CLAUDE.md` documents shipped reality, so it changes in the same PR as the code it describes, never ahead of it.
  1. **Registration boundary (8b)** — rewrite the "one registration per training cycle" paragraph, the Phase 5/6/7 responsibility list, and the `calibrate.py` / `register.py` split, so they describe `register.py` as the single entry point that mints, points `challenger`, reads the verdict, and flips or rejects. Detail in the registration-extraction note above.
  2. **Manifest provenance field (8a)** — rename `dvc_data_hash` → `data_content_hash` in the `training_manifest.json` field description and name its `sha256`-of-the-features-parquet source. Detail in the `_dvc_hash` retirement note above.
  3. **New DVC/MLflow ownership rule (8a)** — *DVC tracks what the DAG consumes; MLflow owns terminal artifacts; figures are never DVC outs.* This is a genuinely new rule with no existing home, so it goes in a new DVC section rather than amending an existing one. Specifically, it does **not** amend the evaluation-run artifact-layout paragraph (`metrics.json` etc. at the run root, `figures/` and `slices/` as sub-paths) — that paragraph describes MLflow's layout, matches `evaluate.py`'s `artifact_path="figures"` exactly, and is made more load-bearing by this decision, not less.
  4. **Phase checklist row 8 (8a or 8b — no dependency either way)** — its key deliverable reads *"`dvc.yaml` with 5 stages"*; the DAG is nine. A one-word fix, listed because the phase checklist is the part of `CLAUDE.md` most likely to be trusted without being checked against the plan.
  5. **The `params:` exemption rule (8a)** — ⚠ **this entry says "two classes" and lists two; there are four.** It was written before the stage walk added `paths.*` (a self-described "third category" in the exemption list) and `mlflow.experiment_name` (the fourth), and it is the copy marked for `CLAUDE.md` — i.e. the one that outlives the phase — so it is the copy where the omission costs most. **Carry all four across, with the deciding test as the unifier rather than a merged category label:** the four are organised by *failure mode*, not by essence, and two of them need opposite treatment (`paths.*` requires a correspondence guard because `dvc.yaml` restates it; `experiment_name` requires none because nothing does), so a label spanning them would have to be un-spanned a sentence later. The list as it stands: *two classes of key may never be declared as `params:`: **env-resolved** keys (`${oc.env:...}` — `mlflow.tracking_uri`, `database.url`), because DVC records a param's **value** into `dvc.lock` and a machine-dependent value makes the committed lockfile churn on every host it crosses, re-running stages that nothing changed; and **identity tokens** (`calibration.run_id` and siblings), because they name which run rather than choosing a setting — the signal already arrives through the receipt file the stage `deps` on, so declaring them watches one fact through two mechanisms, and pinning a real id into the config would leave the stage permanently stale.* Goes in the same new DVC section item 3 creates, since it is the same rule family and neither is comprehensible alone. **Belongs in `CLAUDE.md` rather than only here because it outlives the phase** — the roadmap is read once at implementation time, while every stage added to the DAG afterwards needs this, and Phase 9's serving config and Phase 12's AWS URIs are both full of env-resolved keys someone will reasonably try to declare. The test that decides new cases: *would changing this change what the stage produces?* — env-resolved keys move without the output moving, identity tokens move only because it already did. Enforced by the `params:`-coverage guard in `tests/unit/test_architecture.py`, whose exemption list is this rule in executable form.

#### Resolved stage designs

The stage table above is the at-a-glance index. The entries below are the authoritative declarations, settled one stage at a time by reading each module rather than by trusting the table — which is exactly what the params note above requires, and which is why these entries correct it in places. **Where an entry differs from its table row, the entry wins and states the reason.**

**Stage 0 — bootstrap.** `dvc` is in neither `pyproject.toml` nor `uv.lock`, so the phase opens with `uv add --dev dvc`, then `dvc init`. Four things land together and nothing else: the dev dependency, the `.dvc/` directory, `.dvcignore` carrying `reports/figures/`, `reports/validation/`, and `mlruns/` (the housekeeping bullet above), and the `.gitignore` rewrite below. No remote — that is Phase 12 — and no `dvc add`, since the raw CSV stays git-owned.

- **⚠ `cache: false` inside a blanket-gitignored `reports/` means tracked by *neither* DVC nor git — and it fails silently. Fix it here, before any stage is written.** `.gitignore:229` is a bare `reports/`, and `git ls-files reports/` is empty. `cache: false` means *"DVC skips the cache because git tracks it instead"*; with the directory ignored, git skips it too, and the "git-tracked and diffable" property the declaration exists to buy simply does not happen. No error, no warning — `git status` shows nothing. **It belongs in stage 0 rather than in any row it affects**: it is a one-file edit with no per-row content, it is the substrate every `cache: false` declaration rests on, and its failure is silent — so there is no later checkpoint that catches it, and no row can be written correctly until it lands. Every row written afterwards is then correct by construction rather than correct-pending-a-fix.
  - **Affects six of the nine stages, and nine files.** `ingest`'s `reports/ingest_receipt.json`; `validate`'s `reports/validation_receipt.json` (where `git log --follow` is the *designated* answer to "when did the gate fail", per the receipt note's four-layer routing); `train`'s `reports/train_receipt.json`; `calibrate`'s `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`; `threshold`'s `reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json`; and `evaluate`'s `reports/metrics.json`, `reports/promotion_decision.json`, `reports/metrics_summary.json` and `reports/plots/decile_lift.csv` — the last feeding `dvc metrics diff main`, one of the three checks this phase nominates for Phase 11 CI in place of the `dvc.lock` idiom. **This entry read "at least three stages" until the resolved designs caught up with it**, which is the whole argument for the shape of the fix below: an enumeration written against a half-settled DAG was stale within four stages, silently, and re-enumerating it now only resets the same clock.
  - **The obvious fix does not work, and this is worth recording because it is the one anyone will reach for.** Adding `!reports/validation_receipt.json` under `reports/` has no effect: git never descends into an excluded directory, so a negation for a file whose parent is excluded is never evaluated. Verified empirically — under `reports/` plus negations, `git check-ignore` reports all three files still ignored.
  - **Delete the bare `reports/` line, and do not replace it with `reports/*` plus negations.** The negation form is the natural next move once the rule above is understood, and it is a trap: one `!` line per `cache: false` out is a hand-maintained copy of a fact that lives in `dvc.yaml`, failing in the same silent direction as the bare `reports/` it replaces — forget a line and that file is tracked by neither system, with nothing in `git status` to say so. It is strictly *worse* than the original defect, because seven lines of negations **look** like the case is handled. **Invert it: name only what must stay untracked, and let everything else in `reports/` be tracked by default:**
    ```gitignore
    reports/figures/
    reports/validation/
    reports/feature_discovery/
    reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet
    reports/dev_shap_values.parquet
    reports/metrics.json
    reports/economics.json
    reports/error_analysis.json
    ```
    - **⚠ Three of the five names above are hand-written *because they stopped being outs*, which is why this list grew after it was first drafted.** `metrics.json`, `economics.json` and `error_analysis.json` were all cached outs when this block was written; the out-earns-its-place test removed all three, and a file DVC no longer knows about gets no `.gitignore` entry from `dvc repro`. This is the failure mode the inverted list was chosen to make loud rather than silent — under a negation list the same change would have left three untracked mirrors quietly committed.
    - **The remaining cached outs need no entries at all, because DVC writes its own.** The prediction parquets and the processed datasets are cached, and `dvc repro` adds `.gitignore` entries for cached outs itself — the behaviour this plan already anticipates at stage 3 (*"expect DVC to write a redundant `datasets/processed/.gitignore` entry; that is normal, not a conflict"*). So the only files needing a hand-written rule are the ones DVC does not know about: the untracked human mirrors and the three convenience subdirectories.
    - **This is the same fail-safe reasoning `promotion_status: pending` rests on — the safe state must be the one you get for free.** Under the negation list, forgetting an entry produces the silent defect this whole item exists to remove. Under the inverted list, forgetting one produces a file in `git status` that gets committed: a visible mistake, caught in review, fixed in one line. The cost is real but bounded — a *new* untracked mirror added later is committed by accident — and it is the right trade, because a loud wrong file beats a quiet missing one. It also stops this list needing an edit every time a stage gains a `cache: false` out, which is precisely what went stale above.
    `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` needs no part of this — `configs/` is not ignored, so `threshold`'s other `cache: false` out already works.

**Stage 1 — `ingest`.**

```yaml
stages:
  ingest:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.data.ingest
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/data/ingest.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/schema.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/checks.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/validate.py
      - sql/schema/001_create_raw.sql
      - datasets/raw/WA_Fn-UseC_-Telco-Customer-Churn.csv
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - validation.min_rows
          - validation.max_null_rate
    outs:
      - reports/ingest_receipt.json:
          cache: false
```

No `metrics:`, no `plots:` — nothing this stage emits is a number a reviewer diffs across revisions.

- **`cmd` form — `uv run python -m …`, and the same form for all nine stages.** It runs correctly from a shell with no activated venv, matches the Makefile's existing convention, and nests harmlessly under `uv run dvc repro`. Bare `python -m …` would make stage correctness depend on ambient shell state — the same class of fragility as a bare relative path, and rejected for the same reason.
- **Params — two keys, where the table row named one and an earlier draft of this note named three.** (`paths.raw_data` and `paths.reports` were both here until the DAG-wide `paths.*` rule removed them; the CSV reaches this stage as a declared dep, and the key that names it is policed by the correspondence guard.) `ingest.py:206-207` passes `validation.min_rows` and `validation.max_null_rate` into `ingest()`, which forwards them to `validate_raw` as gate-5 thresholds. Loosening `max_null_rate` makes ingest accept a CSV it previously rejected, so it must invalidate the stage. `database.url` stays out, per the params note — declaring it writes a literal `user:pass` template into `dvc.lock` for no invalidation benefit, since the template itself never changes.
- **Deps — the one DDL file, not the directory.** `sql/schema/` also holds `000_create_mlflow_db.sql`, which this stage never executes; `ingest.py:27` hardcodes `001_create_raw.sql`. A directory dep would rerun ingest on an MLflow-database DDL edit.
- **Deps — three data modules the table row omits.** `ingest()` calls `validate_raw` before touching Postgres (`ingest.py:164`) and derives its required-column set from `RawSchema` (`ingest.py:31`), so a Pandera schema edit changes what this stage *accepts*. `utils/db.py`, `utils/paths.py`, and `utils/logging.py` are deliberately **not** declared here or anywhere: every stage imports them, so declaring them makes a logging tweak rerun the entire DAG. **The line is "modules whose contents change this stage's behaviour on unchanged data"** — schema/checks/validate qualify, shared infrastructure does not. Declaring `validate.py` here creates no cycle and, given a deterministic receipt, no cascade either: editing it reruns `ingest`, the receipt comes out byte-identical, and `validate`'s dep hash is unchanged, so DVC skips it. The determinism rule pays for itself on the first stage.
- **`cache: false` on the receipt**, for the reason the note above gives for `validation_receipt.json`: ~1 KB, and git-tracking it makes `git log --follow reports/ingest_receipt.json` a row-count and null-count history for free.

**The receipt is new code — `ingest.py` has no writer today and `ingest()` returns a bare `int`.** Introduce a frozen `IngestReceipt` (`rows_loaded` — the DB-reported count from `_merge_from_staging`, not the CSV count; `csv_rows`; `null_counts`; `frame_checksum`), change `ingest()`'s return type to it, and write the file from `__main__` via `write_ingest_receipt()` so file I/O stays at the boundary. Blast radius is one line — `tests/integration/test_ingest_postgres.py:63` is the sole caller asserting the return value. `frame_checksum` is a `sha256` over the post-coercion frame canonicalised as `df.sort_values("customerid").to_csv(index=False)`, **not** `pd.util.hash_pandas_object`, which carries no cross-version stability guarantee and would cascade the DAG on a pandas upgrade.

> **Considered and rejected: hashing the table read *back* out of Postgres**, so the receipt attests to materialised state rather than to the input frame. It reads as strictly stronger and is not — DVC only recomputes the receipt when it reruns the stage, so a hand-edited `customers_raw` is equally invisible either way (the limitation the Postgres note above already states and accepts). Read-back buys nothing against that failure mode, and adds a dtype round-trip through SQLAlchemy that could drift the hash across library versions. The merge is already guarded directly: `ingest.py:170-174` raises when the DB-reported count and the CSV count disagree.

**Stage 2 — `validate`.**

```yaml
  validate:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.data.validate
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/data/validate.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/checks.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/schema.py
      - reports/ingest_receipt.json   # invalidation edge — not read by this stage
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - validation.min_rows
          - validation.max_null_rate
    outs:
      - reports/validation_receipt.json:
          cache: false
```

The stage reads `customers_raw` from Postgres (`validate.py:260`), so the raw CSV is correctly absent from its deps — the ingest receipt is the entire path a CSV change takes to reach here. `sql/schema/001_create_raw.sql` is deliberately not declared either: this stage never opens it, and a DDL edit arrives soundly via `ingest` re-executing the DDL and rewriting its receipt. `paths.validation_reports` and `paths.reports` were both declared here and are now removed with every other `paths.*` key — but the reason the first was declared is worth keeping, because it is what shaped the guard's second branch. `reports/validation/` is pinned into both `.dvcignore` and `.gitignore`, so moving that key silently stops the ignore entries matching and timestamped evidence dirs begin landing in git. A params entry does not fix that: it surfaces the move once in `dvc params diff`, after which the two ignore files still name the abandoned path forever. It is the same two-document correspondence as the `dvc.yaml` case, against a different second document — which is exactly why the guard checks mirror keys against `.gitignore`/`.dvcignore` rather than exempting them.

> **📌 The five gates run *three* times per `dvc repro`, and only one of the three is genuinely redundant.** `ingest.py:164` validates the CSV frame with `strict=True` before writing; `validate.py:262` validates `SELECT * FROM customers_raw`; `split.py:187` validates `read_sql_table("customers_raw")` — the same table, the same gates, the same config keys as the stage that precedes it.
>
> **`ingest`'s call stays.** It is a precondition guard on a mutation — the same principle as *validate, then promote, never promote then validate*. Bad rows must not reach the table and then be discovered a stage later. It is also what makes stage 1's `schema.py`/`checks.py`/`validate.py` deps correct rather than over-broad: `ingest()` genuinely executes that code, and tightening a gate genuinely changes whether it writes at all.
>
> **`split`'s call goes** (actioned in stage 3). `split` already declares `reports/validation_receipt.json` as a dep, and that edge means precisely *validation passed on this table*. Re-running the gates it has just declared a dependency on having passed is a second, divergeable definition of when `split` may proceed, bought with an extra DB read.
>
> **Why the `validate` stage survives at all, stated honestly, because the case is not what it first appears.** Within a single `dvc repro` it re-asserts what `ingest()` already asserted and catches nothing new — and that is acceptable, because DVC skips it whenever the receipt is unchanged. **Its justification is that it makes *"is the warehouse valid right now?"* an independently invocable question, asked of a table the caller did not just write.** That is what Phase 11's scheduled `data-quality.yml` and Phase 13's Prometheus gauges are pointed at, and it is a monitoring check rather than a load-time assertion only if it can run without re-ingesting. Merging it into `ingest` would also flatten `dvc dag` — the artifact this phase's housekeeping bullet calls the single best thing it produces for a portfolio reader — from a legible lifecycle into a loader with validation hidden inside it. The stage boundary is bought for those two reasons, not for defect-detection inside a repro run.
>
> **Considered and rejected: a strong form in which `validate` reads the ingest receipt and cross-checks frame checksums**, to catch round-trip corruption the gates cannot express — a value swap between two `VARCHAR(3)` Yes/No columns is individually legal in both. It does not survive inspection: `_merge_from_staging` names its columns explicitly on both the target and the `SELECT` side (`ingest.py:122-131`) specifically so the mapping is order-independent, so that class of bug is already prevented by construction. The cross-check would defend against something the code cannot do, at the cost of a `Decimal`/`float64` canonicalisation that must hold exactly across `NUMERIC(8,2)`/`NUMERIC(10,2)` round-trips or the stage fails spuriously. The receipt therefore stays an invalidation edge, not a read. *(This note originally closed by saying the plan's "exactly two deps qualify" annotation "holds unchanged." The claim it was defending — that `validate` does not **read** the ingest receipt — is untouched, but the count is now three: stage 4 gave `features` an edge on `validation_receipt.json`. Corrected here because a line that is right about its own argument and wrong in its closing clause is exactly the kind that survives a review.)*

**The frame checksum in the receipt is load-bearing independently of any cross-check, and this is the reason to get it right.** Consider a changed CSV that still passes all five gates: `ingest` reruns, its receipt changes, `validate` reruns — and a receipt carrying only per-check pass/fail comes out **byte-identical**, so `split` never reruns and the model trains on different data behind an unchanged split. The checksum is the only field that carries a data change across that hop. Same canonicalisation as stage 1, computed on the frame read back from Postgres.

**Entry-point shape — `strict=False`, against the letter of the Phase 2 deliverable line.** The receipt must be written on both the pass and the fail path, and `write_validation_receipt()` cannot live inside `_run_gates()`: `validate_raw` has two other callers (`ingest.py:164`, `split.py:187`), so writing from the shared path means the `ingest` and `split` stages both overwrite the `validate` stage's declared out and `dvc status` reports `validate` as modified without it having run. Writing it from `__main__` instead, with `strict=False` and an explicit exit branch, makes "always written" structural rather than a write duplicated into an `except` block:

```python
result = validate_raw(df, strict=False, min_rows=..., max_null_rate=..., reports_dir=...)
write_validation_receipt(result, checksum, receipt_path)   # always — pass or fail
if not result.can_proceed:
    logger.error("pipeline_blocked", errors=len(result.errors), ...)
    sys.exit(1)
```

Observable behaviour is unchanged (exit 1 on any blocking gate). The `try/except` stays for genuine failures — an unreachable database — where writing no receipt is the correct outcome. `save_validation_report()` is untouched.

> **📌 `CleanedSchema` and `validate_clean` are unreachable — delete them, but promote the invariant first, or a real check is lost.** `check_schema(cleaned=True)` (`checks.py:84`) is reachable only through `validate_clean` (`validate.py:235`), and `validate_clean` is *called* nowhere in `src/` — only from `tests/unit/test_validate.py:84,162,173`, alongside the `cleaned=True` cases in `test_checks.py:56,67,77`. It is nonetheless **re-exported** from the package's public API (`data/__init__.py`): `validate_clean` in `__all__:31` and `_LAZY_SOURCES:49`, `CleanedSchema` in `__all__:16` and as an *eager* import at `:7` (eager because `schema.py` carries no `__main__` block, so the runpy collision the lazy map exists to avoid does not apply to it). A re-exported symbol is not a caller, but it is a promise to external consumers — so the deletion touches four `src/` files, not three. The artifact it contracts — the raw table with `totalcharges` imputed — stopped being produced when imputation moved into the fitted `ColumnTransformer` (`preprocessing.py:91,175`), which fills the gaps in memory at training time and writes no file. `FeatureOutputSchema` is not a successor: it declares `totalcharges` **nullable** (`features/schema.py:75-77`), the exact inverse of what `CleanedSchema` required.
>
> **What a plain deletion would silently discard.** `totalcharges >= monthlycharges` for `tenure >= 1` (`schema.py:104-116`) exists on `CleanedSchema` alone. `RawSchema` carries a different rule (`totalcharges != 0` when `monthlycharges > 0`, `schema.py:90`) and `FeatureOutputSchema` carries none. So a corrupted lifetime billing total passes every gate the pipeline actually executes today, in all three calls above.
>
> **Fix: move that one `@pa.dataframe_check` onto `RawSchema`, then delete `CleanedSchema`, `validate_clean`, and `check_schema`'s `cleaned` parameter.** The rule needs no imputation to be valid — all 11 null-`totalcharges` rows have `tenure = 0` and are already exempt, and a null compared against anything yields `False` in pandas, so such a row passes rather than errors. Verified against the raw file: **0 violations across the 7,032 billed rows.** `CleanedSchema`'s other addition — non-nullable `totalcharges` — is dropped deliberately: it is the half that genuinely required imputation, and nothing on disk satisfies it. Net effect is one invariant enforced for the first time and one unreachable path removed. **Four `src/` files:** `schema.py` (promote the check, delete the class, drop `"CleanedSchema"` from `__all__:17`), `checks.py` (drop the `cleaned` parameter and the `CleanedSchema` import at `:12`), `validate.py` (delete `validate_clean` and its `__all__` entry, drop `_run_gates`'s `cleaned` argument), and `data/__init__.py` (drop the eager import at `:7`, both `__all__` entries, and the `_LAZY_SOURCES` row). Tests: `test_checks.py:53` (asserting NULL `totalcharges` is *rejected*) is the single assertion that ceases to be true and is deleted; `test_checks.py:63,77` and `test_schema.py:63-73` repoint at `RawSchema`; `test_validate.py`'s three `validate_clean` cases go — `:156` and `:166`, plus `:82` (`test_validate_clean_defaults_match_checks_gate_constants`), whose `validate_raw` twin at `:69` already covers the constant-parity contract.
>
> **Correction owed to Phase 2's deliverables, which described this code as live.** Four places named `validate_clean` / `clean_dataframe` without qualification, scheduled a `clean_dataframe()` removal that has **already happened**, or instructed *"update `validate_clean()` to expect the features stage output directly"* — an instruction that cannot be executed, since that output preserves the 11 NaNs by design and so can never satisfy `CleanedSchema`. All four — Phase 2's `schema.py` and `validate.py` deliverable lines, its `test_validate.py` line, its "Phase 8 cleanup required" list, and this phase's own Phase 2 cleanup bullet — are corrected in place. Cited by description rather than line number: this document's own line numbers shift with every edit to it, unlike the `module.py:NNN` citations elsewhere in this phase, which point at code.

**Stage 3 — `split`.**

```yaml
  split:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.data.split
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/data/split.py
      - reports/validation_receipt.json   # invalidation edge — not read by this stage
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - random_seed
          - training_setup.test_size
    outs:
      - datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet
```

**The deps list is deliberately shorter than stage 2's.** Once `split.py:187`'s `validate_raw` call goes, the module's `__main__` no longer imports `validate.py`, so `validate.py`/`checks.py`/`schema.py` are correctly absent: the receipt dep already means *validation passed on this table*, and re-running the gates it has just declared a dependency on having passed is a second, divergeable definition of when `split` may proceed. Four edits — the import at `:174`, the call at `:187-192`, the `except ValidationError` branch at `:215-221`, and the two config keys that become unread (`validation.min_rows`, `validation.max_null_rate`), which is why they are absent from `params:` too. No test asserts the removed behaviour: `test_split_postgres.py` covers exit 0 and the missing-table exit 1, and the `ValueError` branch that second test depends on stays.

**Two params, both honest — and `paths.processed_data` is no longer among them, though the prerequisite it depended on matters more than ever.** Under the superseding `paths.*` rule it leaves this block for the guard; what follows is why the prerequisite is still required, since the guard compares literals and can no more read an `${oc.env:…}` value than DVC can hash one. `paths.processed_data` was the one key DVC could not track — as `${oc.env:PROCESSED_DATA_DIR,…}` it hashed an unresolved string, so declaring it promised an invalidation guarantee it could not keep and omitting it left the stage's output location undeclared. As a plain literal it simply tracks, and the question stops being a judgement call. `_default_manifest_path()` (`split.py:39`) needs no change whatever: it keeps calling `load_config()`, which now returns the config `activate_config()` installed. The module's entire diff is the `validate_raw` removal plus `compose_config()` at `:182` becoming `compose_config(overrides=sys.argv[1:] or None)` with `activate_config(cfg)` after it.

**`cmd` passes no Hydra override, and that absence is load-bearing.** Every entry point accepts `sys.argv[1:]` as overrides after the prerequisite, so a later reader may be tempted to "document" the location by adding `paths.processed_data=datasets/processed/` to the command. **Do not** — that restores exactly the duplication the prerequisite removed, with `dvc.yaml` stating the path twice and nothing checking the two still agree. Passing nothing is what guarantees the code and the `outs:` declaration resolve from a single source.

**First cached out in the DAG, and the seal settles the choice.** The receipts are `cache: false` because they are small, git-committed and human-readable; the manifest is neither of the first two — `datasets/processed/` is gitignored wholesale, so `cache: false` would leave it tracked by neither git nor DVC, the worst of both. Default caching puts its hash in `dvc.lock`, committed, so **any re-seal of the test set surfaces in a git diff**, while the bytes live in the DVC cache and `dvc checkout` restores them. Expect DVC to write a redundant `datasets/processed/.gitignore` entry; that is normal, not a conflict.

> **📌 `random_seed` and `training_setup.test_size` matter more here than anywhere else in the DAG.** Changing either produces a *different* sealed test set. Within one `dvc repro` DVC is entirely correct — every downstream stage reruns — so the hazard is not mechanical but human: a re-seal invalidates every prior cycle's evaluation and breaks *test set touched once* **across** cycles, which no single repro can detect. Declaring them puts the change into `dvc params diff` and into the `dvc.lock` diff beside a changed manifest hash. **DVC makes a re-seal visible, not impossible** — impossible would require `split.py` to diff against a committed manifest, which is over-built for this project. Visibility is what is bought here, and the note should be read as that rather than as a guarantee.

**The receipt's frame checksum is what makes the second dep more than decoration.** The manifest is a function of `(customerid, churn)`, and since `train_test_split` is positional over sorted ids, adding a single customer reshuffles the partition for *everyone*. The checksum is the only field carrying a CSV change from `ingest` through `validate` to here; without it a changed-but-still-valid CSV yields a byte-identical receipt, this stage never reruns, and the model trains on different data behind an unchanged split.

> **📌 Not a hazard to guard against — a pre-emption of a plausible wrong fix.** Adding a sixth gate to `checks.py` changes `validation_receipt.json` (a new check name in the per-check block) even when that gate passes, so `split` reruns. The tempting conclusion is that the receipt should therefore carry only `can_proceed` + checksum, since per-check detail would invalidate the downstream DAG on every `checks.py` edit. **That reasoning stops one step too early.** `split` rewrites a **byte-identical** manifest, DVC records the same out hash, and `features` onward stay cached — DVC is content-hashed, not timestamped like `make`, so *"this stage reran"* and *"this stage's output changed"* are separate facts and only the second propagates. The cascade dies here, the saving is imaginary, and trimming the receipt would trade away real audit value (which gates ran, which failed, how many rows each touched) to buy it. Cost of the actual event: one re-read of `customers_raw` and a 30 KB write, once, on the day a gate is added.
>
> **The generalisation, which is the part worth carrying forward: the cascade dies only where the out is deterministic.** `split` and `features` emit content-determined artifacts, so a spurious rerun of either stops at its own out. `train` and `calibrate` emit **run-id receipts**, which are identity tokens that change on every execution by design — the stated exception to the receipt-determinism rule — so a spurious rerun there propagates through everything downstream and costs the full Optuna study. The same event is seconds at this stage and potentially hours at stage 5, which is a live question to check when stage 5 is designed rather than assumed then. **Asserted, not assumed:** the phase Verification line above ends with the `checks.py`-sixth-gate test of exactly this short-circuit, because if DVC did not behave this way the receipt design would be wrong rather than merely unoptimised.

> **📌 The `activate_config` guard protects the seal from the test suite — not the DAG from itself.** Omitting the `activate_config(cfg)` line is harmless under `dvc repro`: no override is passed, so `load_config()`'s fallback reads the same literal from disk and the stage behaves identically. It is *not* harmless under `pytest` — the subprocess tests would silently stop redirecting and write over the real `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet`, which is the sealed dev/test partition itself. That is the specific damage the architectural test in the prerequisites section prevents. It belongs here as well as there, because the guard reads like generic hygiene until you notice which artifact it is standing in front of.

**No `metrics:`, no `plots:`.** `n_dev`/`n_test` and per-partition prevalence are all derivable from the out, and a changed manifest hash in `dvc.lock` flags a re-seal more loudly than `dvc metrics diff` would. A `split_summary.json` metric would duplicate the artifact in order to say something the artifact says better.

**One benign cascade, recorded so it is not later diagnosed as a bug:** pyarrow stamps `created_by: parquet-cpp-arrow version X.Y.Z` into the Parquet footer, so a pyarrow upgrade rewrites the manifest bytes and reruns the whole downstream DAG on a logically identical split. Arguably correct — the environment did change — and not worth engineering around.

**Stage 4 — `features`.**

```yaml
  features:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.features.build
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/features/build.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/sql_features.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/schema.py
      - sql/features/
      - reports/validation_receipt.json   # invalidation edge — not read by this stage
    outs:
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
```

**No `metrics:`, no `plots:`** — the same argument as `split`. Row count, column count and per-column nullity are all derivable from the out, and nothing this stage emits is a number a reviewer diffs across revisions. A `feature_summary.json` would duplicate the artifact in order to say something the artifact says better.

> **📌 The table row left `features` a root node of the DAG, and that is a silent correctness hole — the receipt dep is the fix.** As previously tabled (`deps: features/build.py, sql/features/`) nothing upstream reached this stage. But it reads `customer_features`, a view over `customers_raw`. So: **edit the raw CSV → `ingest` reruns → `validate` reruns → `split` reruns and emits a new manifest → `train` reruns — while `features` stays cached, and `train` reads stale features joined to a fresh split.** If the CSV gained or lost a customer that is not a stale-metrics problem, it is a partition that does not line up with the feature frame; nothing errors at the DVC level and the failure surfaces, if at all, deep inside `partition()`.
>
> **The anchor is the validation receipt**, for exactly the reason `split` uses it: its frame checksum is the only field carrying a CSV change past `validate`, and *"validation passed on this table"* is precisely the precondition for building views over it. `features` and `split` become siblings under `validate`, which is also the more legible `dvc dag`.
>
> **Two alternatives rejected, recorded so they are not re-proposed.** *`reports/ingest_receipt.json`* — strictly weaker, and it would build feature views over a table that may have failed its gates. *`datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet`* — wrong coupling: changing `test_size` must rerun `split` → `train` **without** re-triggering `features`, which the `split`-is-its-own-stage note above already requires.
>
> **The added cascade is benign, by the mechanism stage 3 already relies on.** A `checks.py` edit now reruns `features` as well, but `features` emits a content-determined artifact, so the rerun dies at its own out and `train` onward stay cached. This is the same content-hash short-circuit the phase Verification line tests with the sixth-gate case — which now covers one more stage than when it was written.

**Deps — three the table row omitted, and the shared-infrastructure line held.**

- **`features/sql_features.py`** — the entry point calls `build_sql_features` (`build.py:77`), and `_SQL_FILES` there fixes execution order. Editing it changes what the views *are*.
- **`features/schema.py`** — `CustomerFeaturesSchema` / `FeatureOutputSchema` decorate `build_feature_df`, **and** `FEATURE_SCHEMA` constructs `SQL_FEATURE_COLS` (`build.py:28-34`), i.e. which columns are read out of the view at all. A schema edit changes both what is accepted and what is written.
- **Not declared:** `utils/db.py`, `utils/paths.py`, `utils/logging.py` — shared infrastructure, per stage 1's line (*"modules whose contents change this stage's behaviour on unchanged data"*). Nor `features/generate.py`, which is notebook discovery machinery and not in the runtime path. Nor `features/accessor.py` — **this stage writes; the accessor is the readers' dependency** and belongs to stages 5–9. Nor the raw CSV, which arrives via the receipt chain, the same reasoning as `validate`.

> **📌 `sql/features/` is a directory dep here, where stage 1 named a single file — and the distinction is which direction the mistake runs.** Stage 1 named `001_create_raw.sql` explicitly because `sql/schema/` also holds `000_create_mlflow_db.sql`, which nothing executes. Here both files are in `_SQL_FILES`, so a directory dep is honest today, and it stays honest under the failure mode that matters: adding a third view and wiring it into `_SQL_FILES` but forgetting `dvc.yaml` would **under**-invalidate with an explicit list and cannot with a directory. The reverse mistake — a new `.sql` file not wired in — merely over-invalidates once. Under-invalidation is the dangerous direction, so the directory wins.

**Params — none at all, and that is the correct reading rather than an omission.** `paths.sql_features` and `paths.processed_data` are the only config this entry point reads, and both leave under the superseding `paths.*` rule — so `features` declares no `params:` block whatsoever. It is the one stage fully determined by its code, its SQL and its data deps: **nothing in `configs/` changes what it computes, only where the result lands**, which is precisely the distinction that rule draws. Both keys are still policed, against `dvc.yaml` by the correspondence guard. `database.url` stays out per the params note — a `user:pass` template in `dvc.lock` for zero invalidation benefit. **No `random_seed`:** `build_feature_df` is a stable sort, and nothing in this stage is stochastic. **No `validation.*`:** this stage runs no gates; its schema enforcement is the Pandera decorators, which are code and are covered by the `schema.py` dep. And as at stage 3 the `cmd` passes **no** Hydra override — the path is stated once, in `outs:`, and the code resolves it from config.

**Out — cached, and the determinism to get right.** Cached rather than `cache: false`, the same argument as `split_manifest.parquet`: `datasets/processed/` is gitignored wholesale, so `cache: false` would leave it tracked by neither git nor DVC. It inherits stage 3's benign pyarrow-footer cascade. Determinism is most of the way there already — `build_feature_df` sorts by `customerid` (stable) and resets the index, and `read_sql_table(columns=SQL_FEATURE_COLS)` pins column order (which is why the on-disk order differs from `customer_features.sql`'s `SELECT` order). The write needs **`index=False`** explicitly, or pandas serialises the index and its metadata into the footer.

**What the artifact contains, since three later stages are specified against it.** 22 columns × 7,043 rows: `customerid`, the 6 binary and 10 multi-category columns, the 4 numeric columns, and `churn` — `SQL_FEATURE_COLS` is the contract. **Exactly one column is engineered** (`charge_per_service`, computed in SQL over nine service flags); everything else is a `customers_raw` pass-through with the ingest-time renames. It deliberately carries **no preprocessing** (`totalcharges` keeps its 11 NaNs — imputation lives in the fitted `ColumnTransformer`, which is also why `CleanedSchema` can never be satisfied by anything in this DAG), **no split** (all 7,043 rows; the seal is enforced by `split_manifest.parquet` joined on `customerid`, not by withholding rows), and **no feature selection** (this is the full feature space; `select.py`'s surviving subset is a per-run training decision recorded in `feature_columns.txt`).

**Entry point — `build.py`'s own `__main__`, rewritten in place.** Resolved above; the alternative was a separate `features/materialize.py`. Four changes to the existing block: Parquet output, the zero-row `raise`, `compose_config(overrides=sys.argv[1:] or None)` + `activate_config(cfg)` replacing `load_config()`, and the `_load_processed` deletion in `models/train/common.py` landing in the same atomic commit (see the accessor note above). The existing `try/except` structure is kept as-is — `test_sql_features_postgres.py:324` exercises the exit-1 path through `SQLAlchemyError`, and that path is unchanged.

**`sql_features.py`'s own `__main__` stays untouched.** It duplicates part of this stage but is independently useful (rebuild the views without materialising), it carries its own exit-0/exit-1 subprocess tests (`test_sql_features_postgres.py:358,383`), and it is not a DVC stage, so it costs the DAG nothing.

**Ripple:** `Makefile:67-68`'s `features` target description still says "write the processed dataset" as CSV.

**Stage 5 — `train`.**

```yaml
  train:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.models.train
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/models/train/
      - src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/select.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/preprocessing.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/schema.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/split.py
      - src/telco_churn/utils/stats.py
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
      - datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - random_seed
          - training_setup
      - configs/training/lightgbm.yaml:
      - configs/training/logreg.yaml:
      - configs/training/feature_selection.yaml:
      - configs/tuning/optuna.yaml:
    outs:
      - reports/train_receipt.json:
          cache: false
```

**No `metrics:`, no `plots:` — and here the reason is different from stages 3 and 4.** Those two omit them because nothing they emit is a number worth diffing. This stage emits plenty — CV PR-AUC, the paired Δ and its CI, 50 trial scores — and still declines, because **every one of them already lives in MLflow, keyed by run, and `metrics:` would create a second copy with no owner.** DVC metrics are a git-diffable snapshot of the *last* run; MLflow holds every run, compares them, and is what `ANALYSIS.md` cites. Declaring both means the two can disagree, and the one that is wrong is the one nobody is looking at. `dvc plots` against a 50-trial Optuna study is strictly worse than the Optuna dashboard this project already logs figures from. **The receipt is the DVC-side handle; MLflow is the metric store.** That division is what the `train_receipt.json` out exists to make explicit.

**Params — four whole-file entries, and the empty key list is deliberate syntax, not an omission.** `configs/training/{lightgbm,logreg,selection}.yaml` and `configs/tuning/optuna.yaml` are listed with **no keys**, which is DVC's whole-file form: every key in the file is hashed. That is correct here in a way it is not at stages 1–4, because these files contain *nothing but* this stage's inputs — there is no key in `optuna.yaml` that `train` does not read, so an enumerated list could only ever drift out of date in the under-invalidating direction. Contrast `configs/config.yaml`, which is shared across all nine stages and must be enumerated.
- **No `mlflow` key at all — neither the subtree nor `experiment_name`.** ✅ **Superseded: an earlier draft declared `mlflow.experiment_name` here** and used the rest of this bullet to explain why the *subtree* had to be excluded around it. **The subtree half stands and is worth keeping** — `tracking_uri` resolves `${oc.env:MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI,sqlite:///mlflow.db}` and **keeps that form**, being genuinely environmental unlike `paths.processed_data`, so declaring the subtree would write an unresolved `${oc.env:…}` string into `dvc.lock` and reintroduce at stage 5 precisely the defect the prerequisite removes at stages 3 and 4. That argument generalises past this stage: Phase 9's serving config and Phase 12's AWS URIs are full of env-resolved keys someone will reasonably try to declare as a subtree. **The key half does not stand** — `experiment_name` is exempt everywhere as a record-location key (see the exemption list), which removes the last `configs/config.yaml` entry this stage had beyond `random_seed` and `training_setup`.
- **`training_setup` as a subtree, not enumerated keys.** It is small, entirely consumed here, and naming the subtree means the prerequisite's `cv_n_jobs` deletion needs no `dvc.yaml` edit at all. `training_setup.test_size` is also stage 3's param — shared, correctly, since a re-seal must invalidate both.
- **`random_seed`** is declared even though `training_setup` covers the CV knobs: it is the root-level seed the candidate estimators and the Optuna sampler read, and it is the single key whose change alters every number this stage produces.

> **⚠ This stage's `deps:` are only honest once the prerequisite's `common.py:49-59` deletion lands — see that item, which owns the fix.** As shipped, `models/train/` does not read the artifact declared here: `common.py:57` hardcodes `telco_churn_processed.csv` and reads it directly, so DVC would verify `telco_churn_features.parquet` while the module opens something else. The fix (delete `_load_processed`, read through `features/accessor.py::load_features()`, plus the guard test) is scheduled in the prerequisites because it must precede stage 4's rename and because that PR is already editing this file for `_dvc_hash`.
>
> **What is stage 5's own, and the only part to act on here: `paths.processed_data` comes out of `params:` regardless — the DAG-wide `paths.*` rule removes it unconditionally, and this note now records only the second, independent reason it would have gone anyway once that lands.** It is declared above because `_load_processed` and `_dvc_hash` read it; both are being deleted, and no other module under `models/train/` touches the key. The parquet dep then carries the edge alone. Leaving the param behind would be a phantom of exactly the kind the DAG-wide item describes — declared, never read, promising an invalidation nothing can trigger.

- **Two further audit results for this stage, neither blocking.** **`paths.reports` is declared but read nowhere** in `models/train/` or `features/select.py` — a phantom of the same family as `random_seed` at stages 6–9, and it should come out now rather than waiting on anything. **`mlflow.registered_model_name` is read and correctly *not* declared:** `log_model.py:223` writes it into `training_manifest.json`'s `model_name` field, which is an MLflow artifact rather than a DVC out, so it changes nothing this stage is hashed on. Recorded because "read but undeclared" is the shape of a real defect elsewhere, and the next auditor should not have to re-derive that this instance is benign.
- **No override on `cmd`, for the reason stage 3 states.** Every entry point takes `sys.argv[1:]` after the prerequisite, which makes "documenting" the config on the command line tempting and wrong.

**Deps — one that has never been declared anywhere and is invisible without it.**
- **`features/select.py`** — `feature_audit.py:16-22` imports `mint_committed_list`, `compute_shap_audit`, `flag_high_shap_dropouts`, and `decide_survivors` from it. **Its behaviour is mediated by no tracked file**: it reads the same parquet every other module does and writes only to MLflow, so a change to the noise-floor rule or the correlated-group logic changes what this stage produces with every declared dep byte-identical. It survives the selection retirement — the retirement deletes the CV wrapper, not the importance diagnostic — so this dep is permanent, not transitional.
- **`features/schema.py`** — closes a gap the parquet dep cannot. `FEATURE_SCHEMA`'s `binary`/`multi_cat`/`numeric` grouping decides `ColumnTransformer` routing (`log_model.py:364-366`), so **moving a column between groups changes the fitted preprocessor while leaving the parquet identical.** Nothing else would catch it.
- **`utils/stats.py`** — `paired_bootstrap_ci` (`comparison.py:32`) computes the Δ CI that *is* the `lgbm`/`logreg` decision rule (`comparison.py:87-92`). This is the boundary case for stage 1's "shared infrastructure is not declared" line, and it falls on the declared side: the test is whether the module changes this stage's behaviour on unchanged data, and a change to the bootstrap changes the verdict.
- **`data/split.py`** in addition to `split_manifest.parquet` — the manifest is the partition, `partition()` is how it is applied (`common.py:31`). Both can change independently.
- **Not declared:** `utils/paths.py`, `utils/logging.py`, `utils/db.py` — shared infrastructure per stage 1. Nor **`utils/mlflow.py`**: `ensure_experiment_metadata` / `set_run_description` write run metadata and cannot change a fitted model or a decision, which puts it on the logging side of the same line. - **Not declared, despite being imported: `features/build.py`** — and this one is worth the trace, because the obvious reading ("it's imported, declare it") is wrong. **Every path from it into this stage runs through the parquet.** `FEATURE_SCHEMA` is defined in `schema.py:131` and merely re-exported here, so it arrives via a dep already declared. `TARGET_COL` (`:26`) is genuinely owned by `build.py` — but it feeds `SQL_FEATURE_COLS` (`:33`), which *is* the column list stage 4 reads out of the view, so changing it changes the parquet's columns. `SQL_FEATURE_COLS` and `build_feature_df` likewise produce the parquet and nothing else. So there is no under-invalidation to guard against, and stage 4's `sql/features/` directory-dep argument does **not** transfer: that one closed a real gap, this would close none.
  - **The cost of declaring it is also asymmetric with stage 4's, which is what settles it.** Edit only `build.py`'s `__main__` block: `features` reruns, the parquet is byte-identical, the content-hash short-circuit fires — and then `build.py`'s own changed hash reruns `train` regardless. Because this stage's receipt is non-deterministic (below), that spurious rerun **cascades through `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis`**, where a spurious `features` rerun would have died at its own out.
