Over-invalidating a stage whose out is content-determined is free; over-invalidating this one is not.

**Two runtime dependencies `deps:` structurally cannot express, both already noted above.** The MLflow tracking server, and `POSTGRES_URL` for Optuna's `RDBStorage` (`tuning.py:281-293`, with the SQLite fallback that keeps a fresh clone working). Neither is a file. The relevant consequence for *this* stage is that **Optuna's trial state lives outside the DAG entirely** — which is what the next note is about.

**⚠ The `out` is a receipt, and unlike stages 1–2 it cannot be deterministic — which is the single most important property of this stage.** `reports/train_receipt.json` is **new code**: `run_model_logging_step` returns a `run_id` in its result dict and nothing writes it to disk. `__main__` writes it, keeping file I/O at the boundary, the same shape as `write_ingest_receipt()`. **It carries `{run_id, logged_model_id, model_uri}`, the shape stage 6's receipt settles on** — `_log_model_run` already holds the `ModelInfo` those come from, and the manifest already records `logged_model_uri`, so nothing new is computed. One shape for both receipts, and one `read_receipt`/`write_receipt` pair serving stages 5–8, rather than a bare id here and a JSON there. `cache: false` for the same reasons — tiny, git-committed, and `git log --follow reports/train_receipt.json` becomes a commit-to-MLflow-run history for free.
- **But a new MLflow run id per execution is the point of the file, so it changes on every run by construction.** The receipt determinism that makes stages 1–4 cheap to re-run does not and cannot apply here. **So a spurious `train` rerun propagates through `calibrate` → `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis` rather than dying at its own out** — the asymmetry the phase Verification line below is written to probe. Considered and rejected: writing the receipt only when the run "meaningfully" changed. There is no such predicate — every execution mints a genuinely new run, and suppressing the write would make the receipt lie about which run the downstream stages consume.
- **The cost of that propagation is bounded and should not be designed around.** Post-retirement a cold `train` is ~10 min and a warm one ~4 min. The exposure in this stage is not compute; it is the study key going data-blind, below.

**⚠ Optuna's study cache sits outside the DAG, and DVC cannot see it — this is the stage's one correctness hazard.** `_study_name()` (`tuning.py:296-324`) content-addresses the study and `create_study(..., load_if_exists=True)` resumes any study sharing that name, so **`dvc repro --force` with unchanged deps re-runs the stage and executes zero trials** (`n_remaining_trials = max(n_trials - len(study.trials), 0)`, `:429`). That is correct and desirable — it is what makes a forced rerun cheap. It becomes a silent wrong answer only if the key is incomplete, which it is today: `_dvc_hash` is permanently `"unknown"` after this phase, and the selection retirement removes the only other data-sensitive component. **The `data_content_hash` fix in the prerequisite is therefore a precondition of this stage being correct, not an improvement to it** — with it, a new parquet mints a new study; without it, `train` reruns on new data and tunes against the old one. The phase Verification's `n_trials_run_this_invocation == 0` assertion is what turns study reuse from an assumption into an observed fact, and it requires that field to exist in `training_manifest.json`.

**⚠ The stage has an exit-0-with-no-output path, fixed in the prerequisite.** If the comparison returns `logreg`, `__main__.py:39-53` skips Steps 3–5 and exits **0** with no run id — so DVC reports `output 'reports/train_receipt.json' does not exist` after Steps 1–2 have spent their full CV budget, with the real reason in a log line the error never mentions. The prerequisite's `sys.exit(1)` makes the diagnosis come from the stage. Reachable only on a genuine material LogReg win, so it has never fired.

**Not outs: the figures.** `train` writes to `reports/figures/`, which `.dvcignore` already excludes (stage 0). They are MLflow artifacts first; the local copies are a human-inspection mirror, the same status `reports/` has for every later stage.

**Ripple:** `Makefile`'s `train` target and `CLAUDE.md`'s `make train` line both predate the receipt; neither is wrong, but the receipt is worth naming where the command is documented.

#### DAG-wide corrections — found while walking the stage table against `src/`, and each one spans stages rather than sitting in a single row

These three were discovered auditing stages 6 and 7 against the modules they invoke. Each is recorded here rather than in a stage note because the fix is one change covering several rows, and because a per-row fix would leave the other rows silently wrong.

> **🔴 A fourth, found on the stage 9 walk and different in kind from the other three: the reproducibility contract is three stores, not one — and `dvc status` no longer means what a reader will assume.** This is not a defect in any row. It is a property the DAG *acquired* across nine stage designs, one out-earns-its-place decision at a time, and it has never been stated in one place. Artifact after artifact moved from a DVC out to an MLflow-only artifact for individually correct reasons; the aggregate is that **DVC's cleanliness signal has been decoupled from artifact availability**, and nothing says so.
> - **The precise failure, which is narrower and worse than a vague "back things up".** It is *not* a fresh clone — there the cached outs are absent (no DVC remote before Phase 12), so `dvc status` reports them and everything reruns. The dangerous state is **DVC cache intact, MLflow gone**: `docker compose down -v` removes the Postgres volume, every receipt in git still hashes correctly, every cached out is still present, `dvc status` reports clean — and every model, SHAP array, `metrics.json` and `error_analysis.json` is gone. `mlruns/` is in both `.gitignore` and `.dvcignore`, so nothing else notices either. **One routine command, the one people run to reset a stack.**
> - **The blast radius is larger than "MLflow", and the code says so explicitly.** `_build_optuna_storage`'s docstring: the Postgres path *"reuses `POSTGRES_URL` (the same server MLflow's backend store runs against)"*, isolated in an `optuna` schema but **the same volume**. So one command takes the tracking store, the model registry, and the Optuna studies together — the last being the most expensive thing in the DAG to rebuild.
> - **The fix is detection and disclosure, not moving artifacts back into DVC.** Three reasons the intuitive response is wrong. **(1)** Everything in MLflow is regenerable from git + the raw CSV + fixed seeds — `tuning.py:367-368` seeds the TPE sampler, so a lost study reproduces the same trial sequence and the same hyperparameters. This is a **cost** problem, not a loss problem. **(2)** Dual-writing large artifacts to both the DVC cache and MLflow creates two copies that can disagree — the failure class this phase spends most of its guards preventing. **(3)** The *decisions* are already in git: `promotion_decision.json`, `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, `metrics_summary.json`, `dev_oof_diagnostics.json`, and every receipt. The audit trail survives; only the expensive derived artifacts do not.
> - **What to build: a receipt-resolvability check.** Walk every receipt in `reports/`, resolve each `run_id` and `logged_model_id` against the tracking store, report the dangling ones. Metadata lookups only — no artifact download — so it is fast enough for `make verify` and for CI. This is the missing signal, and it is the whole fix: it converts *silently unrecoverable* into *one command tells you*.
> - **What to state, in the plan and again in the runbook, because it is the counterintuitive part:** a clean `dvc status` does **not** imply the artifacts exist, because these stages' outs are identity tokens whose referents live in a store DVC cannot see. Anyone who has internalised DVC's normal guarantee will read the green status exactly wrong.
> - **📌 Bounded, not permanent.** Phase 12's RDS + S3 ends the exposure by making both stores durable; until then it is local-dev only. The recovery procedure and its one non-automatable step belong in Phase 14's runbook — see the entry there, which is where the limits of the recovery story are recorded rather than discovered during one.

> **Sequencing: two of the three land before any stage is written; the third cannot, and the difference is worth stating because "do the DAG-wide corrections first" is unimplementable as a blanket instruction.** They are not the same kind of item.
>
> - **The `.gitignore` correction lands first, unconditionally — and has been moved into stage 0 accordingly**, since that is the one section that precedes every row and already owns the sibling `.dvcignore` setup. Only an index entry remains below. It is a one-file edit that changes the substrate every `cache: false` declaration rests on, independent of any row's content, and writing the rows first makes each `cache: false` out void the moment it is written — **silently**, since `git status` reports nothing.
> - **The run-id migration lands before *B1*, not merely before stages 6–9 — it is PR B0.** The weaker reason still holds: a stage cannot be defined against a module that does not yet resolve its own run id, since `dvc repro` passes no `calibration.run_id`. The binding reason is three PRs earlier — **B1 removes the registry version those modules resolve against**, so anything later than B0 leaves `main` unable to run four modules from B1 until the stage rows land. An earlier draft sequenced this inside stage 6's resolved design and called it *"needs no separate scheduling"*; that was true only while the extraction was assumed to land alongside the stages rather than in the prerequisites.
> - **The `params:` audit cannot land first, because it has no artifact to land in.** It is not a change to something the stages rest on — it *is* the content of the stage rows, a constraint each one must satisfy as it is written. There is nothing to build ahead of them. Its enforcement (the coverage guard above) is likewise structurally *after* the rows: the guard parses `dvc.yaml`, which does not exist until the stages do, which is the same reason the prerequisites' `test_architecture.py` item defers it to this phase.
>   - **That ordering constraint is the whole reason this correction was found by hand five times over** — stages 6, 7 and 8 each surfaced the same phantom on a separate manual walk, and stage 9 is predicted to surface it again. A cross-cutting rule whose only enforcement necessarily post-dates the thing it constrains has no mechanism except the author's attention, once per row. Treat the per-row `params:` list as reviewable code (per the Hydra corollary below), and expect the guard to *confirm* the audit rather than to have prevented the need for it.

**Audit coverage, so it is not repeated or assumed.** Every `cfg.*` read in each stage's modules has now been enumerated and matched against its declared `params:` for **stages 1–8**. **Stages 1, 2 and 4 are clean** — stage 4's *"no `random_seed`, no `validation.*`"* absences are correct against `features/build.py`, and stages 1–2 declare exactly what `ingest.py`/`validate.py` read, with `paths.reports` since removed alongside every other `paths.*` key — it had been declared ahead of the receipt writers that read it, which the DAG-wide rule makes moot rather than pending. **Stage 3 is clean and already self-documenting** — its own note pre-empts the `validation.min_rows`/`max_null_rate` reads that disappear when `split.py`'s `validate_raw` call goes. **Stage 5 has one blocking defect and one phantom**, both recorded in its own design below. **Stage 8 is now walked and is the worst row in the table — four discrepancies, recorded in the sub-bullet below.** **Stage 9 is now walked too, and the audit is complete for all nine rows.** Its result: `random_seed` is a phantom for the **sixth** consecutive stage (a full enumeration of `cfg.` reads in `error_analysis.py` returns no seed of any kind), and `mlflow.registered_model_name` comes out for a reason unique in the table — B1 deletes its only repro-path consumer. Both are recorded in the Stage 9 resolved design, which is authoritative.

- **🔴 The `params:`-coverage guard — its spec is now writable, and it has something better than a spec: nine hand-audited rows to be validated against.** `:1404`'s sequencing note establishes that this guard cannot precede the stage rows, and names the acceptance criterion — *"expect the guard to **confirm** the audit rather than to have prevented the need for it."* With the rows complete, that criterion is executable: **its first run must reproduce this audit.** A discrepancy means either a row is wrong or the guard is, and both are worth knowing. This is the strongest circumstance the guard will ever be built in, which is an argument for building it now rather than deferring it again.
  - **Rule 1 — scope: a stage's read set is every `cfg.<path>` read in the transitive first-party import closure of the modules in its `deps:`.** This dissolves L1's second limitation rather than working around it. `models/train/__main__.py` reads **no** config keys — all 73 live in six sibling modules — so an entry-point-only pass collects an empty set and inverts the guard completely, as that limitation's note already works through. The closure collects them. **And the scope is self-maintaining**: the stage row already enumerates the module set, so scope and declaration cannot drift apart. Same property that makes stage 9's sealed-test reachability guard work, pointed the other way.
  - **Rule 2 — the module-deps corollary, which is what keeps the closure honest.** Any module contributing a **non-exempt** read must itself be a declared dep. Without this the closure is a loophole: a key could be read from a module no stage declares and still count as covered. It also validates a decision already made — `utils/mlflow.py` stays undeclared at stages 6–9, and post-extraction everything it contributes is exempt (`tracking_uri`, `experiment_name`), so the rule is *satisfied* rather than violated. This is the executable form of the extraction note's *"module mapping is a code move, not a guard feature."*
  - **Rule 3 — both directions block, and they catch different things.** `declared ⊆ read` catches **phantoms**; `read ⊆ declared` catches **undeclared reads**. The second is the correctness-critical direction (silent staleness, versus merely wasted compute), but **the first is where the defects actually were**: six consecutive stages carrying `random_seed`, ten `paths.*` entries, and `mlflow.experiment_name`, against three undeclared reads (`ece_n_bins`/`ece_strategy`, `configs/model_promotion.yaml` in no stage's deps, `mlflow.registered_model_name` at stage 8). Worth recording, because the instinct is to build only the safety-critical direction and the phantom direction is what this DAG kept getting wrong.
  - **Rule 4 — unresolvable reads are an explicit third state, and this is what stops the guard being suppressed.** A bare `cfg.top_k_shap_features`, where `cfg` is really the `error_analysis` sub-tree passed into a helper, matches no top-level key and cannot be resolved without dataflow analysis. Rather than pass it silently or fail it noisily, the guard **lists it and requires the owning file to be declared whole** — which is precisely what makes it resolvable. That converts the aliasing trap from a silent hole into a declaration requirement, and it retroactively justifies stage 9's whole-file choice as the design the guard depends on rather than a convenience.
  - **⚠ The limitation to state rather than paper over.** Config files read by `OmegaConf.load` at a path — L1's *first* limitation, the three files reached without going through `cfg` — are not `cfg.` reads at all and this guard cannot see them. They are the **correspondence guard's** job. Build both in the same `dvc.yaml` parse, but keep them conceptually distinct in the code and the docstrings, or the next reader will assume one covers the other.
  - **📌 Treat the first run's output as a punch list, not as a target to tune toward.** The guard confirming nine hand-audited rows on the first attempt would be a mild surprise; discrepancies it finds are audit findings to fix in the same PR. Tuning the guard until the rows look right inverts the whole exercise — the rows were audited by hand precisely because no mechanism existed, and the guard's value is that it disagrees with a human when the human was wrong.

- **⚠ Stage 8 (`evaluate`) audit result — four discrepancies, of which only two were visible from the row itself.** Every `cfg.*` chain in `evaluate.py` was enumerated (12 keyed reads, 8 block-level bindings) and matched against the declared `configs/config.yaml: random_seed, training_setup` · `configs/evaluate/default.yaml` · `configs/costs.yaml` · `configs/model_promotion.yaml`. **✅ All four are now resolved in the Stage 8 resolved design below, which is authoritative; this entry is the audit trail for how they were found.**
  1. **`random_seed` is a phantom**, the fifth consecutive instance — `evaluate.py:1238` reads `cfg.evaluate.random_state`, and `cfg.random_seed` appears nowhere in the module. **Removing it costs no coverage**: `configs/evaluate/default.yaml` is already declared whole and carries `random_state: 42` at `:12`, so the seed is tracked through the correct file.
  2. **`training_setup` is over-declared 10:1** — `evaluate.py:1259` reads exactly `fixed_recall_thresholds`. Narrow to `training_setup.fixed_recall_thresholds`, matching what stage 6 already does with `training_setup.delta_threshold`. Verified safe against the aliasing trap: no `cfg`-receiving helper in stage 8's call path reads another key from the block (`calibrate.py`'s two `training_setup` reads sit in `pr_auc_gate_passes` and `select_calibration_method`, neither of which `evaluate.py` imports).
  3. **🔴 `configs/calibration/default.yaml` is read but not declared — a genuine missing invalidation edge, and the only one of the four that can produce a wrong artifact.** `evaluate.py:563-564` reads `cfg.calibration.ece_n_bins` and `cfg.calibration.ece_strategy` (`configs/calibration/default.yaml:28-29`) for the sealed-test reliability bins. **Three consumers, not one — and two are invisible from `evaluate.py`:** `expected_calibration_error` (`calibrate.py:371`) and `murphy_decomposition` (`:435`) each take `cfg` and read the same two keys internally, both called from `evaluate.py:558-559`. So the affected outputs are the reliability diagram, `test_ece`, **and** the full Murphy reliability/resolution/uncertainty split — the decomposition that makes a Brier movement attributable rather than opaque. Edit either key and all three change while DVC sees nothing move, because no code file changed and neither key is on the watch list — `dvc status` reports the pipeline current against a `metrics.json` that no longer matches its config. This is exactly the failure mode the guard bullet's *"bites later, when someone changes only that key's value"* paragraph describes, sitting in the table today. **Declare the two keys, not the file** — the calibration *method* knobs reach this stage only through the receipt, so declaring the whole file would re-fingerprint an edge the receipt already carries. Note this does **not** contradict stage 7's *"not `configs/calibration/`"*: `threshold.py` genuinely reads none of it, whereas `evaluate.py` reads two keys directly. ECE being a diagnostic rather than a gate criterion lowers the severity but does not change the defect — a reported number that silently stops matching its configuration is still wrong.
     - **This is also the live instance of the guard's *second* stated limitation, and it is the one that would have hidden this defect.** Two of the three reads sit *"inside a helper in a module the test has not mapped to that stage"* — the exact blind spot recorded in the limitations bullet. An AST pass over `evaluate.py` alone finds `:563-564` and stops; it never sees `expected_calibration_error`/`murphy_decomposition` reading the same keys. Here that blind spot is benign in direction (it under-reports reads, so the guard would still have flagged the undeclared key from `:563-564`) — but had `evaluate.py` called only those two helpers and read nothing directly, the guard would have reported the row **clean** while the edge was entirely missing. Worth carrying into the guard's design: the read set must be collected over the modules a stage's `deps:` name, not over the entry point alone — which is a second reason PR C's extraction matters, since it makes those modules nameable in `deps:` in the first place. **Two corrections to that prescription, both established at the guard bullet and both load-bearing here:** (1) the same limitation is *not* benign on **stage 5**, whose entry point `models/train/__main__.py` reads no keys at all, so an entry-point pass collects an empty read set and flags every one of its correct declarations as a phantom — the direction of harm depends on the row, not on the limitation; and (2) deps-walk alone does **not** close this stage's own gap, because `models/calibrate.py` is deliberately absent from stage 8's `deps:`. It closes only after PR C moves the two readers into `calibration_metrics.py`, which is then a legitimate dep. Until then the row must be maintained by hand, as it was here.
  4. **`paths.costs_config` / `paths.figures` / `paths.reports` are read and undeclared** (plus `mlflow.registered_model_name`, read for the comparative regime's champion lookup; `mlflow.tracking_uri` is correctly exempt as env-resolved). **Resolved as a category, in the exemption list — see the third-category bullet there; this row now just applies it.** The open question was whether output-location keys are params at all, given that `paths.reports` was simultaneously *declared-but-unread* at stage 5 and *read-but-undeclared* here. The decision: **report/figure locations are exempt** (they restate `outs:` and are `${…}`-interpolated, so DVC cannot hash them meaningfully anyway), **file-content pointers are not**. Applied here: **`paths.figures` and `paths.reports` need no declaration**; **`paths.costs_config` does** — it points at a file whose contents this stage already declares, so repointing it silently invalidates the declaration beside it. `mlflow.registered_model_name` (the comparative regime's champion lookup) also needs declaring; `mlflow.tracking_uri` stays exempt as env-resolved.
  > **✅ Superseded — the declare/exempt line drawn above no longer exists.** The exemption is now the whole `paths.*` prefix, so **all three keys in this row resolve by removal** and the *"file-content pointers are not \[exempt\]"* half of the decision is void; `mlflow.registered_model_name` still needs declaring, unchanged. **The diagnosis survives and the remedy does not:** repointing `paths.costs_config` really does invalidate the `configs/costs.yaml` params entry beside it, but a params entry hashes the key's *value*, so it fires once on the repoint and then hashes the abandoned file forever — it converts a silent fault into a fault that is visible once and silent after, while looking handled. The correspondence guard closes it instead. **This row was a genuine missing *correspondence*, not a genuine missing *declaration*** — the exemption bullet spells this out, and it is the only conclusion in this audit that inverted rather than refined.

**⚠ `config.yaml: random_seed` is a phantom param on every stage after `train` — declared, never read.** `cfg.random_seed` has exactly six consumers (`data/split.py:197`, and `models/train/`'s `candidates.py:78`, `comparison.py:170`, `:318`, `feature_audit.py:52`, `log_model.py:361`), so stages 3 and 5 declare it correctly. `calibrate.py` reads `cfg.calibration.random_state` (6 sites); `threshold.py` reads `cfg.threshold.random_state` (`:697`, `:1050`); `configs/evaluate/default.yaml:12` carries its own `random_state: 42` likewise, read at `evaluate.py:1238`. `error_analysis.py` reads **no seed at all** — a phantom of a second kind, where the table declares a key the module has no analogue for rather than the wrong one of two. The stage table declares `random_seed` for all four. **Stages 6, 7 and 8 now record this in their own resolved designs; this item survives for stage 9, which has not been walked, and to make the pattern searchable in one place.**
- **The cause is that both are `42`, and `CLAUDE.md`'s "random state is always 42 for reproducibility across all splits, samplers, and models" reads as though one key serves all of them.** For DVC they are unrelated: different files, different keys, different consumers. `log_model.py:139` already draws the distinction in a comment — *"not `cfg.random_seed`, is the seed that actually produced those folds."*
- **A phantom param is the same defect class as the `oc.env` wrapper the prerequisites remove, in the opposite direction.** That one promised an invalidation DVC could not deliver; this one promises an invalidation nothing will ever trigger, and it costs a spurious rerun of every downstream stage the day someone edits the global seed. **Do not remove the config key** — it is load-bearing for `split` and `train`. Remove the declaration from stages 6–9 and audit all nine rows against the module's actual `cfg.` reads.

**⚠ `cache: false` inside a blanket-gitignored `reports/` means tracked by *neither* DVC nor git — and it fails silently. → Moved to stage 0; the fix and its **six** affected rows are specified there.** Kept as an index entry only, because the defect spans `ingest`, `validate`, `train`, `calibrate`, `threshold` and `evaluate` — nine files in all — and someone auditing those rows should find it from here. **The count was "three" until the resolved designs settled the four receipts and `evaluate`'s metrics disposition;** the fix in stage 0 is now shaped so that it does not need recounting again. It was relocated because — unlike the other two items in this subsection — it is a one-file edit with no per-row content, so it has a section it can actually land in ahead of every row it protects.

**⚠ The run-id migration is four modules, not two — and it is PR B0's, not stage 6's.** The blocking-consequence note near the stage table scopes it to *"both modules"* (`threshold.py`, `evaluate.py`), on the grounds that `configs/{threshold,evaluate}/default.yaml` each declare `model_version: null`. `calibrate.py:1443-1447` raises identically on an unset `calibration.run_id`, so `dvc repro` would kill that stage too. **Stage 6's `cmd` paragraph already resolves this** — read it rather than this item, which exists only so the count is corrected where the "both modules" phrasing is likely to be encountered first. It also carries the detail this audit missed: the reflex fix `calibration.run_id=$(cat reports/train_receipt.json …)` fails because DVC shells through `cmd.exe` on Windows, where `$(…)` is not a construct. The uniform rule across all three: **error on both an explicit run id and an explicit version; treat neither as "read the upstream receipt"** — the literal both-or-neither reading cannot stand, since neither is what every stage receives under `dvc repro`. **The fourth module comes from the stage 9 walk:** `error_analysis.py:1179` resolves through `resolve_model_run_id(model_version, cfg)` and `configs/error_analysis/default.yaml:4` marks `model_version` required, so it fails on exactly the same line as the other three.
- **Check during the extraction, not now:** `cfg.calibration.override_trial_count_gate` (`calibrate.py:969`, message at `:981` reading *"…to force registration"*) may need to move to `register.py` alongside the mint, or it becomes a gate guarding nothing. Same question for `cfg.mlflow.registered_model_name`, whose five reads (`:1221`–`:1349`) are all inside the block being extracted — which is why stage 6's params correctly omit it, but only *after* the extraction lands.

**Verification:** *(Precondition: a live Postgres — `docker compose up postgres -d`. The `features` and `ingest` stages are the DAG's entry points and both reach the database; on a fresh clone there is no cached Parquet to fall back to. MLflow and Optuna need nothing running — see the fresh-clone bullet above.)* Change a hyperparameter in `configs/training/lightgbm.yaml`; `dvc repro` re-runs `train`, `calibrate`, `threshold`, `evaluate`, and `error_analysis` only, not `ingest`, `validate`, `split`, or `features`. Change `configs/costs.yaml`; `dvc repro` re-runs `threshold`, `evaluate`, and `error_analysis` only, leaving `train` and `calibrate` cached. **The discriminating assertion is a negative one, and it is the whole point of `params:`** — edit a knob in `configs/threshold/default.yaml` and confirm `train` and `calibrate` stay cached; under the blanket `deps: configs/` this line was written against, they would rerun, and every clause above would hold vacuously. Add a third case for the edge that no directory dep could ever have caught: raise `pr_auc_bar` in `configs/model_promotion.yaml` and confirm `threshold` and `evaluate` re-run — a promotion gate that does not invalidate the verdict computed against it is the failure this declares away. Finally, run `dvc repro --force` with every dep unchanged and assert `training_manifest.json`'s **`n_trials_run_this_invocation == 0`** with the selected trial number unchanged — the check that makes Optuna study reuse an observed fact rather than an assumed one (see the resumption bullet above). **Then assert the content-hash short-circuit rather than assuming it, because the receipt design rests on it:** add a sixth gate to `checks.py` that passes, and `dvc repro` must re-execute `validate` and `split` while reporting `features` and everything downstream as cached — DVC propagating *output change* rather than *stage execution*. If instead the whole DAG rebuilds, the stage-2 decision to keep the receipt informative is wrong and it has to be trimmed to `can_proceed` + checksum, so this is the one assertion here whose failure invalidates a design choice rather than revealing a bug. **Assert the two new writers exist before any of the above, because their absence fails differently from everything else here.** Every other assertion in this block probes *caching* — the wrong stages ran, or the right ones did not. A declared `metrics:`/`plots:` target that nothing produces is not a caching question: `dvc repro` errors out the moment the stage completes without it, so `evaluate` never reports success and no downstream assertion in this paragraph can even be reached. Both targets are new code this phase adds (`reports/metrics_summary.json`, `reports/plots/decile_lift.csv` — see the metrics/plots note), so the first `dvc repro` of the `evaluate` stage is also their first execution. Check them directly: `dvc metrics show` renders the summary as a flat table with no nested rows, `dvc plots show` renders the decile series, and `dvc metrics diff` against a revision with a different model produces a non-empty table rather than the silent empty result a `cache: false` mistake would give. Note the asymmetry it is probing: `split` and `features` emit deterministic artifacts, so a spurious rerun of either dies at its own out, whereas `train`/`calibrate` emit run-id receipts that change on every execution by design — a spurious rerun there propagates through the rest of the DAG and costs the full Optuna study.

**Stage 6 — `calibrate`.**

```yaml
  calibrate:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.models.calibrate
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/models/calibrate.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/calibration_metrics.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/artifacts.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/shap_values.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/explain.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/split.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py
      - src/telco_churn/utils/stats.py
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
      - datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet
      - reports/train_receipt.json
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - training_setup.delta_threshold
      - configs/calibration/default.yaml:
          - method
          - outer_cv_folds
          - inner_cv_folds
          - shuffle
          - random_state
          - brier_bootstrap_n_samples
          - slope_bootstrap_n_samples
          - golden_n_rows
          - override_trial_count_gate
    outs:
      - reports/calibrate_receipt.json:
          cache: false
```

No `metrics:`, no `plots:` — the calibration slope that *gates* is `test_calibration_slope`, which `evaluate` emits. The dev-OOF slope, BSS, and ECE are terminal diagnostics, already logged as MLflow metrics and already plottable across cycles; the reliability diagram is excluded by the figures decision above.

> **⚠ Why the receipt is a JSON carrying a `LoggedModel` id, and not the bare run id every other receipt in this DAG holds.** Earlier drafts specified `reports/calibrate_run_id.txt`, by analogy with `train`'s. That analogy fails, and the failure is silent: **`calibrate.py` does not create a run.** `_log_calibration_run` opens `mlflow.start_run(run_id=run_id)`, which *resumes* the run `train` produced. So calibrate's "run id" **is** train's run id, and the two receipts would have been byte-identical on every cycle, forever.
>
> The edge that would have been lost is the one the `calibrate_receipt.json` note above asserts: *"changing the calibration method correctly invalidates `evaluate`."* Flip `method: sigmoid → isotonic` and `calibrate` reruns (its `params:` moved) and refits a different calibration map — but rewrites identical bytes, so DVC reports `threshold`, `evaluate`, and `error_analysis` as fresh. A different shipped model never reaches the sealed-test evaluation, and nothing anywhere reports it. Note the direction: this is the *silent* failure mode of the receipt-determinism note — DVC declining to rerun when something did change — not the noisy one a timestamp causes.
>
> **Hence `{run_id, logged_model_id, model_uri}`.** `logged_model_id` is minted fresh by every `mlflow.sklearn.log_model` call, so it moves exactly when *this* stage produced a new artifact — the identity-token exception satisfied on the alignment test rather than on the derived-values rule of thumb, and the reason that note now carries an explicit caveat against reading "identity token" as "run id". `run_id` stays because it is what `threshold.py::load_dev_oof_predictions` resolves against (`runs:/{run_id}/calibration/dev_oof_predictions.parquet`); `model_uri` saves every downstream reader reconstructing it. **`train`'s receipt is renamed to `reports/train_receipt.json` and takes the same shape** — its run id was never wrong, but a stage that logs a model has the same reason to name it, and two receipts of one shape beat two of two.

**`cmd` passes no Hydra override, and the run id must be read from the receipt rather than substituted by the shell.** `configs/calibration/default.yaml:32` declares `run_id: null` with *"required, e.g. `run_id=<id>`"*, and the reflex under DVC is `calibration.run_id=$(cat reports/train_receipt.json …)`. That does not work here: DVC shells out through `cmd.exe` on Windows, where `$(…)` is not a construct. Teach `__main__` to read the receipt when `calibration.run_id` is null, let an explicit override still win, and make neither-present an error — the same both-or-neither discipline the blocking-consequence note applies to `model_version`/`run_id`. One small `read_receipt`/`write_receipt` pair serves stages 5–8 rather than three ad-hoc reads.

- **Params — thirteen candidate keys audited down to ten, and the audit is what the guard test mechanises.** Reading a key is the weaker test; changing the artifact is the operative one.
  - **`ece_n_bins` / `ece_strategy` move to `evaluate`, which is where they drive a tracked number.** Nothing downstream reads calibrate's dev ECE — `register.py:995` pulls `brier`/`bss` from the **test** metrics, not `calibration_summary.json` — while `evaluate.py:44-52` imports `expected_calibration_error` *from this module* and computes `test_ece` with the same two keys into `metrics.json`. The consistency objection does not survive inspection either: `calibrate.py:396` derives bin edges as `np.unique(np.quantile(p, …))`, so dev-OOF and sealed test never share edges regardless of config, and there is no cross-artifact bucketing to keep in step. Declaring them here would refit the calibration model — and mint a new registry candidate — to re-bucket a reported number.
  - **`slope_bootstrap_n_samples` stays, against the plain reading of its own config comment.** It is annotated *"a diagnostic, not the family-decision bootstrap"*, but `threshold.py:808-811` screens the slope's **CI** against `model_promotion.yaml`'s band and raises `RuntimeError` when it falls entirely outside. The resample count sets the CI width, so it can decide whether the next stage blocks. Recorded because it is the key most likely to be pruned by someone auditing against the comments rather than the consumers.
  - **`mlflow.experiment_name` is inert here.** `ensure_experiment_metadata` calls `set_experiment`, but `start_run(run_id=…)` resumes, and a resumed run's experiment is fixed by the run, not by the active setting. Calibrate creates no run, so the name cannot change anything it produces. **This is now an instance of a general rule rather than a local finding** — the key is exempt at every stage, including the three that *do* create runs (see the exemption list's fourth category). The stage-6 case kept its own line because it is the strongest form of the argument: elsewhere the key is exempt because a rename does not change what the stage produces, and here it could not change it even in principle.
  - **`random_seed` is not read at all** — calibration seeds from `calibration.random_state`. The stage-table row naming `random_seed` and the whole `training_setup` block is over-broad on both counts; only `delta_threshold` is read (the PR-AUC gate's materiality bar, `pr_auc_gate_passes`). Every other `training_setup` key reaches this stage through `train_receipt.json` or `split_manifest.parquet` already.
  - **`gate_metric` is dead config — resolve it in this stage's PR.** `per_fold_mean_pr_auc` appears in `configs/calibration/default.yaml:17` and nowhere in `src/`. Either wire it into `pr_auc_gate_passes` or demote it to a comment; a live-looking key is what invites a future declaration, and it is the worked example in the guard-test bullet above.
- **⚠ Four deps were added after this section was first written, and the section's own rule is what demanded them.** `calibration_metrics.py` and `artifacts.py` are PR C's outputs: today `pooled_brier`, `expected_calibration_error`, `murphy_decomposition`, `calibration_slope` and the five artifact loaders (`:118,131,136,163,169`) live *inside* `calibrate.py`, so they need no declaration; afterwards they are separate files this stage imports, and an undeclared edit to any of them changes the artifact silently. **The rule was already stated here and applied to only one of its instances:** `utils/stats.py` is declared because `paired_bootstrap_ci` *"produces the ΔBrier CI that can flip which method gets registered"* — and `pooled_brier` produces the Δ that CI is taken of. `shap_values.py` and `explain.py` arrive from the V3 relocation (PR B2), which moves the dev SHAP pass into this stage; the stage-table row already lists both, and this block predated it. **Both lags are the same failure and it is the one this phase keeps rediscovering:** a resolved design is authoritative, so when a prerequisite changes the module graph beneath it, the design does not notice — nothing recomputes a hand-written list.
- **Deps — stage 1's convention applied, plus the point at which stage 1's *cost* argument stops holding.** The rule is unchanged: per-file, never the directory, *"modules whose contents change this stage's behaviour on unchanged data."* `utils/stats.py` is declared despite living under `utils/` — that exclusion is for ubiquitous infrastructure (`db.py`, `paths.py`, `logging.py`), not for the folder, and `paired_bootstrap_ci` produces the ΔBrier CI that can flip which method gets registered. `utils/mlflow.py` stays out on the infrastructure side.
  - **`features/build.py` is deliberately excluded, and this is not an oversight.** `calibrate.py:41` imports it solely for `TARGET_COL`, whose value is materialised as a **column name in the features parquet** — already a dep. Change it and the parquet changes, so the edge arrives through the data; a mismatch would raise `KeyError` on `dev_df[TARGET_COL]`, loud rather than silently stale. Declaring it would instead rerun the stage on every docstring edit to the `features` stage's main module. **The same reasoning covers `evaluate.py:43` and `error_analysis.py:61`, which import the same constant** — do not declare it there either.
  - **`models/plots.py` is excluded for the leaf reason**, not the data reason: it affects only the reliability diagram, an untracked MLflow artifact that no stage consumes. It re-renders on the next genuine cycle.
  - **Stage 1's *"declaring a marginal dep is free"* reasoning does not survive into this half of the DAG.** There it holds because the receipt is deterministic: a spurious rerun rewrites identical bytes and dies at its own out. From `train` onward the receipts are identity tokens by design, so **every** spurious rerun changes the out and cascades — here into `threshold` → `evaluate` → `error_analysis`. Stage 5's Verification line already names the asymmetry; it is restated here because this is the stage where someone reading stage 1's bullet would apply it and be wrong.

**Registration is not part of this stage — see the extraction bullet above, which owns that scope (8b).** What remains in `calibrate.py` is fit, log, verify, receipt. **`_verify_reload_parity` stays put:** it checks the reloaded artifact against the in-memory pipeline via `model_info.model_uri`, needs no registry version to do it, and is exactly what makes the receipt worth depending on — a receipt pointing at a model that does not deserialize faithfully is worse than no receipt.

**Stage 7 — `threshold`.**

```yaml
  threshold:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.models.threshold
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/models/threshold.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/gate.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/explain.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/artifacts.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/policy_config.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/preprocessing.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/split.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
      - datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet
      - reports/calibrate_receipt.json
    params:
      - configs/threshold/default.yaml:
          - random_state
          - n_bootstrap
          - v3_top_k_features
          - v3_min_direction_magnitude
      - configs/costs.yaml:
      - configs/model_promotion.yaml:
          - calibration_slope_band
    metrics:
      - configs/policy/threshold.yaml:
          cache: false
      - reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json:
          cache: false
```

`configs/costs.yaml` is declared **whole-file**, unlike every other params entry in this DAG — `load_costs_config` consumes essentially all of it (margins, horizon, ARPU quantiles, all three scenarios, the `r`-sweep, the capacity limits, the argmax-EV resample count), so an enumeration would list every key and then rot on the next scenario added. Enumeration is the rule where a file's keys have *different* consumers; here they have one.

**`configs/policy/threshold.yaml` is `metrics:` too — so this stage declares no `outs:` at all, which is correct rather than an omission.** `metrics:` is a *kind* of out in `dvc.yaml`, not an addition to `outs:`, so the entry moves and the file keeps every property it had: still produced by this stage, still `cache: false`, still the invalidation edge into `evaluate`. What the move buys is `dvc metrics diff` surfacing **`t*`**, which is the single number a PR reviewer most needs to see move — DVC's `metrics:` accepts YAML, so no format work is involved. **It is load-bearing, not a preference:** `metrics_summary.json` deliberately omits `t*` on the stated grounds that it is *already* a metric here (see the metrics/plots note's key table), so leaving this file under `outs:` would put the shipped threshold in no metrics surface anywhere in the DAG — the one duplication-avoidance decision in that note that depends on a declaration made in this stage rather than in stage 8.

**`reports/dev_oof_diagnostics.json` is `metrics:`, not `outs:` — and `reports/dev_oof_predictions.parquet` is neither.** The diagnostics file carries the V1/V2/V2b verdict **and `v3_direction_sanity`**, so `dvc metrics diff main` surfacing a newly-flagged fairness axis or a sign-flip violation in a PR is worth the declaration on the **third** clause of the out-earns-its-place test — small, flat, and diffable in git — even though nothing downstream reads it from disk (an earlier draft cited the *second* clause, "worth caching", for a file this same block declares `cache: false`; a file DVC does not cache cannot earn its place by being worth caching, and the third clause is what was actually meant) — `evaluate.py:244` fetches it from `runs:/<id>/threshold/`, and `error_analysis.py` inherits the V3 audit set through that same URI. The predictions parquet fails **both** clauses: `error_analysis.py:1188` and `register.py:501` resolve it via `runs:/<id>/calibration/` too, and the local copy is a *re-aligned derivative* of calibrate's MLflow artifact rather than the canonical one — restoring it from cache would hand back a second-generation file. Keep the write as an untracked human mirror, same status as the figures. **`reports/dev_shap_values.parquet` is stage 6's write and takes the same disposition** — MLflow artifact plus untracked mirror, neither `out` nor `metric` here or there.

> **📌 V3 adds no model load to this stage — the SHAP pass belongs to `calibrate`.** Worth stating explicitly, because the first design of the V3 relocation put it here and that reading is the intuitive one. `calibrate.py` holds the fitted pipeline in memory and emits a per-feature `(mean_abs_shap, direction)` summary; this stage fetches it from `runs:/<run_id>/calibration/`, ranks, cuts at `v3_top_k_features`, and calls `explain.py::direction_sanity_check`. So `threshold.py` gains **no `shap` import, no preprocessor call, and no deserialization round trip** — which matters because a round trip is where a silent environment mismatch would compute V3's verdict on a subtly different model than the one promoted, with sklearn raising only a warning. It is the same measure-vs-judge split the calibration-slope note above already describes, and it keeps `models/shap_values.py` out of this stage's deps entirely.
>
> **The one new failure mode is a missing artifact on the hand-run path.** A version registered before the V3 screen landed has no SHAP summary; `dvc repro` never sees this (`calibrate` reruns first), but `threshold.model_version=N` against an older version does. Fail loudly naming the cause rather than skipping V3 — a skipped V3 makes `screen_passed` mean something weaker than it claims on precisely the path where a human is driving. The receipt makes the diagnosis unambiguous: the run id came from `calibrate_receipt.json`, so a missing artifact can only mean *that run didn't produce it*. **Say in the message that a rollback needs no threshold action**: `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` holds `rule: closed_form_c_over_r_ltv` and no model reference at all, so `t*` is a pure function of `configs/costs.yaml` and does not move when the champion does — see the V3 prerequisite for why a bare refusal instead invites re-running `calibrate` on an old version to recompute a number that never changed.

> **⚠ `datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet` is a dep, and the earlier stage-table row omitted it — the one genuine correctness hole found auditing this stage.** `threshold.py:465` calls `partition(df)` with no manifest argument, which defaults to `load_split()` → `_default_manifest_path()`. So a re-split changes which customers `t*` is derived over, and nothing in the original row tracked it. This is the third consumer stage 3's own note already predicts (*"`train`, `calibrate`, and `evaluate` all declare `split_manifest.parquet`"*) — that sentence needs `threshold` added to it, since the six-stage design it was written against had no such stage.

> **📌 The dev-OOF slope arrives through MLflow, and `configs/calibration/` is therefore *not* a param here — declaring it would fingerprint one edge twice.** `threshold.py:807` reads `loaded["calibration_summary"]["calibration_slope"]`: `calibrate.py` **measures** the slope, `threshold.py` **judges** it against `model_promotion.yaml`'s band. So `slope_bootstrap_n_samples` — which sets the CI width and can therefore decide whether this stage raises — is genuinely decision-bearing here, yet belongs to stage 6's `params:`, not this one. Change it, and `calibrate` reruns, mints a new `logged_model_id`, moves `calibrate_receipt.json`, and `threshold` reruns off the dep. That is the whole reason the receipt carries a `LoggedModel` id rather than a run id; declaring `configs/calibration/` here as well would add a redundant second fingerprint of that edge and imply the receipt edge is decorative.

> **📌 `configs/model_promotion.yaml` is keyed to `calibration_slope_band`, not declared whole.** `threshold.py:809` reads exactly that one field. The gate bars beside it (`pr_auc_bar`, `recall_bar`, the two non-inferiority margins) are `evaluate`'s, and a whole-file declaration would rerun the dev-OOF screen — a 1,000-resample sliced computation — whenever a bar this stage never consults moves.

**`cmd` passes no Hydra override; the model is resolved from the receipt.** Identical to stage 6's situation and for the identical reason — `configs/threshold/default.yaml:4` declares `model_version: null` as *"required"*, and `$(cat reports/calibrate_receipt.json …)` is not available because DVC shells through `cmd.exe` on Windows. `threshold.model_version` is **not** a param: it is an *identity*, resolved from the receipt that is already a dep, not a knob. Keep an explicit `threshold.model_version=N` override for the post-registration and rollback cases, error when both an explicit run id and a version are passed, and treat *neither* as "read the receipt" — the literal both-or-neither rule cannot stand, since neither is exactly what `dvc repro` supplies on every run.

- **Params — `random_seed` is not among them, and this is the same phantom stage 6 records.** `threshold.py` seeds from `cfg.threshold.random_state` (`:697`, `:1050`) and `cfg.threshold.n_bootstrap` (`:818`); `cfg.random_seed` appears nowhere in the file. Its real consumers are `data/split.py:197` and five sites under `models/train/`, and it reaches this stage through `split_manifest.parquet` already.
  - **`v3_top_k_features` and `v3_min_direction_magnitude` join the enumeration with the V3 screen**, and both are genuine params on the operative test: change either and the screen can flip pass-to-fail on identical data and an identical model. They live in `configs/threshold/default.yaml` rather than `configs/error_analysis/default.yaml` because the *verdict* is made here — `error_analysis.py`'s surviving `top_k_dependence_features` drives only the dependence grid and the `shap_importance_*` metrics, and the test side receives V3's audited **feature list**, never a `k` of its own. Neither value can be written in advance: both must be derived against the dev ranking during PR B2, since the existing `8` was hand-derived off the sealed-test ranking and a binding cutoff tuned on the test set is a whisper of leakage into the gate.
  - **`paths.costs_config` / `paths.model_promotion_config` are deliberately *not* params, and the gap they leave is closed by a test instead.** `threshold.py:693` resolves the costs file through `cfg.paths.costs_config` while `dvc.yaml` names the literal path — two statements of one path. A params entry would catch only the *moment* of a redirect and then keep hashing the file named in the params list, leaving every later edit to the newly-pointed file untracked: *silently wrong* becomes *visible once, then silently wrong*. **This bullet's diagnosis is now the DAG-wide rule** — no `paths.*` key is a param anywhere — **but its remedy has changed, and the reason is worth carrying:** it proposed literal pins (`tests/unit/test_paths.py` already pins `paths.model_promotion_config`; add `assert cfg.paths.costs_config == "configs/costs.yaml"`). A pin guards one side of a symmetric invariant, is silent when a new key is added without one, and forbids the legitimate coordinated repoint — so it trains people to edit the assertion, which is how a guard stops being one. The correspondence guard in the params note replaces it; leave the shipped `model_promotion_config` pin in place as harmless, but do not extend the pattern. Keep both keys — they are the channel ~14 subprocess tests use to redirect a separate process at a scratch or deliberately-missing costs file, and they are the *target* pattern the `PROCESSED_DATA_DIR` migration is moving toward.
- **Deps — stage 6's convention applied unchanged: per-file, and only modules whose contents change this stage's behaviour on unchanged data.**
  - **`models/diagnostics.py`, `models/gate.py` and `models/explain.py` are declared** — they *are* the screen. `diagnostics.py` computes V1 (segment collapse), V2 (the two fairness differences), and V2b (per-group calibration collapse); `gate.py::slope_passes` is the band comparison that decides whether this stage raises; `explain.py` carries `direction_sanity_check`, `EXPECTED_EDA_DIRECTIONS` and `_check_top_k_elbow`, so V3's verdict changes when it changes. The entry module alone under-declares by a wide margin.
    - **`explain.py` is the second Phase 7 → Phase 6 import edge in this file**, after `:62 from telco_churn.models.gate import slope_passes`. Safe on identical grounds and worth recording now that it is a pattern rather than a one-off: `explain.py` imports only `numpy` and `typing`, so it is leaf-pure and cycle-immune. The rule is that a *pure* downstream module may be imported upstream; `error_analysis.py` may not be, which is why `EXPECTED_EDA_DIRECTIONS` and `direction_sanity_check` move out of it rather than being imported from it.
  - **`features/preprocessing.py` is declared for a non-obvious reason:** `diagnostics.py:39` imports `TENURE_COHORT_EDGES`/`TENURE_COHORT_LABELS`, which define the cohort boundaries every slice is computed over. Move an edge and V1/V2b resolve differently on identical data and an identical model — the rule's operative test, met exactly.
  - **⚠ `artifacts.py` and `policy_config.py` are added post-extraction, and they are what make the next bullet's claim true.** `threshold.py:44-49` imports exactly four names from `calibrate.py` — `committed_features_from_manifest`, `load_dev_customer_ids`, `load_dev_features`, `load_training_manifest` — every one an artifact loader, which is why `artifacts.py` is the destination and **`calibration_metrics.py` is not a dep here**: this stage *judges* the calibration slope against the band and never recomputes it (`:807` reads `calibration_summary.calibration_slope`), so none of the five metric functions is reachable from it. `policy_config.py` and the artifact loaders also absorb code defined *inside* `threshold.py` today — `load_dev_oof_predictions` (`:397`) and the cost/policy family (`:102,118,128,414,428,448`) — which this module then imports back, so the declaration is not optional bookkeeping: `t*` is a direct function of `load_costs_config`'s parse, and every screen runs on the vector `load_dev_oof_predictions` returns.
  - **`models/calibrate.py` is excluded despite being imported** (`load_dev_features`, `load_dev_customer_ids`, `committed_features_from_manifest`, `load_training_manifest`; and again transitively through `diagnostics.py:40`). It is stage 6's own dep, so any edit reruns `calibrate`, mints a new `logged_model_id`, and moves `calibrate_receipt.json` — the edge arrives through the receipt, the same shape as `TARGET_COL` arriving through the parquet. Declaring it here would double-count and cascade on docstring edits. **`utils/stats.py` falls out with it:** this module never imports it, and its presence in the import closure is via `calibrate.py` alone.
  - **`utils/mlflow.py`, `utils/paths.py`, `utils/logging.py` stay out** on stage 6's infrastructure exclusion.

> **📌 A failed dev-OOF screen leaves its outs on disk. That is expected — record it, and do not reorder the writes.** `run_threshold_step` calls `_write_threshold_reports` *before* `if not screen_passed: raise`, so a failure writes `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` and the `reports/` mirrors, then exits 1; DVC does not roll back outs on a failed stage, so the policy file is left holding a threshold derived from a model that failed its screen, unrecorded in `dvc.lock`. **The ordering is correct** — a failed screen is precisely when `dev_oof_diagnostics.json` is most needed, and suppressing the policy write would leave the *previous* model's threshold in place looking valid, trading a loud wrong file for a quiet one.
>
> **⚠ Two cautions once V3 joins the screen.** First, `screen_passed` now has **two independent causes** — a slope-band failure and a sign-flip violation, *not* V1/V2/V2b, which remain reported rather than binding — with unrelated remediations, so the raise needs a payload that says which. Leaving the existing message in place is not a neutral omission: it would attribute a sign flip to the calibration slope and print a healthy CI as evidence. Second, **do not generalise write-then-raise to the artifact fetches.** The rule above is specific to a *screen verdict*, where the diagnostic is the thing worth preserving. A missing SHAP summary or an unreadable artifact has no diagnostic to preserve, and must raise **before** any write — a `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` produced by a run that never completed its screen is strictly worse than no file at all.
>
> **Under DVC the exposure shrinks rather than grows**, which is worth stating so this reads as a property rather than a new defect: `dvc repro` halts the DAG at a failed stage, and `evaluate` deps on `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`, so the automated path cannot consume the stale file. What remains is the manual path, and it pre-dates this phase.
>
> **The residual hole is that the existing guard does not cover it.** `_log_threshold_run` runs before the raise, so `threshold_validation.json` reaches MLflow stamped with the failing model's `run_id`/`model_version`. Running `evaluate` by hand against that same model therefore *passes* `check_threshold_provenance` — that check asks *"was this threshold derived for this model?"* (it was), never *"was that derivation accepted?"* — and spends the sealed test set on a model the screen already rejected, which is the exact harm the screen exists to prevent. **✅ No longer deferred — moved into PR B0, and the payload named here was the wrong one.** `screen_passed` goes into `validation_payload`, not the policy payload: this module's own `_assemble_threshold_payloads` docstring (`threshold.py:836-847`) forbids a model stamp on the model-independent file, and a screen verdict is a stamp about one specific model — so the fix as originally recorded would have traded this quiet hole for the rollback-staleness one that split exists to prevent. The reader gets its own `check_threshold_screen_passed` rather than an extension of `check_threshold_provenance`. Full argument in B0's bullet; it lands there because B0 already opens all three files this deferral names.

**Stage 8 — `evaluate`.**

```yaml
  evaluate:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.models.evaluate
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/models/evaluate.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/gate.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/diagnostics.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/economics.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/plots.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/calibration_metrics.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/artifacts.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/policy_config.py
      - src/telco_churn/data/split.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py
      - src/telco_churn/utils/stats.py
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
      - datasets/processed/split_manifest.parquet
      - reports/calibrate_receipt.json
      - configs/policy/threshold.yaml
    params:
      - configs/config.yaml:
          - training_setup.fixed_recall_thresholds
          - mlflow.registered_model_name
      - configs/evaluate/default.yaml:
      - configs/calibration/default.yaml:
          - ece_n_bins
          - ece_strategy
      - configs/costs.yaml:
      - configs/model_promotion.yaml:
    outs:
      - reports/promotion_decision.json:
          cache: false
      - reports/eval_receipt.json:
          cache: false
      - reports/test_predictions.parquet
    metrics:
      - reports/metrics_summary.json:
          cache: false
    plots:
      - reports/plots/decile_lift.csv:
          cache: false
```

**⚠ This row cannot be written before the extraction lands — three of its eight first-party module deps do not exist yet.** `calibration_metrics.py`, `artifacts.py` and `policy_config.py` are PR C's outputs, and they are what makes this the only row in the table whose `deps:` were previously *empty of first-party modules altogether* — the original declared `models/evaluate.py` and nothing else, while `evaluate.py:41-104` imports from **nine** first-party modules. That was not an oversight in the row so much as a symptom: the things it imports were unnameable, because they lived inside two other stages' entry points. The row above is the post-extraction shape; before it, the only correct alternative is the coarse fallback (`calibrate.py` + `threshold.py` declared whole, over-invalidating), which the prerequisite records as a deliberate choice rather than an oversight.

**Deps — what is in, and the three exclusions that each have a different reason.**
- **`models/gate.py` is a dep and was missing entirely.** `evaluate.py:74` imports `decide_promotion`, which *is* the promotion verdict; a gate whose decision logic can change without invalidating the verdict computed from it is the same defect class as `configs/model_promotion.yaml` being in no stage's deps. Post-extraction it also carries `load_model_promotion_bars`, so the bars parser and the bars file both reach this stage through declarations rather than through nothing.
- **`models/plots.py` is a dep here, though stage 6 excludes it — and the contrast is the rule, not an inconsistency.** Stage 6 excludes it on the leaf reason: there it renders only a reliability diagram, an untracked MLflow artifact no stage consumes. Here `decile_lift_table`, `classification_summary_points`, `reliability_diagram_bins`, `pr_curve_points` and `roc_curve_points` (`evaluate.py:75-81`) all produce **content inside `metrics.json`** and the `plots:` CSV. Same module, different disposition, because the operative test is whether its output enters a tracked artifact — not what kind of module it is.
- **`models/calibrate.py` and `models/threshold.py` are both excluded, and post-extraction that is finally true rather than aspirational.** Stage 7's row already claims the pattern — *"`calibrate.py` is excluded despite being imported, its edge arriving through the receipt"* — but the claim only holds once the imported *code* moves out. Today `evaluate.py:44-52` pulls seven names from `calibrate.py` and `:82-89` pulls six from `threshold.py`; afterwards those come from `calibration_metrics.py`/`artifacts.py` and `policy_config.py`, and the two entry points genuinely reach this stage only through `reports/calibrate_receipt.json` and `configs/policy/threshold.yaml`.
- **`features/build.py` stays out** on stage 6's `TARGET_COL` reasoning, which names `evaluate.py:43` explicitly: the constant is materialised as a column in the features parquet, already a dep, and a mismatch raises `KeyError` rather than going stale.
- **`utils/mlflow.py` stays out, `utils/stats.py` comes in — and the split is not "utils is infrastructure".** `utils/stats.py` supplies `bootstrap_metric_ci` / `paired_bootstrap_ci` / `paired_bootstrap_metric_ci` (`:100-103`), which produce the CIs the comparative regime's three non-inferiority checks are decided on; the resample logic changing changes the verdict. What remains in `utils/mlflow.py` after the extraction is tracking-URI resolution, experiment metadata and run/model-id lookups — genuinely inert plumbing, which is what finally makes stage 6's *"`utils/mlflow.py` stays out on the infrastructure side"* a true statement rather than an assumed one. **Both of the decision-bearing functions it holds today leave, and neither goes to `gate.py`:** `load_model_promotion_bars` to `policy_config.py` (it reads a policy YAML by path, like its two neighbours there) and `resolve_champion_version` to `artifacts.py` (it reads MLflow state, like `load_fitted_model`). **`resolve_champion_version` is decision-bearing despite looking like plumbing, and its own docstring says so** — `evaluate.py:641-647` records that any `MlflowException` other than the two cold-start codes *"must propagate rather than be misread as cold start, which would silently switch the gate to the wrong regime."* Cold-start versus comparative is the largest fork in the gate, so this is code that changes the verdict on unchanged data and unchanged registry state: the deps rule's operative test, met exactly. Routing it to `artifacts.py` rather than `gate.py` keeps `gate.py` stdlib-only — see the extraction item for why that property is worth protecting.

**Params — the row's four audited discrepancies, all resolved here.** The stage-table row declared `configs/config.yaml: random_seed, training_setup` plus three whole files; every part of that changed.
- **`random_seed` is gone** — `evaluate.py:1238` reads `cfg.evaluate.random_state`, and `cfg.random_seed` appears nowhere in the module. No coverage is lost: `configs/evaluate/default.yaml` is declared whole and carries `random_state: 42` at `:12`. Fifth consecutive stage to carry this phantom.
- **`training_setup` narrows to `fixed_recall_thresholds`** — `evaluate.py:1259` reads exactly that, 1 key of 10. Checked against the aliasing trap: no `cfg`-receiving helper on this stage's call path reads another key from the block.
- **🔴 `configs/calibration/default.yaml: ece_n_bins, ece_strategy` is new, and it is the one discrepancy that could produce a wrong artifact.** `evaluate.py:563-564` reads both for the sealed-test reliability bins; `expected_calibration_error` and `murphy_decomposition` read them again internally, so `test_ece` and the whole Murphy reliability/resolution/uncertainty split move with them. Undeclared, editing either key changes all three while `dvc status` reports the pipeline current. **Two keys, not the whole file** — the calibration *method* knobs reach this stage through the receipt, and declaring the file would fingerprint that edge twice. (After PR C's signature change the two helpers take `n_bins`/`strategy` as values, which collapses the read set to `:563-564` and makes this declaration guard-checkable rather than hand-maintained.)
- **`mlflow.registered_model_name` is added; no `paths.*` key is.** An earlier draft added `paths.costs_config` here on the reasoning that it points at a file whose *contents* this stage declares, so repointing it silently invalidates the declaration beside it — a correct diagnosis whose remedy has since moved to the correspondence guard, since a params entry detects the redirect once and then hashes the abandoned file forever. `paths.costs_config`, `paths.figures` and `paths.reports` are therefore all absent, for one reason rather than two. `mlflow.registered_model_name` selects which registered model the comparative regime looks the champion up in; `mlflow.tracking_uri` stays exempt as env-resolved.

> **📌 `configs/model_promotion.yaml` is declared whole here and keyed to one field at stage 7 — the asymmetry is correct.** `threshold.py:809` reads only `calibration_slope_band`, so declaring the file there would rerun a 1,000-resample sliced screen whenever a bar it never consults moves. This stage reads **all six** through `load_model_promotion_bars` — both bars, the band, and the two non-inferiority margins — so an enumeration would list the entire file and then rot on the next margin added. Same file, opposite treatment, on the same rule that governs `configs/costs.yaml`: enumerate where a file's keys have different consumers, declare whole where they have one.

**Outs — two, plus one metric and one plot. An earlier draft declared four, and `economics.json`/`metrics.json` are both removed under the out-earns-its-place test.**
- **`test_predictions.parquet` stays, cached** — `error_analysis.py:1187` genuinely reads it, so it is this stage's one clause-(a) out and the only file-content edge into stage 9. Binary, so git diff value is nil and caching is right.
- **`promotion_decision.json` stays, `cache: false`** — 1.7 KB, and the gate verdict flipping is exactly what a reviewer must see in a PR. It earns its place on the **third** clause, not the first: post-B1 nothing in `src/` reads the local copy, since `register.py` resolves `runs:/{eval_run_id}/promotion_decision.json`.
- **`economics.json` is dropped — 513 KB, cached, and read by nothing.** `register.py:1381` resolves `runs:/{eval_run_id}/economics.json`. It fails all three clauses: no downstream reader, nothing to cache that MLflow does not already hold per-run, and far too large and nested for a git diff anyone reads. Untracked human mirror, same status as the figures.
- **`metrics.json` is dropped too, and it lost its justification *during* this phase's design rather than arriving without one.** Its single DAG reader was `error_analysis.py`'s dev-OOF carry-through, which PR B0 repoints at `evaluate.py::load_dev_oof_diagnostics` — so clause (a) went with it. What remained was clause (c), and 42 KB of nested JSON does not clear it: `metrics_summary.json` exists *precisely because* `metrics.json` cannot be rendered by `dvc metrics show`. **The decisive argument is the disanalogy with the receipts**, which are git-tracked because the plan deliberately keeps validation out of MLflow, making git the only history that exists. MLflow already holds one `metrics.json` per cycle — immutable, per-run, reachable from the model version through `eval_run_id` — so tracking it in git adds a second, weaker history of the same document, recording only the cycles somebody happened to commit. **The file is not deleted:** it is still logged to the eval run (where `register.py` assembles the model card from it) and still mirrored to `reports/` for notebook 05 and human inspection. `:1611`'s attributability rule is untouched — that governs what the file contains, not who versions it.

**The line this settles: the tracked diff surface is small and flat; the detail lives in MLflow.** What a reviewer diffs across cycles is `metrics_summary.json`, `promotion_decision.json`, `configs/policy/threshold.yaml` and `dev_oof_diagnostics.json` — four small files, three of them under `metrics:`. Both remaining `reports/` entries still require the stage 0 `.gitignore` rewrite to precede them, or the `cache: false` one is tracked by neither system, silently.

> **📌 `reports/promotion_decision.json` is declared unconditionally here, and the stage-table row's *"valid as an out only once the review split lands"* is superseded — the split is a prerequisite, so by the time this row exists it has already landed.** Per the preamble to these resolved designs, the entry wins over the table and states the reason. What the split changes is the document itself: the human verdict moves to `promotion_review.json` on the **eval run** as an append-only `entries` list, and the `review` field leaves the decision payload entirely (`gate.py:344` returns `"review": "pending"` today; `register.py` reads the verdict from `entries[-1]` afterwards). **That is what makes this a well-behaved out rather than a tolerated one:** with no human mutation path, `promotion_decision.json` becomes a pure function of the metrics computed in this same stage — deterministic bytes, no last-writer-wins race with notebook 05, and `dvc status` quiet across a review instead of reporting drift every time someone approves. The pre-split arrangement — DAG and human writing one path, DVC reverting the tracked out to its recorded content — is the defect that prerequisite removes, not a constraint this row has to work around. **The dependency is one-directional and worth stating for anyone reordering the work:** this declaration is correct *because* PR A precedes it, so the row cannot be lifted into a branch that skips it.

> **📌 The two new writers are co-requisites of this row, not of the phase.** Nothing in `src/` writes `reports/metrics_summary.json`, and the decile rows exist only nested at `evaluate.py:1460`; nothing emits a CSV. `dvc repro` errors when a declared `metrics:`/`plots:` target is absent after the stage runs, so this row is unexecutable until both land — in the same PR, since neither is a change to existing behaviour that the row's content depends on. Both are **serialization of already-computed values, never new computation**: `flatten_metrics_summary(metrics_payload, regime)` beside `_assemble_payloads` (`:1447`), and the CSV written from the same `core_metrics["decile_rows"]` list rather than a second `sealed_test_decile_lift` call. Specification and key table are in the metrics/plots note.

> **📌 No `figures:` entry, and it is not a judgement call.** This stage writes eight PNGs (`evaluate.py:1530-1537`) into `reports/figures/`, which four stages share — and two stages naming the same directory out is a parse-time DVC error that stops the whole pipeline loading. See the figures decision; nothing about this stage changes it.

**`cmd` passes no Hydra override — the model resolves from `reports/calibrate_receipt.json`, and this is where the sealed-test invariant and the DVC design agree rather than collide.** `configs/evaluate/default.yaml` declares `model_version: null`, and `dvc repro` supplies nothing, so `__main__` reads the receipt for `{run_id, logged_model_id, model_uri}` under the same both-or-neither rule stages 6 and 7 use: an explicit override wins, neither present is an error. **That path is also the invariant.** `CLAUDE.md` requires this module to resolve by explicit `run_id` or version and never by alias, because `challenger` moves every retrain cycle; the receipt hands it a run id fixed by the upstream stage that produced the artifact, so the DVC-correct resolution and the invariant-correct resolution are the same one. A refactor to `models:/telco-churn-pipeline@challenger` would break both at once. The resolved version is logged into `metrics.json` so every number here is attributable to one artifact.

> **📌 The test split is read here and nowhere else, and `split_manifest.parquet` is what makes that checkable.** `evaluate.py:41` imports `partition`, which takes the manifest — so a re-split changes which customers the seal covers, and the dep records it. This is the one stage permitted to touch `X_test`/`y_test`; the DAG makes that legible, since no other stage declares a path by which the test rows could reach it.

> **⚠ `reports/eval_receipt.json` was missing from this block and has been added.** The receipts bullet states it is *"B1's to write, not Phase 8's — Phase 8 only **declares** them as stage outs"*, and the declaration had not been made. `cache: false` for the same reason as its `train`/`calibrate` siblings — an identity token belongs in git, where its movement is legible in a diff — and it needs no `.gitignore` companion, since stage 0's inverted list tracks anything it does not explicitly name. Found while writing stage 9, whose receipt is the exact structural parallel.
> - **⚠ An earlier draft justified this with *"a stale copy would survive a rerun of the stage that is supposed to produce it."* That is false and is replaced here** — `evaluate.py` writes the receipt on every execution, so a rerun overwrites it and no staleness-through-rerun path exists. **The real reasons are three.** (1) `dvc repro` **verifies the file was produced**, so a refactor that drops the write becomes a hard error rather than a missing file discovered when `register.py` fails. (2) The hash lands in `dvc.lock`, which is the **only record tying a pipeline state to the MLflow evaluation run** that produced the model it describes. (3) **A hand-run overwrite becomes visible — and this is the mirror of the hazard B0 fixes on `test_predictions.parquet`.** Rollback inspection is a supported path: hand-run `evaluate` against an older version and this receipt is overwritten with that run's id while every dep is unchanged, so the pipeline still reports clean. Declared, `dvc status` reports the output modified; undeclared, nothing notices. The staleness is real, but it enters through the *hand-run*, not the rerun.
>   - **The two are halves of one hazard, found separately.** The hand-run path leaves both of this stage's cross-cycle artifacts unverifiable — `test_predictions.parquet` by **content** (fixed by B0's `logged_model_id` stamp) and `eval_receipt.json` by **identity** (fixed here). Neither fix covers the other, and they surfaced three sections apart.
> - **📌 It fails clause (a) of the out-earns-its-place test, and the asymmetry with the two files just removed is worth stating.** Nothing downstream consumes it: stage 9 deliberately does not dep on it (B0's rejected identity-token cascade) and `register.py` is not a stage. `metrics.json` and `economics.json` were dropped from this same block minutes earlier, so a reader will reasonably ask why this one stays. **The distinction is which side of the read the file sits on:** those two have a consumer that is *contractually barred* from reading the local copy, which makes the local file a pure mirror; this one **is** the channel `register.py` bootstraps from, and the declaration is what protects the channel's integrity.

---

**Stage 9 — `error_analysis`.**

```yaml
  error_analysis:
    cmd: uv run python -m telco_churn.models.error_analysis
    deps:
      - src/telco_churn/models/error_analysis.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/explain.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/shap_values.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/artifacts.py
      - src/telco_churn/models/policy_config.py
      - src/telco_churn/features/accessor.py
      - datasets/processed/telco_churn_features.parquet
      - reports/calibrate_receipt.json
      - reports/test_predictions.parquet
      - configs/policy/threshold.yaml
    params:
      - configs/error_analysis/default.yaml:
    outs:
      - reports/error_analysis_receipt.json:
          cache: false
```
